Now, let us reduce Proposition
4.1
to the case of smooth, small initial data by compactness arguments, i.e. Proposition
4.2
in the following contents.

4.3.
A reduction to the case of small initial data

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
and
\delta_{*}\in[0,s-2)
. Assume (
1.29
) and (
1.28
) hold. Consider the Cauchy problem (
1.13
). Suppose the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},{\rho}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},h_{0})
be smooth, supported in
B(0,c+2)
and satisfying

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.11)

Then the Cauchy problem (
1.13
) admits a smooth solution
(\mathbf{v},\rho,\mathbf{w},h)
on
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, which have the following properties:

\mathrm{(1)}
energy estimates

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\\
&\|\mathbf{v},\rho,h\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.12)

\mathrm{(2)}
dispersive estimate for
\mathbf{v}
and
\rho

\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}+\|d\rho,\partial\mathbf{v}_{+},d\mathbf{v},dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(4.13)

\mathrm{(3)}
dispersive estimate for the linear equation

For any
t_{0}\in[-1,1)
, let
f
satisfy
the linear wave equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}f=0,\qquad(t,x)\in(t_{0},1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{0}\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\quad\partial_{t}f(t_{0},\cdot)=f_{1}\in H^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\end{cases}

(4.14)

where
g
has the formulations defined in (
1.10
). For each
1\leq r\leq s+1
, the Cauchy problem (
1.35
) admits a solution
f
in
C([-1,1];H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-1,1];H_{x}^{r-1})
if the data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
. Moreover, for
k<r-1
, the following estimate holds:

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-1,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}},

(4.15)

and the same estimates hold with
\left<\partial\right>^{k}
replaced by
\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d
.

4.4.
Proof of Proposition
4.1
by Proposition
4.2

In Proposition
4.1
, the corresponding statement is considering the small, supported data. While, the initial data is large in Proposition
4.2
. Firstly, by using a scaling method, we can reduce the initial data in Proposition
4.2
to be small.

Step 1: Scaling
. Assume

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}\leq M_{0}.\end{split}

(4.16)

If taking the scaling

\begin{split}&\underline{\mathbf{v}}(t,x)=\mathbf{v}(Tt,Tx),\quad\underline{\rho}(t,x)={\rho}(Tt,Tx),\quad\underline{h}(t,x)=h(Tt,Tx),\end{split}

(4.17)

we then obtain

\begin{split}&\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq M_{0}T^{s-\frac{3}{2}},\qquad\qquad\|\underline{\rho}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq M_{0}T^{s-\frac{3}{2}},\\
&\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\|\underline{h}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}}\leq M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.18)

Here we use the fact that
\underline{\mathbf{w}}(0)=\mathrm{e}^{-\underline{\rho}(0)}\mathrm{curl}\underline{\mathbf{v}}(0)
. Choose sufficiently small
T

such that

\max\{M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}},M_{0}T^{s-\frac{3}{2}}\}\ll\epsilon_{3}.

(4.19)

Therefore, we get

\begin{split}&\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\quad\|\underline{{\rho}}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\quad\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\quad\|\underline{h}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

Using (
1.27
), we also have

\begin{split}\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}(0),\underline{\rho}(0),\underline{h}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.20)

Secondly, to reduce the initial data with support set, we need a physical localization technique.

Step 2: Localization
.

For the propagation speed of (
1.13
) is finite, we set
c
be the largest speed of (
1.13
). Set
\chi
be a smooth function supported in
B(0,c+2)
, and which equals
1
in
B(0,c+1)
. For given
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we define the localized initial data for the velocity and density near
\mathbf{y}
:

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{x})=&\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{x})-\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{y})\right),\\
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(\mathbf{x})=&\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(\underline{\rho}_{0}(\mathbf{x})-\underline{\rho}_{0}(\mathbf{y})\right),\\
\widetilde{h}_{0}(\mathbf{x})=&\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(\underline{h}_{0}(\mathbf{x})-\underline{h}_{0}(\mathbf{y})\right).\end{split}

To let
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}
be the specific vorticity, we should keep the relation in (
1.4
). That is,

\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}.

(4.21)

Then
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}
is the specific vorticity. Since
s,s_{0}\in(2,\frac{5}{2})
, we can verify

\begin{split}&\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}\\
&\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|\underline{\rho}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}\\
&\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.22)

For
s>2
, using (
4.22
) and Sobolev imbeddings, we get

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s}}\leq 1.\end{split}

(4.23)

A calculating starting from (
4.21
) yields

\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}\nabla\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot(\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{x})-\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{y}))+\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}{\underline{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}.

(4.24)

Hence, we have

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\partial\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.

(4.25)

Substituting
\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=\mathrm{e}^{-{\underline{\rho}}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}
into (
4.24
), we can rewrite (
4.24
) as

\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho_{0}}}\nabla\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot(\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{x})-\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{y}))+\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho_{0}}+{\underline{\rho}}_{0}}\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}.

Therefore, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}&=\|\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho_{0}}}\nabla\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot(\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{x})-\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(\mathbf{y}))+\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{{\rho}_{0}}+{\underline{\rho}}_{0}}\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\\
&\lesssim\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}+\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}+\|\underline{\rho}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.26)

Adding (
4.25
) and (
4.26
), we can get

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.

(4.27)

By Proposition
4.2
, there is a smooth solution
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{h},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
on
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying the following equation(citing (
1.14
))

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}=-\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0}(y)}\tilde{c}^{2}_{s}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}+\widetilde{\mathbf{Q}},\\
&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\widetilde{\rho}=-\frac{1}{\gamma}\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\Delta\widetilde{h}+\widetilde{{D}},\\
&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\widetilde{h}=\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\Delta\widetilde{h}+\widetilde{E},\\
&\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}h=0,\\
&\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)+(\gamma-2)(\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0}(y))}\epsilon^{iab}\partial_{a}\widetilde{\rho}\partial_{b}\widetilde{h}.\end{cases}

(4.28)

Above, the quantities
\tilde{c}^{2}_{s}
and
\tilde{{g}}
are defined by

\begin{split}\tilde{c}^{2}_{s}:&=\frac{dp}{d{\rho}}(\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0}(y),\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{g}:&=g(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y),\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}:&=\partial_{t}+(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y))\cdot\nabla,\end{split}

(4.29)

and

\begin{split}\widetilde{Q}^{i}=&\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{h}+\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{1j}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v}^{j}+\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{2j}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{h}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v}^{j},\\
\widetilde{D}=&\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{h}+\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v}^{j}+\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{h}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v}^{j}\\
\widetilde{E}=&\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{h}+\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\rho}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v}^{j}+\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{h}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v}^{j},\end{split}

and
\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta},\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{1j},\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{2j},\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta},\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j},\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta},\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}
have the same formulations with
{Q}^{i\alpha\beta},{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{1j},{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{2j}
,
{D}^{\alpha\beta},{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j},{E}^{\alpha\beta},{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}
by replacing
({\mathbf{v}},{\rho},{h})
to
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y),\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0}(y))
. Furthermore, the specific vorticity
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}
satisfies

\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}=\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}.

(4.30)

Set

\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{+}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{-},\quad\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{-}:=-\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}.

(4.31)

By Proposition
4.2
again, we can find the solution of (
4.29
) satisfying

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\rho}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{h}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\leq C(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}),

(4.32)

and

\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},d\widetilde{\rho},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}+\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{+},d\widetilde{\rho},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq C(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}).

(4.33)

Furthermore, the linear equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\tilde{f}=0,\\
&\tilde{f}(t_{0},\cdot)=\widetilde{f}_{0},\ \partial_{t}\tilde{f}(t_{0},)=\widetilde{f}_{1},\end{cases}

(4.34)

admits a solution
\widetilde{f}\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([0,T],H_{x}^{r-1})
, and the following estimate holds:

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}},\quad k<r-1.

(4.35)

Consequently, the function
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
is also a solution of (
4.28
), and its initial data coincides with
(\mathbf{v}_{0},{\rho}_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
in
B(\mathbf{y},c+1)
. Giving the restrictions, for
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right)|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\quad(\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0})|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\\
\quad(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0})|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\\
\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},

(4.36)

where
\mathrm{K}^{y}=\left\{(t,\mathbf{x}):ct+|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|\leq c+1,|t|<1\right\}
, then the restrictions (
4.36
) solve (
1.1
)-(
1.3
) on
\mathrm{K}^{y}
. By finite speed of propagation, a smooth solution
(\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\rho},\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{w}})
solves (
1.13
) in
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where
\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\rho},\bar{h}
and
\bar{\mathbf{w}}
is denoted by

\begin{split}\bar{\mathbf{v}}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y),\ \ \ \ \ (t,\mathbf{x})\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\bar{\rho}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0}(y),\ \ \ \ \ \ (t,\mathbf{x})\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\bar{h}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\ \ \ \ \ \ (t,\mathbf{x})\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\bar{\mathbf{w}}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\mathrm{e}^{-\underline{{\rho}}_{0}(y)}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}},\qquad(t,x)\in\mathrm{K}^{y}.\end{split}

(4.37)

From (
4.37
), by time-space scaling
(t,\mathbf{x})
to
(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}\mathbf{x})
, we also obtain

\begin{split}&(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})=(\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\rho},\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{w}})(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}\mathbf{x}),\\
&(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})|_{t=0}=(\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\rho},\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{w}})(0,T^{-1}\mathbf{x})=(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}).\end{split}

(4.38)

Therefore, the function
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
defined in (
4.38
) is the solution of (
1.13
) according to the uniqueness of solutions, i.e. Corollary
3.6
. To obtain the energy estimates for
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, let us use the cartesian grid
3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and a corresponding smooth partition of unity

\textstyle{\sum}_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})=1,

such that the function
\psi
is supported in the unit ball. Therefore, we have

\begin{split}&\bar{\mathbf{v}}=\textstyle{\sum}_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y)),\\
&\bar{\rho}=\textstyle{\sum}_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\widetilde{\rho}+\underline{\rho}_{0}(y)),\\
&\bar{h}=\textstyle{\sum}_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)),\\
&\bar{\mathbf{w}}=e^{-\bar{\rho}}\textrm{curl}\bar{\mathbf{v}}.\end{split}

By (
4.33
) and (
4.37
), we can see

\begin{split}&\|d\bar{\mathbf{v}},d\bar{\rho},d\bar{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}+\|d\bar{\mathbf{v}},\partial\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{+},d\bar{\rho},d\bar{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\\
\leq&\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}(\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},d\widetilde{\rho},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}{C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}}+\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{+},d\widetilde{\rho},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\\
\leq&C(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}).\end{split}

(4.39)

Using (
4.20
), (
4.22
), (
4.27
), and (
4.32
), we shall obtain

\begin{split}&\|\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\rho},\bar{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{h}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\underline{\rho}_{0},\underline{h}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.40)

Taking changing of coordinates and using
{\delta_{*}}\in(0,s-2)
and
2<s_{0}<s
, we get

\begin{split}&\|d{\mathbf{v}},d{\rho},d{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}+\|d{\mathbf{v}},\partial{\mathbf{v}}_{+},d{\rho},d{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\\
\leq&T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+{\delta_{*}})}\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},d\widetilde{\rho},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}+\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{+},d\widetilde{\rho},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\\
\leq&C(T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+{\delta_{*}})}+T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+s_{0}-2)})(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\leq&C(T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+{\delta_{*}})}+T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+s_{0}-2)})(T^{s-\frac{3}{2}}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}+T^{s-\frac{3}{2}}\|{\rho}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+T^{s_{0}+1-\frac{3}{2}}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}+T^{s_{0}+1-\frac{3}{2}}\|{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\leq&C(\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|{\rho}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}).\end{split}

(4.41)

By using (
4.40
) and changing of coordinates from
(t,\mathbf{x})\rightarrow(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}\mathbf{x})
, we get

\begin{split}&\|{\mathbf{v}},{\rho},{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}=\|\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\rho},\bar{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.42)

Using Theorem
3.2
and (
4.41
), we can obtain that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
satisfies (
4.2
) and (
4.3
), which is stated in Proposition
4.1
. It remains for us to prove (
4.5
) in Proposition
4.1
.

We consider the following homogeneous linear wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}}f=0,\quad[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f,f_{t})|_{t=0}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H^{r-1}_{x},\quad 1\leq r\leq s+1.\end{cases}

(4.43)

For (
4.43
) is scaling invariant, so we transfer the system (
4.43
) to the localized linear equation

\begin{cases}\square_{\widetilde{g}}{f}^{y}=0,\quad[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
({f}^{y},{f}^{y}_{t})|_{t=0}=(f_{0}^{y},f^{y}_{1}),\end{cases}

(4.44)

where

\begin{split}&f_{0}^{y}=\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\widetilde{f}_{0}-\widetilde{f}_{0}(\mathbf{y})),\quad f^{y}_{1}=\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\widetilde{f}_{1},\\
&\widetilde{f}_{0}=f_{0}(T\mathbf{x}),\quad\widetilde{f}_{1}=f_{1}(T\mathbf{x}).\end{split}

(4.45)

Let

\widetilde{f}=\textstyle{\sum}_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})({f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(\mathbf{y}))

(4.46)

Using Proposition
4.2
again, for
k<r-1
, we shall obtain that

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d{f}^{y}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\widetilde{f}_{0}-\widetilde{f}_{0}(\mathbf{y}))\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\\
\leq&C(\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.47)

We again use the finite speed of propagation to conclude that
\widetilde{f}={f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(\mathbf{y})
on the region
K^{y}
. Using (
4.46
) and (
4.47
), so we get

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
=&\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d({f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(\mathbf{y}))\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
=&\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d{f}^{y}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.48)

By changing of coordinates from
(t,\mathbf{x})\rightarrow(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}\mathbf{x})
, we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}=&T^{k-\frac{1}{2}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(4.49)

Combing (
4.48
) with (
4.49
), it follows

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&CT^{r-1-k}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}})\\
\leq&C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.50)

Above, we also use
k<r-1
and
T\leq 1
. By using energy estimates for (
4.43
) and using (
4.41
), then we obtain

\begin{split}\|d{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\exp\{\|dg\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T]L^{\infty}_{x}}}\}\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\exp\{\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T]L^{\infty}_{x}}}\}\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.51)

By using

\begin{split}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}=&\|\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{t}fd\tau-f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\leq&T\|d{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}+\|{f}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.52)

Adding (
4.51
) and (
4.52
), so we have proved

\begin{split}\|{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.53)

Combining (
4.52
), (
4.53
), we have proved (
4.5
) and (
4.4
) in Proposition
4.1
. So we complete the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

5.
A bootstrap argument

For Proposition
4.2
is for the existence of small solutions, so we can consider it as a small pertubation of
(\mathbf{0},0,\mathbf{0},0)
. Therefore, the acoustic metric
g
in (
1.10
) is a pertubation near a flat Minkowski metric. So it’s possible for us to give a proof of Proposition
4.2
by a bootstrap argument, i.e. using Proposition
5.1
to prove Proposition
4.2
. To reduce the proof of Proposition
4.2
to Proposition
5.1
. Let us make some preparations. We denote
\mathbf{m}
being a standard Minkowski metric,

\mathbf{m}^{00}=-1,\quad\mathbf{m}^{ij}=\delta^{ij},\quad i,j=1,2,3.

Taking
\mathbf{v}=0,h=0,\rho=0
in
g
(please see the formulation of
g
in (
1.10
)), the inverse matrix of the metric
g
is

g^{-1}(0)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}-1&0&0&0&0\\
0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0&0&0\\
0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0&0\\
0&0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0\\
0&0&0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)\end{array}\right).

By a linear change of coordinates which preserves
dt
, we may assume that
g^{\alpha\beta}(0)=\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}
. Let
\chi
be a smooth cut-off function in the region
B(0,3+2c)\times[-2,2]
which equals to
1
in the region
B(0,2+2c)\times[-1,1]
and equals to
0
in
B(0,3+2c)\times[-2,-\frac{3}{2}]
. Set

\mathbf{g}=\chi(t,x)(g-g(0))+g(0).

(5.1)

Here

{g}=-dt\otimes dt+c_{s}^{-2}\sum_{a=1}^{3}\left(dx^{a}-v^{a}dt\right)\otimes\left(dx^{a}-v^{a}dt\right).

Now we are ready to consider the Cauchy problem of the following system

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}v^{i}=-\mathrm{e}^{\rho}c_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}+Q^{i},\\
\square_{\mathbf{g}}{\rho}=-\frac{1}{\gamma}c_{s}^{2}\Delta h+D,\\
\square_{\mathbf{g}}h=c_{s}^{2}\Delta h+E,\\
\mathbf{T}w^{i}=w^{a}\partial_{a}v^{i}+\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{h+(\gamma-2)\rho}\epsilon^{iab}\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{b}h.\end{cases}

(5.2)

where
Q^{i},E^{i}
(
i=1,2,3
), and
\mathcal{D}
has the same formulations in (
1.14
), and
\mathbf{g}
is defined in (
5.1
). We denote by
\mathcal{H}
the family of smooth solutions
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
to equation (
5.2
) for
t\in[-2,2]
, with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
supported in
B(0,2+c)
, and for which

\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

(5.3)

\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2},

(5.4)

\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh,\partial\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C_{x}^{\delta}}+\|d\rho,dh,\partial\mathbf{v}_{+},d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2}.

(5.5)

Therefore, the bootstrap argument can be stated as follows:

Proposition 5.1
.

Assume that (
1.29
) holds. Then there is a continuous functional
G:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
, satisfying
G(0)=0
, so that for each
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
satisfying
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
the following hold:

\mathrm{(1)}
The function
\mathbf{v},\rho,h
, and
\mathbf{w}
satisfies
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

\mathrm{(2)}
The following estimate holds,

\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(5.6)

\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}_{+},d\rho,dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

(5.7)

\mathrm{(3)}
For
1\leq k\leq s_{0}+1
, the linear wave equation (
4.14
) endowed with the metric
\mathbf{g}
is well-posed when
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
, and the Strichartz estimates (
4.15
) hold.

5.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.2
by Proposition
5.1

Note the initial data in Proposition
4.2
satisfing

\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.

We denote by
A
the subset of those
\gamma\in[0,1]
such that the equation (
5.2
) admits a smooth solution
(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},h_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
having the initial data

\begin{split}\mathbf{v}_{\gamma}(0)=&\gamma\mathbf{v}_{0},\\
\rho_{\gamma}(0)=&\gamma\rho_{0},\\
h_{\gamma}(0)=&\gamma h_{0},\\
\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}(0)=&\bar{\rho}\mathrm{e}^{-\rho_{\gamma}(0)}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}_{\gamma}(0),\end{split}

and such that
G(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},h_{\gamma})\leq\epsilon_{1}
and (
5.6
), (
5.7
) hold.

If
\gamma=0
, then

(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},h_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})(t,x)=(\mathbf{0},0,0,\mathbf{0}).

is a smooth solution of (
5.2
) with initial data

(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})(0,x)=(\mathbf{0},0,0,\mathbf{0}).

Thus, the set
A
is not empty. If we can prove that
\text{A}=[0,1]
, then
1\in\text{A}
. Also, (
4.11
)-(
4.15
) follow from Proposition
5.1
. As a result, Proposition
4.2
holds. It is sufficient to prove that
A
is both open and closed in
[0,1]
.

(1)
A
is open. Let
\gamma\in\text{A}
. Then
(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},h_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
is a smooth solution to (
5.2
), where

\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\rho_{\gamma}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}_{\gamma}.

Let
\beta
be close to
\gamma
. By the continuity of
G
, it follows that

G(\mathbf{v}_{\beta},\rho_{\beta},h_{\beta})\leq 2\epsilon_{1},

and also (
5.3
)-(
5.5
) holds. Using Proposition
5.1
, we have

G(\mathbf{v}_{\beta},\rho_{\beta},h_{\beta})\leq\epsilon_{1},

and the estimates (
5.6
), (
5.7
) hold. Thus, we have showed that
\beta\in\text{A}
.

(2)
A
is closed. Let
\gamma_{k}\in\text{A},k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Let
\gamma
be a limit satisfying
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\gamma_{k}=\gamma
.
Then there exists a sequence
\{(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},\rho_{\gamma_{k}},h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
being the smooth solutions to (
5.2
) and

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},\rho_{\gamma_{k}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},\partial_{t}\rho_{\gamma_{k}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|h_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\partial_{t}h_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|d\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},d\rho_{\gamma_{k}},dh_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C_{x}^{\delta}}+\|d\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},d\rho_{\gamma_{k}},dh_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

Then there exists a subsequence of
\{(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},\rho_{\gamma_{k}},h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
such that
(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},h_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
is the limit, and the limit satisfies

\begin{split}&\|(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\partial_{t}\rho_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|h_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\partial_{t}h_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|d\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},d\rho_{\gamma},dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}C_{x}^{\delta}}+\|d\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},d\rho_{\gamma},dh_{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\end{split}

Thus,
G(\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\rho_{\gamma},h_{\gamma})\leq\epsilon_{1}
, which implies
\gamma\in\text{A}
. This completes the proof of Proposition
4.2
.

6.
Regularity of the characteristic hypersurface

To prove Proposition
5.1
, we need to give a definition of
G
(please see (
6.2
)) and also prove the Strichartz estimtes (
5.6
)-(
5.7
). Inspired by Smith-Tataru’s result
[
47
]
for quasi-linear wave equatons, we try to find the character of the system (
5.2
) and analysing the regularity of characteristic hypersurface. Next, we will prove Proposition
5.1
by using Proposition
6.2
, Proposition
7.2
, and Proposition
7.4
.

Following
[
47
]
, for
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
,
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and let
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
be the flowout of the set
\theta\cdot\mathbf{x}=r-2
along the null geodesic flow with respect of
\mathbf{g}
in the direction
\theta
at
t=-2
.
Let
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\theta}
be given orthonormal coordinates on the hyperplane in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
perpendicular to
\theta
. Then,
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
is of the form

\Sigma_{\theta,r}=\left\{(t,\mathbf{x}):\theta\cdot\mathbf{x}-\phi_{\theta,r}=0\right\}

for a smooth function
\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\theta})
.
For a given
\theta
, the family
\{\Sigma_{\theta,r},\ r\in\mathbb{R}\}
defines a foliation of
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, by characteristic hypersurfaces with respect to
\mathbf{g}
.

We now introduce two norms for functions defined on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\begin{split}&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}=\sup_{-2\leq t\leq 2}\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\|\partial_{t}^{j}f(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-j}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\\
&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2}=\big(\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\int^{2}_{-2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}f(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-j}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}dt\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(6.1)

The same notation applies for functions in
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We denote

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|_{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2},

where the right hand side is the norm of the restriction of
f
to
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
, taken over the
(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\theta})
variables used to parametrise
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
. Similarly, the notation
\|f\|_{H^{s_{0}}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}
denotes the
H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
norm of
f
restricted to the time
t
slice of
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
using the
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\theta}
coordinates on
{\Sigma^{t}_{\theta,r}}
.

We now set

G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)=\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}.

(6.2)

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
so that
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}({\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-S_{j}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}),d\Delta_{j}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta},2^{-j}\partial_{x}\Delta_{j}d{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.3)

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
so that
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Then

G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.4)

Furthermore, for each
t
it holds that

\|d\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\cdot)-dt\|_{C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d{\mathbf{g}}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(6.5)

6.1.
Energy estimates on the characteristic hypersurface

Let
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Then the following estimates hold:

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v},\rho,h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s,\infty}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}+\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh,\partial\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh,\partial\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.6)

It is sufficient to prove Proposition
6.1
and Proposition
6.2
for
\theta=(0,0,1)
and
r=0
. We fix this choice, and suppress
\theta
and
r
in our notation. We use
(x_{3},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
instead of
(x_{\theta},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\theta})
, where
x_{\theta}=\theta\cdot\mathbf{x}
. Then
\Sigma
is defined by

\Sigma=\left\{x_{3}-\phi(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=0\right\}.

The hypothesis
G\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
implies that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\cdot)-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\leq 2\epsilon_{1}.

(6.7)

By Sobolev’s imbedding, we have

\|d\phi(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-dt\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\partial_{t}d\phi(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(6.8)

Let us now introduce two lemmas, which is from
[
47
]
.

Lemma 6.3
.

[
47
]

Let
\tilde{f}(t,\mathbf{x})=f(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}+\phi(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}))
. Then we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{f}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty},\quad\|d\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|df\|_{{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}},\quad\|\tilde{f}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}.

Lemma 6.4
.

[
47
]

For
r\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2},\\
\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma^{t})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

If
r>\frac{n+1}{2}
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}.

Similarly, if
r>\frac{n}{2}
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.

Lemma 6.5
.

Suppose
\mathbf{U}
satisfy the hyperbolic symmetric system

A^{0}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{t}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\mathbf{U}_{x_{i}}=0.

(6.9)

Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(6.10)

Proof.

Choosing the change of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
and setting
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}(t,x)=U(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}+\phi(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}))
, the system (
6.10
) is transformed to

A^{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A^{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{i}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\sum_{\alpha=0}^{3}A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}.

Since
\phi
is independent of
x_{3}
, this yields

A^{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A^{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{i}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\sum_{\alpha=0}^{2}A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}.

(6.11)

To prove (
6.10
), we first establish the
0
-order estimate. A direct calculation on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
shows that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\partial d\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x^{\prime}}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\partial d\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

By using Lemma
6.3
, (
6.6
) and (
6.8
), we can prove that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.

(6.12)

We now establish the
s_{0}
-order estimate. Taking the derivative of
\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}
(
1\leq|\beta|\leq s_{0}
) on (
6.11
) and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we get

\begin{split}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma}}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|,\end{split}

(6.13)

where

\begin{split}&I_{1}=-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau,\\
&I_{2}=-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau.\end{split}

We can write
I_{1}
as

\begin{split}I_{1}=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\\
&+\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\phi\cdot\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau,\\
=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}]\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\end{split}

We also write

\begin{split}I_{2}=&-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\\
&+\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\phi\big)\cdot\partial_{\alpha}(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau.\end{split}

By commutator estimates in Lemma
2.6
, we can get

\begin{split}|I_{1}|&\lesssim\big(\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|d\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\end{split}

(6.14)

and

\begin{split}|I_{2}|\lesssim&\big(\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|d\partial\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}d\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
&+\big(\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\partial^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(6.15)

Taking sum of
1\leq\beta\leq s_{0}
on (
6.13
), due to Lemma
6.3
, (
6.14
), (
6.15
), (
6.6
), and (
6.8
), we obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(6.16)

Taking derivatives on (
6.9
), we have

\begin{split}A^{0}(\mathbf{U})(\partial\mathbf{U})_{t}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})(\partial\mathbf{U})_{x_{i}}=-\sum^{3}_{\alpha=0}\partial(A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}))\mathbf{U}_{x_{\alpha}},\end{split}

In a similar process, we can obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(6.17)

Using
\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}=-\sum^{3}_{i=1}(A^{0})^{-1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}
and Lemma
6.4
, we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.18)

Combining (
6.12
), (
6.16
), (
6.18
), and (
6.18
), we obtain (
6.10
). Thus, the proof is finished.
∎

Using Lemma
6.5
, and combining with (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), we can obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 6.6
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Then the following estimate

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\rho|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}

(6.19)

holds.

The estimate (
6.19
) is not sufficient to prove Proposition
6.2
, for we also need the higher-order estimate of
h
and
\mathbf{w}
along
\Sigma
. The next goal is to establish the characteristic energy estimates for
\mathbf{w}
. Compared with the energy estimates for
\mathbf{w}
, the characteristic energy is along the hypersurface, not the Cauchy slices. Therefore, it’s not trivial. To prove the energy estimates of
\mathbf{w}
along the null hypersurface, let us give a lemma firstly.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
f
satisfy the following transport equation

\mathbf{T}f=F.

(6.20)

Set
L=\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
. Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\ \big(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

(6.21)

Proof.

Applying the operator
L
to (
6.20
), we have

\mathbf{T}Lf=LF+[L,\mathbf{T}]f.

Choosing the change of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
and setting
\widetilde{f}=f(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
, then the above equation transforms to

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{Lf}+\tilde{v}^{i}\cdot(\partial_{x_{i}}+\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{Lf}=&\widetilde{LF}+\widetilde{[L,\mathbf{T}]f}.\end{split}

Rewrite it as

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\widetilde{Lf}+(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{Lf}=&\widetilde{LF}+\widetilde{[L,\mathbf{T}]f}-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf}-(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf}.\end{split}

(6.22)

Multiplying
\widetilde{Lf}
and integrating it on
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can show that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ &\big|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}LF\cdot Lfdxd\tau\big|+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(1+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\ +\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\end{split}

(6.23)

Considering
L=\partial(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
, by commutator estimates in Lemma
2.16
, we get

\begin{split}\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.24)

By elliptic estimates, we also have

\|Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}.

(6.25)

By using (
6.23
), (
6.24
), and (
6.25
), it can give us

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ \|\partial v\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(1+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\big|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}LF\cdot Lfdxd\tau\big|.

(6.26)

It remains for us to estimate the high order term. Taking derivatives
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}
on (
6.22
), we have

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}+(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}=&\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\left(\widetilde{[L,\mathbf{T}]f}\right)-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf})\\
&+\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{LF}-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\{(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{Lf}\}\\
&-[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}]\widetilde{Lf}.\end{split}

(6.27)

Multiplying
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}
on (
6.27
) and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{Lf}\|_{L^{2}_{\Sigma}}\lesssim&\ \|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}({[L,\mathbf{T}]f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\ +\big|\int^{t}_{0}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\big|+\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\ +\big|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\{(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf}\}\big)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

We will estimate the right terms one by one. By using elliptic estimates, we can prove

\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}},

(6.28)

and

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}({[L,\mathbf{T}]f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\ \|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.29)

For the right term
\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}
in (
6.29
), by using Lemma
2.16
, we have

\begin{split}\|[L,\mathbf{T}]f\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}&\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,\infty}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\big(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\|f\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Substituting in (
6.29
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}({[L,\mathbf{T}]f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\big(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.30)

Using Lemma
2.18
, we also have

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq&\ \|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}Lf\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|Lf\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.31)

For
\phi
is independent with
x_{3}
, we have

\begin{split}&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\{(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf}\}\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
=\ &\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left([\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf})-[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}-(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
=\ &\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left([\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf})-[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\right)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x_{3}}\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau.\end{split}

(6.32)

We will estimate the right terms on (
6.32
). For the first one and the second one, we use Lemma
2.18
and Lemma
2.15
to bound

\begin{split}&\big(\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\big(\|\partial(\partial_{t}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\partial(\mathbf{v}\partial\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\big(\|\partial(d\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}_{x}}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}},\end{split}

(6.33)

where we take
\beta
=
s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon_{0}>s_{0}-2
. By Sobolev imbedding, we can get

\|\partial(d\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\|\partial(d\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}}\lesssim\|\partial(d\phi-dt)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}(\Sigma)}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma},

and

\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}_{x}}\leq 1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\beta}_{x}}\lesssim 1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}.

Substituting in (
6.33
), we can rewrite (
6.33
) as

\begin{split}&\big(\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}},(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]{Lf}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\big)\|Lf\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big(|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\big).\end{split}

(6.34)

The third term on the right hand side of (
6.32
) can be bounded by

\begin{split}&\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x_{3}}\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\right|\\
\lesssim&\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|Lf\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|f\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.35)

Sustituting (
6.34
) and (
6.35
), we can estimate (
6.32
) by

\begin{split}&\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf})-\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\{(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}{Lf}\}\big)\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{Lf}dxd\tau\right|\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big(|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\big)\\
\lesssim&\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\big(|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}})(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\big),\end{split}

(6.36)

where we use the fact that
\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}
.
Combining (
6.28
) to (
6.36
), we can get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\ \big(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

(6.37)

Adding (
6.26
) and (
6.37
), we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Lf|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\ \big(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{LF}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}Lfdxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

Therefore, we complete the proof of Lemma
6.7
.
∎

We also need to give the estimate of
\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}
along the characteristic hypersurfaces, for
\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}
is a nonlinear term in the wave equation of the velocity. Then,
\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}
decides the regularity of the characteristic hypersurfaces.

Lemma 6.8
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(6.38)

Proof.

By (
6.1
), we see

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}=&\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial_{t}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.\end{split}

(6.39)

We will divide it into several steps for estimating the right terms on (
6.39
).

Step 1:
\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. Noting (
2.6
), we can simply write it as

\mathbf{T}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}=(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla)v^{i}+J^{i}.

(6.40)

Here

\begin{split}J^{i}=&-\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}\mathrm{div}\mathbf{v}-2\epsilon^{imn}\partial_{m}v^{j}\partial_{n}w_{j}+\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\partial^{l}(\mathrm{e}^{h+(\gamma-2)\rho})\partial_{l}\rho\partial^{i}h\\
&-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-2}\partial^{l}(\mathrm{e}^{h+(\gamma-2)\rho})\partial^{i}\rho\partial_{l}h+\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\mathrm{e}^{h+(\gamma-2)\rho}\Delta\rho\partial^{i}h+\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\mathrm{e}^{h+(\gamma-2)\rho}\partial^{m}\rho\partial_{m}\partial^{i}h\\
&-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\mathrm{e}^{h+(\gamma-2)\rho}\partial_{m}\partial^{i}\rho\partial^{m}h-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\mathrm{e}^{h+(\gamma-2)\rho}\partial^{i}\rho\Delta h.\end{split}

By changing of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
, then (
6.40
) becomes to

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}}+(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}}=&(\widetilde{\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{v^{i}}+\widetilde{\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{j}}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{v^{i}}\\
&-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}}-(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\phi\partial_{x_{3}}\widetilde{\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}}+\widetilde{J}^{i}.\end{split}

(6.41)

Multiplying
\widetilde{\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i}}
and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}(1+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
&+\|\mathbf{J}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial d\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}(1+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).\end{split}

Using (
5.4
), and (
5.5
), we have

\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon^{3}_{2}.

(6.42)

It remains for us to bound
\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. Let us first estimate
\|\partial(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. By the Hodge’s decomposition,

\begin{split}\|\partial(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&=\|L(\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w})+H(\mathrm{div}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&=\|L(\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)},\end{split}

(6.43)

where the operators
L
and
H
are given by
\partial(-\Delta_{x})^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
and
\partial(-\Delta_{x})^{-1}\nabla
respectively. By (
2.7
), we can get

\begin{split}\mathbf{T}\underline{F}^{i}=\partial^{i}\mathcal{G}+K^{i}+\mathbf{T}(-\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j}),\end{split}

(6.44)

where we set
\underline{\mathbf{F}}=(\underline{F}^{1},\underline{F}^{2},\underline{F}^{3})
,
G=2\partial_{n}v_{a}\partial^{n}w^{a}
, and

\underline{F}^{i}=\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\rho\cdot\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{k}-2\partial^{a}{\rho}\partial^{i}w_{a}+2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\partial_{k}h.

Operating the operator
L
on (
6.44
), and using Lemma
6.7
, we can deduce that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\underline{\mathbf{F}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ &\ \big(\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|\underline{\mathbf{F}}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{LK^{i}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left\{(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left\{\mathbf{T}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

(6.45)

We set

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}&=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{LK^{i}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|,\\
\mathrm{J}&=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left\{(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial^{3})\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|,\\
\bar{\mathrm{J}}&=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left\{\mathbf{T}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

For
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
, we use Hölder’s inequality to give the bound

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}\leq&\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}L\mathbf{K}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}L\underline{\mathbf{F}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{K}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|\underline{\mathbf{F}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.46)

Using (
2.9
) and Sobolev inequalities, we get

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{K}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|d\rho,d\mathbf{v},dh\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+\|d\rho,d\mathbf{v},dh\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+\|d\rho,d\mathbf{v},dh\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}.\end{split}

(6.47)

By Hölder’s inequality, we also get

\|\underline{\mathbf{F}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}})^{2}.

(6.48)

Inserting (
6.47
) and (
6.48
) into (
6.69
) and using (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}\lesssim&\ \epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.49)

For
\mathrm{J}
, we separate it as

\mathrm{J}\leq\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}(|J^{1}|+|J^{2}|+|J^{3}|+|J^{4}|+|J^{5}|+|J^{6}|+|J^{7}|+|J^{8}|),

where

\begin{split}J_{1}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}dxd\tau,\\
J_{2}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}](\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}dxd\tau,\\
J_{3}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{i}w^{a}\big)}dxd\tau,\\
J_{4}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{i}w^{a}\big)}dxd\tau\\
J_{5}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}dxd\tau,\\
J_{6}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}dxd\tau,\\
J_{7}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}dxd\tau,\\
J_{8}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}dxd\tau.\end{split}

Let us bound the above terms one by one. For
J_{1}
, we have

\begin{split}J_{1}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}dxd\tau\\
=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}})\cdot(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\big\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}\big\}dxd\tau\\
=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}})\cdot[\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}]\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}})\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\big\{(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}\big\}dxd\tau.\end{split}

Using the fact

\begin{split}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}&=(\partial^{i}L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))\\
&=\{L(\mathrm{div}\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})\}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))=0,\end{split}

then

\begin{split}J_{1}=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{L\mathcal{G}})\cdot[\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}]\widetilde{(L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{i})}dxd\tau.\end{split}

For
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
, by Hölder’s inequality and commutator estimates in Lemma
2.18
, we have

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}(|J_{1}|+|J_{2}|)=&\|L\mathcal{G}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{{H}_{x}^{s}}).\end{split}

(6.50)

For
J_{3}
, we use the Plancherel formula in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
which gives

\begin{split}J_{3}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{i}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{i}w^{a}\big)}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x_{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\partial^{i}\phi\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{i}w^{a}\big)}dxd\tau\\
=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial^{i}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{i}w^{a}\big)}dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x_{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\partial^{i}\phi\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-2\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{i}w^{a}\big)}\right)dxd\tau.\end{split}

(6.51)

For
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
, by Hölder’s inequality, we can obtain

\begin{split}&\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}|J_{3}|\\
=&\|\partial\mathbf{v}\partial\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\|\partial\rho\partial\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}+\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\partial\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\|\partial\rho\partial\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\rho\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\end{split}

By using (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we get

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}|J_{3}|\lesssim&\ \epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.52)

For
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
, by Hölder’s inequality, Lemma
2.18
, and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we have

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}|J_{4}|\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}(\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}})\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.53)

Calculate

\begin{split}&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\\
=&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\right\}\\
&\ -\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\\
&\ +\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\\
=&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\right\}\\
&\ -\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\partial^{i}L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\\
&\ +\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}.\end{split}

(6.54)

Taking derivatives, we have

\begin{split}\partial^{i}L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)=&L\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{i}\rho\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)+L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{i}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big),\end{split}

so (
6.54
) yields

\begin{split}&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\\
=&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\}\\
&\ -\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{i}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\\
&\ +\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{i}\rho\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}\\
&\ +\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h\big)}.\end{split}

(6.55)

Here we use the fact that
2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{i}\partial^{j}v_{m}\partial^{m}\partial^{k}h=0
.

Inserting (
6.55
) in
J_{5}
, and using Hölder’s inequality, (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we obtain

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}|J_{5}|\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+(1+\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})(\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+(1+\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})(\|\rho\|^{5}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{5}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{5}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{5}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.56)

For
J_{7}
, we use a similar idea on handling
J_{5}
. Calculate

\begin{split}&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}\\
=&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}\right\}\\
&-\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}\\
&+\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}\\
=&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}\right\}\\
&-\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\{\partial^{i}\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)\}}\\
&+\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}\\
=&(\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}})\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)}\right\}\\
&+\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\{\big(-2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial^{i}\rho\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}+\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial^{k}h\Delta(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\mathbf{w}_{k})\big)\}}\\
&+\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\big(2\partial_{n}v^{a}\partial^{n}w_{a}\big)}\cdot[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\partial^{i}+\partial^{i}\phi\partial_{x_{3}}]\widetilde{L\big(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j}\big)},\end{split}

(6.57)

where we use the fact
\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon_{ijk}\partial^{i}(\partial^{k}h\Delta v^{j})=\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial^{k}h\Delta(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\mathbf{w}_{k})
. Inserting (
6.57
) to
J_{7}
, we get

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}|J_{7}|\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+(1+\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})(\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.58)

By Lemma
2.18
and Hölder’s inequality, we have
