\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}(|J_{6}|+|J_{8}|)\lesssim&\|d\partial\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}(\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\|d\partial\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}(\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}(\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.59)

Combining (
6.50
), (
6.52
), (
6.53
), (
6.56
), (
6.58
), and (
6.59
), we know

\mathrm{J}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.

(6.60)

For
\bar{\mathrm{J}}
, we calculate

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}\leq&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\left\{\mathbf{T}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|\\
\leq&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\mathbf{T},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}]\left\{\widetilde{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{T}\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|\\
=&\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{1}+\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{2},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{1}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\mathbf{T},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}]\left\{\widetilde{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|,\\
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{2}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{T}\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\right\}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}dxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.18
and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we have

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{1}\lesssim&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\int^{2}_{-2}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial h\Delta\mathbf{v}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\sigma}}\|\underline{\mathbf{F}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\sigma}}d\tau\\
\lesssim&\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-1}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.61)

For
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{2}=&\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{21}+\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{22}+\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{23},\end{split}

where

\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{21}=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{d}{dt}\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}\right\}dxd\tau|,\ \ \ \ \ \

\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{22}=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)\left\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}\right\}dxd\tau|,

\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{23}=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\mathbf{T}\{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}}\}dxd\tau|.\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

For
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{21}
, we calculate that

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{21}=&\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})(t)\\
&+(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})(t)\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.62)

By Lemma
2.18
, we can estimate
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{22}
by

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{22}=&\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}\\
&+\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.63)

We then bound
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{23}
by

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{23}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot[\mathbf{T},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}](\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}})dxd\tau|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\{\mathbf{T}(\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}})\}dxd\tau|\\
\leq&\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{231}+\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{232}+\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{233}+\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{234}.\end{split}

(6.64)

Here we set

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{231}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot[\mathbf{T},\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}](\widetilde{L\underline{F}_{i}})dxd\tau|,\\
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{232}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\{\partial_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}+K_{i}\}dxd\tau|,\\
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{233}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma},\mathbf{T}]\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}dxd\tau|,\\
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{234}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-2\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\mathbf{T}\widetilde{\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-2\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}dxd\tau|.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.18
and Hölder’s inequality, we get

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{231}+\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{233}\lesssim&\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}\\
&+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}).\end{split}

(6.65)

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{232}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\{\partial_{i}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}+\widetilde{K}_{i}\}dxd\tau|\\
=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{K}_{i}dxd\tau|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\partial_{i}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}dxd\tau|.\end{split}

(6.66)

By using
-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{i}(\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})=\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial_{k}h\Delta(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\mathbf{w}^{k})
, (
6.66
) yields

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{232}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{K}_{i}dxd\tau|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial_{i}\rho\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{G}dxd\tau|\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial_{k}h\Delta(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\mathbf{w}^{k})\}}\cdot\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{G}dxd\tau|\\
\lesssim&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\|\partial h\Delta\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}\|\mathbf{K}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}+\|\partial\rho\partial h\Delta\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}\\
&\quad+(\|\partial h\Delta\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}+\|\partial h\partial^{2}\rho\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}})\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&(\|d\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|d\rho\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|dh\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\{\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H^{s}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}\}.\end{split}

(6.67)

Integrating by parts on
\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{234}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{234}=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{T}|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\widetilde{\{L(-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h\Delta v_{j})\}}|^{2}dxd\tau\\
\leq&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\|\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\partial h\Delta v\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}(1+\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}})(1+\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}).\end{split}

(6.68)

By using (
6.65
), (
6.67
), (
6.68
), and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we prove that

\bar{\mathrm{J}}_{2}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.

(6.69)

The estimate (
6.69
) adding to (
6.61
), we have

\bar{\mathrm{J}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.

(6.70)

Substituing (
6.49
), (
6.60
), (
6.70
) to (
6.45
) and using (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), (
6.45
) yields

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\underline{\mathbf{F}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.71)

For

L\underline{F}^{i}=L(\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i})+L(\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\rho\cdot\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{k})-2L(\partial^{a}{\rho}\partial^{i}w_{a})+2L(\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\partial_{k}h).

we then get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\underline{F}^{i}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\geq&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}-2|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\partial^{a}{\rho}\partial^{i}w_{a})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\\
&-2|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\partial_{k}h)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}-|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\rho\cdot\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{k})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|.\end{split}

(6.72)

By trace theorem and elliptic estimate, we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L\left(\partial_{a}\rho\partial^{i}w^{a}\right)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\leq&C\|L\left(\partial_{a}\rho\partial^{i}w^{a}\right)\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}+(s-s_{0})}_{x}}\\
\leq&C\|\partial_{a}\rho\partial^{i}w^{a}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}+(s-s_{0})}_{x}}\\
\leq&C\|\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.73)

The same technique tells us

\begin{split}&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\rho\cdot\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{k})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\mathrm{e}^{-\rho}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{m}\partial_{m}\partial_{k}h)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\\
\leq&C(\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|h\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}})\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.74)

Substituting (
6.72
), (
6.73
), and (
6.74
) to (
6.71
), and using (
6.43
), we therefore get

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|L(\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\ \epsilon^{2}_{2}\lesssim\ \epsilon_{2}.

(6.75)

Due to

\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{curl}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}=\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\cdot(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}},

by Hölder inequality and product estimates in Lemma
2.7
, we can deduce the following estimate

\begin{split}\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&\lesssim\|\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\cdot(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.76)

Above, we use the trace theorem

\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}
.
Through (
6.42
) and (
6.76
), we can see

\|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.77)

Step 2:
\|\partial_{t}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. By using (
2.6
) and Sobolev imbeddings, we also have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&\leq\|(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\cdot\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\leq\|(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\cdot\partial\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\leq\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\cdot\partial\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-\frac{3}{2}}_{x}},\\
&\leq\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\|\partial\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\partial\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.78)

Due to (
6.77
), (
6.78
), and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we have proved (
6.38
).
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(6.79)

Proof.

Let us recall (
2.48
). By changing of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
, the equation (
2.48
) becomes to

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\left\{\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})\right\}+(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla)\left\{\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})\right\}=&\widetilde{-2\partial^{k}(\partial_{i}v^{j})\partial_{j}\partial^{i}h}+\widetilde{Y^{k}}\\
&-(\partial_{t}\phi+\tilde{v}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{3}\left\{\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})\right\}.\end{split}

(6.80)

Here we consider
\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})
as a whole. Taking derivatives
\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}
(
\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}
) on (
6.80
), then multiplying
\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\left\{\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})\right\}
and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can obtain

\begin{split}&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\leq\mathrm{Q_{1}}+\mathrm{Q_{2}}+\mathrm{Q_{3}}+\mathrm{Q_{4}}+\mathrm{Q_{5}}.\end{split}

(6.81)

where we denote

\begin{split}\mathrm{Q_{1}}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\mathrm{div}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}|\cdot|\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}}|^{2}dxd\tau,\\
\mathrm{Q_{2}}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{Y^{k}}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})dxd\tau|,\\
\mathrm{Q_{3}}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}},\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla]\{\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})\}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})dxd\tau|,\\
\mathrm{Q_{4}}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial_{t}\phi+\tilde{v}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{3}\{\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})\}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})dxd\tau|,\\
\mathrm{Q_{5}}=&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{-2\partial^{k}(\partial_{i}v^{j})\partial_{j}\partial^{i}h}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\widetilde{\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}})dxd\tau|.\end{split}

By Hölder’s inequality, we can bound
\mathrm{Q_{1}}
by

\mathrm{Q_{1}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.

(6.82)

By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.10
, we can bound
\mathrm{Q_{2}}
by

\begin{split}\mathrm{Q_{2}}\lesssim&\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\|\mathbf{Y}\|_{L^{1}_{t}\dot{H}^{\sigma}_{x}}\|\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}\|_{\dot{H}^{1+\sigma}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{3}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{3}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{3}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
&+\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2},\end{split}

(6.83)

where
\mathbf{Y}=(Y^{1},Y^{2},Y^{3})
. By Lemma
2.18
, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Q_{3}}\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\rho\|^{4}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.84)

Integrating
\mathrm{Q_{4}}
by parts, we have

\mathrm{Q_{4}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\phi\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}})\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2},

(6.85)

where we use the fact that
\phi
is independent with
x_{3}
. For
\mathrm{Q_{5}}
, we use the similar idea on estimating
\tilde{I}_{4}
, i.e. (
3.44
)-(
3.58
), we then conclude that

\begin{split}\mathrm{Q_{5}}\leq&C\|\rho\|_{{H}^{2}_{x}}\|h\|_{{H}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\|h\|_{{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+C\|h\|_{{H}^{\frac{5}{2}+}_{x}}\|\rho\|_{{H}^{2}_{x}}\|\rho\|_{{H}^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h\|_{{H}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|h\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{5}{2}+}}\|\rho\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}}d\tau+C\int^{t}_{0}(\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{{H}^{2}_{x}})\|h\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|\rho\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|(\partial\mathbf{v},\partial\rho,\partial h)\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|(h,\rho,\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|(\rho,\mathbf{v})\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|(\partial\mathbf{v},\partial\rho,\partial h)\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|(h,\rho,\mathbf{v})\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|(\rho,\mathbf{v})\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|(\partial\mathbf{v},\partial\rho,\partial h)\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|(h,\rho,\mathbf{v})\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|h\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|(\rho,\mathbf{v})\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{x}})d\tau.\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.86)

Here we use (
3.61
) and the assumptions (
5.3
)-(
5.5
). Gathering (
6.82
), (
6.83
),
\cdots
, (
6.86
), yields

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial^{k}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},\quad k=1,2,3.

Due to

\partial_{x^{\prime}}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})=\partial(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})\cdot(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}},

by Hölder’s inequality and product estimates in Lemma
2.7
, we can deduce the following estimate

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{x^{\prime}}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.87)

Utilizing (
2.47
) and product estimates, we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}=&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial\mathbf{v}\partial^{2}h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{w}\partial\rho\partial h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\\
\lesssim&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\nabla(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}+1,2,\Sigma}\\
&+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\rho|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}+1,2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

(6.88)

Using (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), (
6.88
) yields

\frac{1}{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.89)

Combining (
6.87
) and (
6.89
), we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.90)

By calculating
\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}=\Delta h-\partial_{i}\rho\partial^{i}h
, we can infer that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\leq&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{i}\rho\partial^{i}h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\\
\leq&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{i}H^{i}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{i}\rho|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial^{i}h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Here we use that
H_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}(\Sigma)
is a algebra for
s_{0}>2
.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
\mathbf{U}=(p(\rho),\mathbf{v},h)^{\mathrm{T}}
satisfy the assumption in Lemma
6.5
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-S_{j}\mathbf{U}),dS_{j}\mathbf{U},2^{-j}d\partial_{x}S_{j}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.91)

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{0}
be a standard multiplier of order
0
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, such that
\Delta_{0}
is additionally bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Clearly,

A^{0}(\mathbf{U})(\Delta_{0}U)_{t}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})(\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U})_{x_{i}}=-[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\mathbf{U}.

Due to Lemma
6.5
, we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.

(6.92)

By commutator estimates, we obtain

\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.

Hence, (
6.92
) yields

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.

(6.93)

To control the norm of
2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-S_{j}\mathbf{U})
, we write

2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-S_{j}\mathbf{U})=2^{j}\sum_{m\geq j}\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U},

where
\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U}
satisfies the above conditions for
\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}
. Applying (
6.93
) and replacing
s_{0}
to
s_{0}-1
, we get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-S_{j}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\sum_{m\geq j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{m}\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\sum_{m\geq j}(2^{m}\|\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}})\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

Taking square of it, we can see

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-S_{j}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Finally, applying (
6.92
) to
\Delta_{0}=S_{j}
and
\Delta_{0}=2^{-j}\partial_{x}S_{j}
shows that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|dS_{j}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{-j}d\partial_{x}S_{j}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
6.10
is completed.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
6.1
.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

Note
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Using Lemma
6.10
,
it is sufficient to verify that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

By Lemma
6.5
and (
6.6
)-(
6.8
), we have

\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\rho|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Noting the expression of
\mathbf{g}
, by Lemma
6.4
, we derive that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|c_{s}^{2}-c_{s}^{2}(0)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Thus, the conclusion of Proposition
6.1
holds.
∎

To prove Proposition
6.2
, let us introduce a null frame of
\Sigma
and give estimates of the coefficients of connection.

6.2.
The null frame

We introduce a null frame along
\Sigma
as follows. Let

V=(dr)^{*},

where
r
is the defining function of the foliation
\Sigma
, and where
*
denotes the identification of covectors and vectors induced by
\mathbf{g}
. Then
V
is the null geodesic flow field tangent to
\Sigma
. Let

\sigma=dt(V),\qquad l=\sigma^{-1}V.

(6.94)

Thus
l
is the g-normal field to
\Sigma
normalized so that
dt(l)=1
, hence

l=\left<dt,dx_{3}-d\phi\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}\left(dx_{3}-d\phi\right)^{*},

(6.95)

so the coefficients
l^{j}
are smooth functions of
v,\rho
and
d\phi
. Conversely,

dx_{3}-d\phi=\left<l,\partial_{x_{3}}\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}l^{*},

(6.96)

so that
d\phi
is a smooth function of
\mathbf{v},\rho,h
and the coefficients of
l
.

Next we introduce the vector fields
e_{a},a=1,2
tangent to the fixed-time slice
\Sigma^{t}
of
\Sigma
. We do this by applying Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization in the metric
\mathbf{g}
to the
\Sigma^{t}
-tangent vector fields
\partial_{x_{a}}+\partial_{x_{a}}\phi\partial_{x_{3}},a=1,2
.

Finally, we let

\underline{l}=l+2\partial_{t}.

It follows that
\{l,\underline{l},e_{1},e_{2}\}
form a null frame in the sense that

\displaystyle\left<l,\underline{l}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=2,\qquad\qquad\ \left<e_{a},e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\delta_{ab}\ (a,b=1,2),

\displaystyle\left<l,l\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\left<\underline{l},\underline{l}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=0,\quad\left<l,e_{a}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\left<\underline{l},e_{a}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=0\ (a=1,2).

The coefficients of each of the fields is a smooth function of
u
and
d\phi
, and by assumption we also have the pointwise bound

|e_{1}-\partial_{x_{1}}|+|e_{2}-\partial_{x_{2}}|+|l-(\partial_{t}+\partial_{x_{3}})|+|\underline{l}-(-\partial_{t}+\partial_{x_{3}})|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

After that, we can state the following corollary concerning to the decomposition of curvature tensor.

Lemma 6.11
.

[
47
]

Suppose
f
satisfies

\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}f=F.

Let
(t,x^{\prime},\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
denote the parametrization of
\Sigma
, and for
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2
, let
/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
denote differentiation along
\Sigma
in the induced coordinates. Then, for
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2
, one can write

/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\beta}(f|_{\Sigma})=l(f_{2})+f_{1},

where

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|df\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.

Corollary 6.12
.

Let
R
be the Riemann curvature tensor of the metric
{\mathbf{g}}
. Let
e_{0}=l
. Then for any
0\leq a,b,c,d\leq 2
, we can write

\left<R(e_{a},e_{b})e_{c},e_{d}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}|_{\Sigma}=l(f_{2})+f_{1},

(6.97)

where
|f_{1}|\lesssim|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}|+|d\mathbf{g}|^{2}+|\Delta h|
and
|f_{2}|\lesssim|d{\mathbf{g}}|
. Moreover, the characteristic energy estimates

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

(6.98)

holds. Additionally, for any
t\in[-2,2]
,

\|f_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(6.99)

Proof.

According to the expression of curvature tensor, we have

\left<R(e_{a},e_{b})e_{c},e_{d}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=R_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}e^{\alpha}_{a}e^{\beta}_{b}e_{c}^{\mu}e_{d}^{\nu},

where

R_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}+\partial^{2}_{\beta\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\mu}-\partial^{2}_{\beta\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\nu}-\partial^{2}_{\alpha\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\mu}\right]+Q(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},d\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\beta}),

where
Q
is a sum of products of coefficients of
\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}
with quadratic forms in
d\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\beta}
. By using Proposition
6.1
, then
Q
satisfies the bound required of
f_{1}
. It is sufficient to consider

\frac{1}{2}e^{\alpha}_{a}e^{\beta}_{b}e_{c}^{\mu}e_{d}^{\nu}\left[\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}+\partial^{2}_{\beta\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\mu}-\partial^{2}_{\beta\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\nu}-\partial^{2}_{\alpha\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\mu}\right].

We therefore look at the term
e^{\alpha}_{a}e_{c}^{\mu}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}
, which is typical. By (
6.7
), Proposition
6.1
, and Lemma
6.7
, we get

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|l^{\alpha}-\delta^{\alpha 0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\underline{l}^{\alpha}+\delta^{\alpha 0}-2\delta^{\alpha n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|e^{\alpha}_{a}-\delta^{\alpha a}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(6.100)

By (
6.100
) and Proposition
6.1
, the term
e_{a}(e_{c}^{\mu})\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}
satisfies the bound required of
f_{1}
, so we consider
e_{a}(e_{c}(\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}))
. Finally, since the coefficients of
e_{c}
in the basis
/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
have tangential derivatives bounded in
L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)
, we are reduced by Lemma
6.11
to verifying that

\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Note

\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}=\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}.

By Lemma
6.8
, Lemma
6.9
, and Corollary
6.6
, we have

\begin{split}\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\ \|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}h\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|(d{\mathbf{g}})^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Delta h\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|d{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Delta h\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Delta h\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Above,
\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w},\Delta h
and
(d{\mathbf{g}})^{2}
are included in
f_{1}
. We hence complete the proof of Corollary
6.12
.
∎

6.3.
Estimate of the connection coefficients

Define

\chi_{ab}=\left<D_{e_{a}}l,e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}},\qquad l(\ln\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}\left<D_{l}\underline{l},l\right>_{\mathbf{g}},\quad\mu_{0ab}=\left<D_{l}e_{a},e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}.

For
\sigma
, we set the initial data
\sigma=1
at the time
-2
. Thanks to Proposition
6.1
, we have

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|l(\ln\sigma)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\mu_{0ab}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(6.101)

In a similar way, if we expand
l=l^{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
in the tangent frame
\partial_{t},\partial_{x^{\prime}}
on
\Sigma
, then

l^{0}=1,\quad\|l^{1}\|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(6.102)

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
\chi
be defined as before. Then

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.103)

Furthermore, for any
t\in[-2,2]
,

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(6.104)

Proof.

The well-known transport equation for
\chi
along null hypersurfaces (see references
[
29
]
and
[
47
]
) can be described as

l(\chi_{ab})=\left<R(l,e_{a})l,e_{b}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}-\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}+\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}+\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac}.

Due to Corollary
6.12
, we can write the above equation in the following

l(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})=f_{1}-\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}+\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}+\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac},

(6.105)

where

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

(6.106)

and for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|f_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(6.107)

Let
\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}
be the fractional derivative operator in the
x^{\prime}
variables. Set

G=f_{1}-\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}+\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}+\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac}.

We have

\begin{split}&\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
\lesssim\ &\|[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1},l](\chi_{ab}-f_{2})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}G\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.\end{split}

(6.108)

A direct calculation shows that

\begin{split}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s-1}G\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}&\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\chi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
&\quad+\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\cdot\|l(\ln\sigma)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
&\quad+\|\mu\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\cdot\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})},\end{split}

(6.109)

where we use the fact that
H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
is an algebra for
s_{0}>2
.

We next bound

\displaystyle\|[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1},l](\chi_{ab}-f_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}

\displaystyle\leq\|/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}l^{\alpha}(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}

\displaystyle\quad\ +\|[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha},l^{\alpha}](\chi-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.

By Kato-Ponce commutator estimate and Sobolev embeddings, the above is bounded by

\|l^{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.

(6.110)

Gathering (
6.101
), (
6.102
), (
6.106
) and (
6.108
)-(
6.110
) together, we thus prove that

\sup_{t}\|(\chi_{ab}-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

From (
6.105
), we see that

\begin{split}\|\chi_{ab}-f_{2}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}&\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\chi_{ac}\chi_{cb}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|l(\ln\sigma)\chi_{ab}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad+\|\mu_{0ab}\chi_{cb}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\mu_{0bc}\chi_{ac}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(6.111)

By Sobolev imbedding
H^{s_{0}-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow C^{\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\delta\in(0,s_{0}-2)
and Gronwall’s inequality, we derive that

\|\chi_{ab}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

∎

6.4.
The proof of Proposition
6.2

Note

G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}.

Using (
6.96
) and the estimate of
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}
in Proposition
6.1
, then the estimate (
6.4
) follows from the bound

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

where it is understood that one takes the norm of the coefficients of
l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})
in the standard frame on
\mathbb{R}^{3+1}
. The geodesic equation, together with the bound for Christoffel symbols
\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|d{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}
, imply that

\|l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

so it is sufficient to bound the tangential derivatives of the coefficients of
l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})
in the norm
L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)
. By using Proposition
6.1
, we can estimate the Christoffel symbols

\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Note that
H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
is a algebra if
s_{0}>2
. We then deduce that

\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}e_{1}^{\beta}l^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

We are now in a position to establish the following bound,

\|\left<D_{e_{a}}l,e_{b}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{e_{a}}l,\underline{l}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{l}l,\underline{l}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

The first term is
\chi_{ab}
, which has been bounded by Lemma
6.13
. For the second term, noting

\left<D_{e_{a}}l,\underline{l}\right>=\left<D_{e_{a}}l,2\partial_{t}\right>=-2\left<D_{e_{a}}\partial_{t},l\right>,

then it can be bounded via Proposition
6.1
. Similarly, we can control the last term through Proposition
6.1
. At this stage, it remains for us to show that

\|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt\|_{C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

To do that, it is sufficient to show that

\|l(t,\cdot)-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{3}})\|_{C^{1,\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

The coefficients of
e_{1}
are small in
C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
perturbations of their constant coefficient analogs, so it is sufficient to show that

\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,e_{1}\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,\underline{l}\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Above, the first term is bounded by Lemma
6.13
, and the second term can be estimated by using

\|\left<D_{e_{1}}\partial_{t},l\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Consequently, we complete the proof of Proposition
6.2
.

7.
Strichartz estimates

In this part, let us prove the Strichartz estimates of linear wave equations including in Proposition
5.1
. To state it clearly, let us introduce it as follows.

Proposition 7.1
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. For each
1\leq r\leq s+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[-2,2]
, then the linear, homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0,\qquad\quad[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1},\end{cases}

is well-posed on the time-interval
[-2,2]
if the initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
. Moreover, the solution satisfies, respectively, the energy estimates

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),

and the Strichartz estimates
3
3

3

here the constant
C
is universal if the solutions
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{{H}}
.

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),\quad k<r-1.

(7.1)

We will postpone the proof of Proposition
7.1
to Section 9. Based on Proposition
7.1
and Lemma
2.14
, we have the following result.

Proposition 7.2
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
.
For any
1\leq r\leq s+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[-2,2]
, the linear, non-homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=\mathbf{T}F,\qquad(t,x)\in(t_{0},2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0}\in H_{x}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\\
&\partial_{t}f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1}-F(t_{0},\cdot)\in H_{x}^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\end{cases}

admites a solution
f\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-2,2],H_{x}^{r-1})
and the following estimates holds:

\begin{split}\|f\|_{L_{[-2,2]}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L_{[-2,2]}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r-1}}\lesssim&\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|F\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{r}}.\end{split}

(7.2)

Additionally, the following estimates hold, provided
k<r-1
,

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|F\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{r}},

(7.3)

and the similar estimates also hold when we repace
\left<\partial\right>^{k}
by
\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d
.

Proof.

Let
V
satisfy the the linear, homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}V=0,\\
&V(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}V(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1},\end{cases}

(7.4)

Let
Q
satisfy the the linear, nonhomogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}Q=\mathbf{T}F,\\
&Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=0,\quad\partial_{t}Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=-F(t_{0},x),\end{cases}

(7.5)

By superposition principle for linear wave equation, and referring (
7.4
) and (
7.5
), then
f=V+Q
satisfies

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=\mathbf{T}F,\\
&(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(f_{0},f_{1}-F(t_{0},x)).\end{cases}

Using Proposition
7.1
, it follows

\|V\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}V\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),

(7.6)

and

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}V\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),\quad k<r-1.

(7.7)

To estimate
Q
, we need to rewrite (
7.5
). Using
\mathbf{T}=\partial_{t}+\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla
,
Q(t_{0},x)=0
, and
\partial_{t}Q(t_{0},x)=-F(t_{0},x)
, it yields

\mathbf{T}Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=\partial_{t}Q(t_{0},x)+(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)Q(t_{0},x)=-F(t_{0},x).

(7.8)

Combining (
7.5
) and (
7.8
), we obtain

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}Q=\mathbf{T}F,\\
&Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=0,\quad\mathbf{T}Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=-F(t_{0},x).\end{cases}

(7.9)

Using Lemma
2.14
and Proposition
7.1
again, we can prove

\|Q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}Q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq C\|F\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{r}},

(7.10)

and

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}Q\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\|F\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{r}},\quad k<r-1.

(7.11)

Adding (
7.6
) and (
7.10
), we get (
7.2
). Adding (
7.7
) and (
7.11
), we get (
7.3
). At this stage, we have finished the proof of Proposition
7.2
.
∎

Proposition 7.3
.

Suppose
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Let
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
. Let
\mathbf{v}_{+}
be defined in (
1.11
). Then for
k<s-1
, we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h),\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}}\\
&+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}},\end{split}

(7.12)

and the similar estimates hold if we repalce
\left<\partial\right>^{k}
with
\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d
.

Proof.

Let us recall that
\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h
and
\mathbf{v}_{+}
satisfy the equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h)=D+\frac{1}{\gamma}E,\\
&\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{v}_{+}=\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}+\mathbf{Q}.\end{cases}

(7.13)

Using Lemma
2.20
, we get

\|D,E,\mathbf{Q}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}.

(7.14)

Operating
\Delta_{j}
on (
7.13
) and matching its initial data, then
\Delta_{j}\rho
satisfies the following Cauchy problems

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}\Delta_{j}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h)=\Delta_{j}(D+\frac{1}{\gamma}E)+[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}](\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h),\\
&\Delta_{j}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h)|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}\rho_{0}+\frac{1}{\gamma}\Delta_{j}h_{0},\\
&\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h)|_{t=0}=-\Delta_{j}\mathrm{div}\mathbf{v}_{0},\end{cases}

(7.15)

and

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}=\mathbf{T}\{\Delta_{j}(\mathbf{T}v_{-}^{i})\}+\Delta_{j}Q^{i}+[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]v^{i}_{+}+[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}](\mathbf{T}v_{-}^{i}),\\
&\Delta_{j}v^{i}_{+}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{j}(v^{i}_{0}-v^{i}_{-0}),\\
&\Delta_{j}(\mathbf{T}v^{i}_{+})|_{t=0}=-\Delta_{j}(c^{2}_{s}(0)\partial^{i}\rho_{0}+\frac{1}{\gamma}c^{2}_{s}(0)\partial^{i}h_{0})-\Delta_{j}(\mathbf{T}v^{i}_{-0}).\end{cases}

(7.16)

Above, we use the (
1.8
) such that

\begin{split}&\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}(\rho_{0}+\frac{1}{\gamma}h_{0})=-\Delta_{j}\mathrm{div}\mathbf{v}_{0},\quad v^{i}_{+0}=v^{i}_{0}-v^{i}_{-0},\\
&\mathbf{T}v^{i}_{+0}=-c^{2}_{s}(0)\partial^{i}\rho_{0}-\frac{1}{\gamma}c^{2}_{s}(0)\partial^{i}h_{0}-\mathbf{T}v^{i}_{-0}.\end{split}

By using the Strichartz estimate in Proposition
7.1
and Proposition
7.2
(taking
r=s
and
k<s-1
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}\Delta_{j}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h)\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}\Delta_{j}\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\Delta_{j}\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\Delta_{j}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\Delta_{j}h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\Delta_{j}\mathbf{v}_{-0}\|_{H^{s}}\\
&+\|\Delta_{j}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]\rho\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\Delta_{j}E\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\Delta_{j}\mathbf{Q}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\\
&+\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}](\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-})\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}.\end{split}

(7.17)

Taking square of (
7.17
) and summing it over
j\geq 1
, we get

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h),\left<\partial\right>^{k}\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim&\|\rho_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{-0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\\
&+\{\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]\rho\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}+\|E,\mathbf{Q}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\\
&+\{\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\\
&+\{\|[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}](\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-})\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}.\end{split}

(7.18)

By Lemma
2.17
, we can see

\begin{split}\{\|[\Delta_{j},\mathbf{T}](\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-})\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim&\|\partial\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H^{1}_{x}}\end{split}

(7.19)

By Lemma
2.19
, we also get

\begin{split}\{\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]\rho\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim&\|d\rho\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.20)

Using Lemma
2.19
again, it follows

\begin{split}\{\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\Delta_{j}]\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\}_{l^{2}_{j}}\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.21)

By (
2.80
), it yields

\begin{split}\|E,\mathbf{Q}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}\lesssim&\|d\rho\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{v},\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}_{x}}).\end{split}

(7.22)

Using (
2.81
) and
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{{H}}
(please refer (
5.3
)-(
5.5
)), we have

\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v},\rho,h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}_{x}}.

(7.23)

Using (
7.23
), we have

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq&\|d\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{v},\rho,h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.24)

Inserting (
7.19
)-(
7.24
) in (
7.18
), (
7.18
) yields

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h),\left<\partial\right>^{k}\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{0}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim&\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.25)

So we have proved (
7.12
).
∎

Remark 7.1
.

We expect that Proposition
7.3
yields a Strichartz estimate of solutions with sharp regularity of the velocity, density, entropy, and vorticity.

Proposition 7.4
.

Suppose
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Let
2<s_{0}<s<\frac{5}{2}
and
\delta_{*}\in[0,s-2)
. Let
\mathbf{v}_{+}
be defined in (
1.11
). Then the following Strichartz estimates and energy estimates hold

\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh,\partial\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}_{+},d\rho,d\mathbf{v},dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(7.26)

and the energy estimates

\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(7.27)

hold.

Proof.

If we take
k=s_{0}-1
in Proposition
7.3
, we then obtain

\begin{split}\|d(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h),d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{1}{2}+}},\end{split}

(7.28)

Using Proposition
7.3
again, for
\delta_{*}\in[0,s-2)
, we shall get

\begin{split}\|d(\rho+\frac{1}{\gamma}h),d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}},\end{split}

(7.29)

By Sobolev’s imbeddings, we have

\begin{split}\|\nabla h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+\delta+}}.\end{split}

(7.30)

Using (
7.30
) and (
1.8
), it follows

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\delta_{*}+}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}.\end{split}

(7.31)

Similarly, we also get

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+\delta_{*}+}}.\end{split}

(7.32)

Using (
7.32
) and (
7.23
), it follows

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{v},\rho,h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+\delta_{*}+}_{x}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+\delta_{*}+}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.33)

As a result, using (
7.29
)-(
7.33
), we get

\begin{split}\|d\rho,dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta_{*}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\delta_{*}+}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\delta_{*}+}}\\
\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\delta_{*}+(s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}-\delta_{*})}}\\
&\ +\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\delta_{*}+(s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}-\delta_{*})}}\\
\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1+s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(7.34)

Above, we use the fact that
\delta*<s-2\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}-\delta_{*}>s_{0}-2>0
.

Similarly, using (
7.28
) and Sobolev’s imbeddings, we have

\begin{split}\|d\rho,dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}}\\
\lesssim&\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1+s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(7.35)

By using Theorem
3.2
and
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
(
5.3
)-(
5.5
), it follows that

\begin{split}&\|\rho,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\\
\leq&C(\epsilon_{3}+\epsilon^{2}_{3})\exp\left\{C\|d\rho,dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\exp(C\|d\rho,dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\right\}\\
\leq&C\epsilon_{3}\mathrm{e}^{C\epsilon_{1}\mathrm{e}^{C\epsilon_{1}}}\\
\leq&\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(7.36)

Therefore, by (
7.36
) and (
7.35
), it turns out

\begin{split}\|d\rho,dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq&C\|\rho,\mathbf{v},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{s}}+C\|h\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+}}\\
&+C\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H^{\frac{1}{2}+}}\\
\leq&C\epsilon_{3}\mathrm{e}^{C\epsilon_{1}\mathrm{e}^{C\epsilon_{1}}}\\
\leq&\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(7.37)

Using (
7.36
) and (
7.34
) we prove that

\begin{split}\|d\rho,dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(7.38)

Gathering (
7.29
), (
7.36
), (
7.37
) and (
7.38
), we have finished this proof.
∎

8.
The proof of Proposition
7.1

In this part, we will give a proof of Proposition
7.1
by using Smith-Tataru’s idea in
[
47
]
. The method relies on a wave-packet construction for the nontruncated metric
\mathbf{g}
. We mention that the use of wave-packets is introduced by Smith
[
45
]
, and developed by Wolff
[
64
]
and Smith-Tataru
[
47
]
. Following this idea, so we can represent approximate solutions to the linear equation as a square summable superposition of wave packets, and the wave packets are localized in phase space. As a result, we can obtain Strichartz estimates of linear wave equations with sufficient regularity of the foliations. Therefore, it’s quite different with Wang’s paper
[
63
]
in establishing Strichartz estimates, where Wang
[
63
]
use the vector field approach and work with foliations on null cones.

In this part, we set
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
, where
\mathcal{{H}}
and
G
are defined in (
5.3
)-(
5.5
) and (
6.2
) respectively. Then
\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}:=\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{0}
and
\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}:=\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{0}
, where
\epsilon_{0}
is stated in (
1.29
).

We divide the proof of Proposition
7.1
into several subparts. The first step is to reduce the problem in a phase space.

8.1.
A reduction to the phase decomposition

Given a frequency scale
\lambda\geq 1
, we consider the smooth coefficients

\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}=S_{<\lambda}\mathbf{g}.

We can state the following result:

Proposition 8.1
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho,h)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Suppose
f
satisfy

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0,\quad(t,\mathbf{x})\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
f|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1}.\end{cases}

(8.1)

Then for each
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
there exists a function
f_{\lambda}\in C^{\infty}([-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
, with

\mathrm{supp}\widehat{f_{\lambda}(t,\cdot)}\subseteq\{\xi:\frac{\lambda}{8}\leq|\xi|\leq 8\lambda\},

such that

\begin{cases}&\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}),\\
&f_{\lambda}(t_{0})=P_{\lambda}f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f_{\lambda}(t_{0})=P_{\lambda}f_{1}.\end{cases}

(8.2)

Additionally, if
r>1
, then the following Strichartz estimates holds:

\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{r-1}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

(8.3)

8.2.
The proof of Proposition
8.1

To prove Proposition
8.1
, we construct an approximate solutions to (
8.1
) by using wave packets, and a wave packet has a finer spatial localization than a plane wave solutions. We divide the proof into two parts, namely (
8.2
) and (
8.3
). The conclusion (
8.2
) is proved in Proposition
8.4
and Proposition
8.5
, and the conclusion (
8.3
) is obtained by Proposition (
8.7
). Let us introduce the wave packets which is related to
\mathbf{g}
and the null hypersurfaces
\Sigma
.

Definition 8.1

(
[
47
]
)
.

Let the hypersurface
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
and the geodesic
\gamma
be defined in Section
6
. A normalized wave packet around
\gamma
is a function
f
of the form

f=\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-1}T_{\lambda}(u\zeta),

where

u(t,x)=\delta(x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})),\quad\zeta=\zeta_{0}((\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}(x^{\prime}_{\omega}-\gamma_{\omega}(t))).

Here,
\zeta_{0}
is a smooth function supported in the set
|x^{\prime}|\leq 1
, with uniform bounds on its derivatives
|\partial^{\alpha}_{x^{\prime}}\zeta_{0}(x^{\prime})|\leq c_{\alpha}
.

We give two notations here. We denote
L(u,\zeta)
to denote a translation invariant bilinear operator of the form

L(u,\zeta)(x)=\int K(y,z)u(x+y)\zeta(x+z)dydz,

where
K(y,z)
is a finite measure. If
X
is a Sobolev spaces, we then denote
X_{a}
the same space but with the norm obtained by dimensionless rescaling by
a
,

\|u\|_{X_{a}}=\|u(a\cdot)\|_{X}.

Since
2(s_{0}-1)>2
, then for
a<1
we have
\|u\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
. In the following, let us introduce what we can get when we take the operator
\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}
on wave packets.

8.2.1.
A normalized wave packet

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
f
be a normalized packet. Then there is another normalized wave packet
\tilde{f}
, and functions
\phi_{m}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega}),m=0,1,2
, so that

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f=L(d\mathbf{g},d\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{f})+\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-1}P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\sum_{m=0,1,2}\psi_{m}\delta^{(m)}(x^{\prime}_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,r}),

(8.4)

where the functions
\psi_{m}=\psi_{m}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})
satisfy the scaled Sobolev estimates

\|\psi_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{a,x^{\prime}_{\omega}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\lambda^{1-m},\quad m=0,1,2,\quad a=(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(8.5)

Proof.

For brevity, we consider the case
\omega=(0,0,1)
. Then
x_{\omega}=x_{3}
, and
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}=\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
. We write

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f=\lambda^{-1}([\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]+P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}})(uh).

(8.6)

For the first term in (
8.6
), noting
\mathbf{g}\lambda
supported at frequency
\leq\frac{\lambda}{8}
, then we can write

[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]=[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{T}_{\lambda}

for some multipliers
\tilde{P}_{\lambda},\tilde{T}_{\lambda}
which have the same properties as
P_{\lambda},T_{\lambda}
. Therefore, by using the kernal bounds for
P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}
, we conclude that

[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]f=L(d\mathbf{g},df).

For the second term in (
8.6
), we use the Leibniz rule

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(u\zeta)=\zeta\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}u+(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{\alpha}u\partial_{\beta}\zeta+u\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\zeta.

(8.7)

We let
\nu
denote the conormal vector field along
\Sigma
,
\nu=dx_{3}-d\phi(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
. In the following, we take the greek indices
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 2
.

For the first term in (
8.7
), by calculation, we have

\begin{split}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}u=&\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(2)}_{x_{3}-\phi}-2(\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(1)}_{x_{3}-\phi}\\
&+(\partial^{2}_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(0)}_{x_{3}-\phi}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\delta^{(1)}_{x_{3}-\phi}\\
&+\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\delta^{(0)}_{x_{3}-\phi}.\end{split}

Here,
\delta^{(m)}_{x_{3}-\phi}=(\partial^{m}\delta)(x_{3}-\phi)
. By Leibniz rule, we can take

\begin{split}\psi_{0}&=\zeta\big\{(\partial^{2}_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}+(\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\big\},\\
\psi_{1}&=\zeta\big\{2(\partial_{3}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\phi\big\},\\
\psi_{2}&=\zeta(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta},\end{split}

By using (
6.4
), Proposition
6.1
, and Corollary
6.6
, we can conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0},\psi_{1},
and
\psi_{2}
satisfy the estimates (
8.5
). For the second term in (
8.6
), we have

\begin{split}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{\alpha}u\partial_{\beta}\zeta=&\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}\zeta\delta^{(1)}_{x_{3}-\phi}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}\partial_{3}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}\zeta\delta^{(0)}_{x_{3}-\phi}.\end{split}

We then take

\psi_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}\partial_{3}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}\zeta,\quad\psi_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}\zeta.

By using (
6.4
), Proposition
6.1
, and Corollary
6.6
, we can conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
satisfy the estimates (
8.5
).
For the third term in (
8.6
), we can take

\psi_{0}=\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\alpha\beta}\zeta.

By using (
6.4
), Proposition
6.1
, and Corollary
6.6
, we can conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0}
satisfies the estimates (
8.5
).
∎

Therefore, a single wave packet is not sufficient for us to constructe approximate solutions to a linear wave equation, so we need to consider the superposition of wave packets.

8.2.2.
Superpositions of wave packets

Firstly, let us introduce some notations. The index
\omega
stands for the initial orientation of the wave packet at
t=-2
, which varies over a maximal collection of approximately
\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\lambda
unit vectors separated by at least
\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. For each
\omega
, we have the orthonormal coordinate system
(x_{\omega},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega})
of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where
x_{\omega}=x\cdot\omega
, and
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}
are projective along
\omega
.

We denote
\mathbb{R}^{3}
by a parallel tiling of rectangles, with length
(8\lambda)^{-1}
in the
x_{\omega}
direction, and
(4\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in the other directions
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}
. The index
j
corresponds to a counting of the rectangles in this decomposition. Let
R_{\omega,j}
denote the collection of the doubles of these rectangles, and
\Sigma_{\omega,j}
denote the element of the
\Sigma_{\omega}
(
\Sigma_{\omega}=\cup_{r}\Sigma_{\omega,r}
) foliation upon which
R_{\omega,j}
is centered. Let
\gamma_{\omega,j}
denote the null geodesic contained in
\Sigma_{\omega,j}
which passes through the center of
R_{\omega,j}
at time
t=-2
.

We let
T_{\omega,j}
be the set

T_{\omega,j}=\Sigma_{\omega,j}\cap\{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}-\gamma_{\omega,j}|\leq(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}.

(8.8)

By (
6.94
) and (
6.101
), then the estimate

|dr_{\theta}-(\theta\cdot d\mathbf{x}-dt)|\lesssim\epsilon_{1},

holds pointwise uniformly on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. This also implies that

|\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\theta})-\phi_{\theta,r^{\prime}}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\theta})-(r-r^{\prime})|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}|r-r^{\prime}|.

(8.9)

On the other hand, if we set
\bar{\rho}(t)=\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}
, then (
6.5
) tells us

\|d^{2}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega})-d^{2}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r^{\prime}}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\bar{\rho}(t).

(8.10)

By using (
8.9
) and (
8.10
), we get

\|d_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega})-d_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r^{\prime}}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}}}\lesssim(\epsilon_{2}+\bar{\rho}(t))^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-r^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(8.11)

For
dx_{\omega}-d\phi_{\omega,r}
is null and also
d\mathbf{g}\leq\bar{\rho}(t)
, this also implies Hölder-
\frac{1}{2}
bounds on
d\phi_{\omega,r}
. So we suppose that
(t,x)\in\Sigma_{\omega,r}
and
(t,y)\in\Sigma_{\omega,r^{\prime}}
, that
|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{\omega}|\leq 2(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and that
|r-r^{\prime}|\leq 2\lambda^{-1}
. Using (
6.95
), we can obtain

|l_{\omega}(t,x)-l_{\omega}(t,y)|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\rho}(t)\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Due to
\dot{\gamma}_{\omega}=l_{\omega}
, and
\|\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}
, so any geodesic in
\Sigma_{\omega}
which intersects a slab
T_{\omega,j}
should be contained in the similar slab of half the scale.

We are ready to introduce a lemma about a superpositions of wave packets from a certain fixed time.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
0<\mu<\delta
. Let a scalar function
\bar{v}(t,\mathbf{x})
be formulated by

\bar{v}(t,\mathbf{x})=\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{\lambda}\sum_{\omega,j}T_{\lambda}(\psi^{\omega,j}\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j}(t,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{\omega})}).

(8.12)

Set
\bar{\rho}(t)=\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}
and
a=(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Then we have

\|\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{\omega,j}\|\psi^{\omega,j}\|^{2}_{H^{1+\mu}_{a}},\qquad\qquad\ \ \text{if}\ \ \bar{\rho}(t)\leq\epsilon_{0},

(8.13)

and

\|\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\bar{\rho}(t)\sum_{\omega,j}\|\psi^{\omega,j}\|^{2}_{H^{1+\mu}_{a}},\qquad\text{if}\ \ \bar{\rho}(t)\geq\epsilon_{0}.

(8.14)

Proof.

This proof follows ideas of Smith-Tataru
[
47
]
(Lemma 8.5 and Lemma 8.6 on page 340-345). Therefore we omit the details here.
∎

Remark 8.1
.

By (
8.13
) and (
8.14
), we get

\|\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\left(1+\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\bar{\rho}(t)\right)\sum_{\omega,j}\|\psi^{\omega,j}\|^{2}_{H^{1+\mu}_{a}}.

(8.15)

Proposition 8.4

(
[
47
]
)
.

Let
f=\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}f^{\omega,j}
, where
f^{\omega,j}
are normalized wave packets supported in
T_{\omega,j}
. Then we have

\|dP_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(8.16)

and

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(8.17)

Proof.

We first prove a weaker estimate comparing with (
8.16
)

\|dP_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(8.18)

By using (
8.15
) and replacing
P_{\lambda}
by
\lambda^{-1}\partial P_{\lambda}
, and
\psi^{\omega,j}=a_{\omega,j}\zeta^{\omega,j}
, we have

\|\partial P_{\lambda}f(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(1+\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\bar{\rho}(t))\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}.

Due to the fact
\|\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}
, we can see that

\|\partial P_{\lambda}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}.

(8.19)

We also need to get the similar estimate for the time derivatives. We can calculate

\partial_{t}\zeta=\dot{\gamma}(t)(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\zeta},\quad\partial_{t}\delta(x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j})=\partial_{t}\phi_{\omega,j}\delta^{(1)}(x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j}).

For
\dot{\gamma}\in L^{\infty}_{t}
and
\partial_{t}\phi_{\omega,j}\in L^{\infty}_{t}
, then we have

\|\partial_{t}P_{\lambda}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}.

(8.20)

Combining (
8.19
) and (
8.20
), we have proved (
8.18
). To prove (
8.17
), we use the formula (
8.4
). Considering the right hand of (
8.4
), by using (
8.18
), we can bound the first term by

\|L(d\mathbf{g},d\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(8.21)

It only remains for us to estimate the second right term on (
8.4
). If we set

\Phi=\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\left(\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}\cdot\sum_{m=0,1,2}\psi^{\omega,j}_{m}\delta^{(m)}_{x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j}}\right),

and use (
8.5
), then we have

\begin{split}\|\Phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}=&(1+\bar{\rho}(t)\epsilon_{0}^{-1})\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}\sum_{m=0,1,2}\lambda^{m-1}\|\psi^{\omega,j}_{m}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1+\mu}_{a}}\\
\lesssim&(1+\bar{\rho}(t)\epsilon_{0}^{-1})\epsilon_{0}^{2}\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j},\end{split}

we therefore get

\begin{split}\|\Phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{2}_{-2}[1+\bar{\rho}(t)\epsilon_{0}^{-1}]^{\frac{1}{2}}dt\right)\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int^{2}_{-2}[1+\bar{\rho}(t)\epsilon_{0}^{-1}]dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

Hence, the second right term on (
8.4
) can be estimated by
\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. So we have proved (
8.17
). Using (
8.18
) and (
8.17
), and classical energy estimates for linear wave equation, we obtain (
8.16
).
∎

8.2.3.
Matching the initial data

Although we have constructed the approximate solutions using superpositions of normalized wave packets, we also need to complete this construction, i.e. matching the initial data for this type of solutions. Since the metric
4
4

4

Please see (
5.1
).

\mathbf{g}
equals to the Minkowski metric for
t\in[-2,-\frac{3}{2}]
, so it’s natural for us to work with wave packets near
t=-2
for the Minkowski wave operator. We refer to the construction from
[
47
, Proposition 8.7, page 346]

or Smith
[
45
, page 815, Section 4]
.

Proposition 8.5

(
[
47
]
)
.

Given any initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
, there exists a function of the form

f=\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}f^{\omega,j},

where the function
f^{\omega,j}
are normalized wave packets, such that

P_{\lambda}f(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}P_{\lambda}f(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{1}.

Furthermore,

\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}^{2}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Remark 8.2
.

Considering the definition of a wave packet, together with the regularity of the
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
, then we can use the quantities
\phi_{\omega,r}
and
\gamma_{\omega,r}
to keep the type of
f
for
t\in[-2,2]
.

If
t_{0}=-2
in Proposition (
8.1
), then the function
f
is the approximate solution matching the initial data by using Proposition
8.5
.

If
t_{0}\in(-2,2)
, then we can use an iteration process as follows. Basically, we hope to find the exact solution
\Phi
to the Cauchy problem

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\Phi=0,\qquad(t,\mathbf{x})\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&(\Phi,\partial_{t}\Phi)|_{t=t_{0}}=(\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1}).\end{cases}

Let
\Phi_{\lambda}=P_{\lambda}\Phi
be the approximate solution with the initial data
(\Phi_{\lambda},\partial_{t}\Phi_{\lambda})|_{t=-2}=({P}_{\lambda}\Phi(-2),{P}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}\Phi(-2))
. Then we have
