\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\Phi_{\lambda}=[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}]\Phi.

The energy estimates tell us that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\Phi_{\lambda}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\Phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|\Phi_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\Phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

However, it does not match the data at
t=t_{0}
. Fortunately, we can obtain

\begin{split}&P_{\lambda}\Phi_{0}-\Phi_{\lambda}(t_{0})=P_{\lambda}\Phi_{0}^{1},\qquad\Phi_{0}^{1}=P_{\lambda}\Phi_{0}-\Phi_{\lambda}(t_{0}),\\
&P_{\lambda}\Phi_{1}-\partial_{t}\Phi_{\lambda}(t_{0})=P_{\lambda}\Phi_{1}^{1},\ \quad\Phi_{1}^{1}=P_{\lambda}\Phi_{1}-\partial_{t}\Phi_{\lambda}(t_{0}).\end{split}

By energy estimates and commutator estimates, we have

\begin{split}\|\Phi_{0}^{1}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\Phi_{1}^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim&\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(P_{\lambda}\Phi-\Phi)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\Phi_{\lambda}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(\Phi_{\lambda}]\Phi)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}(\|\Phi_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\Phi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).\end{split}

Since the norm of the error is much smaller than the initial size of the data,
we can repeat this process with data
(\Phi_{0}^{1},\Phi_{1}^{1})
, and sum this series to
obtain a smooth function
\Phi_{\lambda}
with data
(P_{\lambda}\Phi_{0},P_{\lambda}\Phi_{1})
at time
t=t_{0}
. As a result, it can also match the given data at time
t=t_{0}
.

8.2.4.
Overlap estimates

Since the foliations
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
varing with
\omega
and
r
, so a fixed
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
may intersect with other
\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime},r^{\prime}}
. As a result, we should be clear about the number of
\lambda
-slabs which contain two given points in the space-time
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Corollary 8.6

(
[
47
]
)
.

For all points
P_{1}=(t_{1},\mathbf{x}_{1})
and
P_{2}=(t_{2},\mathbf{x}_{2})
in space-time
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and
\epsilon_{0}\lambda\geq 1
, the number
N_{\lambda}(P_{1},P_{2})
of slabs of scale
\lambda
that contain both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
satisfies the bound

\begin{split}N_{\lambda}(P_{1},P_{2})\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}^{-1}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{-1}.\end{split}

8.2.5.
The proof of Estimate (
8.3
)

After the construction of approximate solutions, we still need to prove the key estimate (
8.3
). This can be directly derived by the following result. Before that, let us define
\mathcal{T}=\cup_{\omega,j}T_{\omega,j}
, where
T_{\omega,j}
is set in (
8.8
). We also denote
\chi_{{J}}
be a smooth cut-off function, and
\chi_{J}=1
on a set
J
.

Proposition 8.7
.

Let
t\in[-2,2]
and

f={\sum}_{J\in\mathcal{T}}a_{J}\chi_{J}f_{J},

where
\sum_{J\in\mathcal{T}}a_{J}^{2}\leq 1
and
f_{J}
are normalized wave packets in
J
. Then
5
5

5

We need
r>1
in (
8.3
) for there is a factor
(\ln\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}
in (
8.22
).

\|f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(8.22)

Proof.

This proof also follows Smith-Tataru
[
47
]
(Proposition 10.1 on page 353). Here we use slightly different factors in (
8.22
).

Let us first make a partition of the time-interval
[-2,2]
. By decomposition, there exists a partition
\left\{I_{j}\right\}
of the interval
[-2,2]
into disjoint subintervals
I_{j}
such that with the size of each
I_{j}
,
|I_{j}|\simeq\lambda^{-1}
, and the number of subintervals
j\simeq\lambda
. By mean value theorem, there exists a number
t_{j}
such that

\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\textstyle{\sum}_{j}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{I_{j}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\textstyle{\sum}_{j}\|f(t_{j},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1},

(8.23)

where
t_{j}
is located in
I_{j}
, and
|t_{j+1}-t_{j}|\simeq\lambda^{-1}
. Let us explain the (
8.23
) as follows. For simplicity, let
I_{j_{0}}=[0,\lambda^{-1}]
and
I_{j_{0}+1}=[\lambda^{-1},2\lambda^{-1}]
. By mean value theorem, on
I_{j_{0}}
, we have

\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{I_{j_{0}}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1}.

We also have

\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{I_{j_{0}+1}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1}.

If
t_{j_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1}
or
t_{j_{0}+1}\geq\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}
, then
|t_{j_{0}+1}-t_{j_{0}}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1}
. Otherwise,
t_{j_{0}}\in[\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1},\lambda^{-1}]
and
t_{j_{0}+1}\in[\lambda^{-1},\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}]
. In this case,
we combine
I_{j_{0}},I_{j_{0}+1}
together and set
I^{*}_{j_{0}}=I_{j_{0}}\cup I_{j_{0}+1}
. On the new interval
I^{*}_{j_{0}}
, we can see that

\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{I^{*}_{j_{0}}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=2\|f(t^{*}_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1},

and

2\|f(t^{*}_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.

Since
f
is a contituous function, we have

\|f(t^{*}_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}=\left\{\frac{1}{2}\big(\|f(t_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad t^{*}_{j_{0}}\in[\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1},\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}].

In case
t^{*}_{j_{0}}\in[\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1},\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}]
, we set
t_{j_{0}}:=t^{*}_{j_{0}}
. On the next time-interval
I_{j_{0}+2}=[2\lambda^{-1},3\lambda^{-1}]
, we obtain

\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{I_{j_{0}+2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1},\quad t_{j_{0}+1}\in[2\lambda^{-1},3\lambda^{-1}].

Hence, we have proved
|t_{j_{0}+1}-t_{j_{0}}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1}
. So we can use this way to decompose
[-2,2]
. Therefore, to prove (
8.22
), and taking (
8.23
) into account, we only need to show that

\textstyle{\sum}_{j}|f(t_{j},x_{j})|^{2}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\lambda\ln\lambda,

(8.24)

where
x_{j}
is arbitrarily chosen. We then set the points
P_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})
.

Since each points lies in at most
\approx\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}
slabs, so we may assume that
|a_{J}|\geq\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Then we decompose the sum
f=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{T}}a_{J}\chi_{J}f_{J}
dyadically with respect to the size of
a_{J}
. We next decompose the sum over
j
via a dyadic decomposition in the numbers of slabs containing
(t_{j},\mathbf{x}_{j})
. We may assume that we are summing over
M
points
6
6

6

We also remark that
M
is
\simeq\lambda
, for
t_{j}\in[-2,2]
and
|t_{j+1}-t_{j}|\simeq\lambda^{-1}
.

(t_{j},\mathbf{x}_{j})
, each of which is contained in approximately
L
slabs
7
7

7

So
L\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\lambda
by using Corollary
8.6
.
. Then
|f(t_{j},\mathbf{x}_{j})|\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}L
and

\textstyle{\sum}_{j}|f(t_{j},\mathbf{x}_{j})|^{2}\lesssim L^{2}MN^{-1}.

(8.25)

Combining (
8.24
) with (
8.25
), we only need to prove

L^{2}MN^{-1}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\lambda\ln\lambda.

(8.26)

This is a counting problem, and we shall prove (
8.26
) by calculating in two different ways. First consider the number
K
of pairs
(i,j)
for which
P_{i}
and
P_{j}
are contained in a common slab, counted with multiplicity. For
J\in\mathcal{T}
, we denote by
n_{J}
the number of points
P_{j}
contained
8
8

8

For
J
, if it contains
3
points
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
, then we will count it
(P_{1},P_{2}),(P_{2},P_{1}),(P_{1},P_{3}),(P_{3},P_{1}),(P_{2},P_{3}),(P_{3},P_{2})
in
K
. Therefore it’s
3^{2}
.
in
J
. Then

K=\sum_{n_{J}\geq 2}n_{J}^{2}.

By Jensen’s inequality, we have

K=\sum_{n_{J}\geq 2}n_{J}^{2}\gtrsim N^{-1}(\sum_{n_{J}\geq 2}n_{J})^{2}.

Note that
\sum_{J\in\mathcal{T}}n_{J}\approx ML
. We consider it into two cases. If

\sum_{n_{J}\geq 2}n_{J}\leq\sum_{n_{J}=1}n_{J},

then
N\approx ML
. In this case, combining with the fact that
L\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\lambda
, then (
8.26
) holds. Otherwise, we have

K\gtrsim N^{-1}M^{2}L^{2}.

(8.27)

In this case, by using Corollary
8.6
, we obtain

K\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq M,i\neq j}|t_{i}-t_{j}|^{-1}.

(8.28)

The sum is maximized in the case that
t_{j}
are close as possible, i.e. if the
t_{j}
are consecutive multiples of
\lambda^{-1}
. Therefore, (
8.28
) yields

K\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\lambda\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq M,i\neq j}|i-j|^{-1}\lesssim M\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\lambda\ln\lambda,

(8.29)

where we use the fact that
M\simeq\lambda
. Combining (
8.29
) and (
8.27
), we get (
8.26
). So we have finished the proof of Proposition
8.7
.
∎

8.3.
The proof of Proposition
7.1

In this part, we will use Proposition
8.1
to prove Proposition
7.1
. We first prove the case of
r=1
and
r=2
. Considering the fractional derivatives on spatial and using Kato-Ponce commutator estimates and Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, we divide the proof into several cases according to Proposition
8.1
.

Step 1:
The case
r=1
. Using basic energy estimates for (
1.35
), we have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\ (\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}})\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\\
\lesssim&\ \|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Then the Cauchy problem (
1.35
) holds a unique solution
f\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{1})
and
\partial_{t}f\in C([-2,2],L_{x}^{2})
. It remains to show that the solution
f
also satisfies the Strichartz estimate (
7.1
).

Without loss of generality, we take
t_{0}=0
in Proposition
7.1
. For any given initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
, and
t_{0}\in[-2,2]
, we take a Littlewood-Paley decomposition

f_{0}=\textstyle{\sum}_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}f_{0},\qquad f_{1}=\textstyle{\sum}_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}f_{1},

and for each
\lambda
we take the corresponding
f_{\lambda}
as in Proposition
8.1
. If we set

f^{*}=\textstyle{\sum}_{\lambda}f_{\lambda},

(8.30)

then
f^{*}
matches the initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})
at the time
t=t_{0}
, and also satisfies the Strichartz estimates (
8.3
). We claim that
f^{*}
is also an approximate solution for
\square_{\mathbf{g}}
in the sense that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{*}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

(8.31)

We can derive the above bound by using the decomposition

\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{*}=\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}+\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}.

The first one
\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}
can be controlled by Proposition
8.1
. As for the second one
\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}
, noting
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, we then have

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}=\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}({\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}})\partial df_{\lambda}.\end{split}

It follows from Hölder inequality that

\begin{split}\|\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\left(\lambda\|{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\left(\sum_{\lambda}\|df_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(8.32)

On the other hand, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\left(\lambda\|{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\lesssim&\ \mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\big(\lambda\sum_{\mu>\lambda}\|\mathbf{g}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\\
\lesssim&\ \mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\big(\lambda\sum_{\mu>\lambda}\mu^{-(1+\delta)}\|d\mathbf{g}_{\mu}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}\big)\\
\lesssim&\ \|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}(\textstyle{\sum_{\mu}}\mu^{-\delta})\lesssim\ \|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}.\end{split}

(8.33)

Gathering (
8.32
) and (
8.33
), we can prove that

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}),\end{split}

where we use the fact that
\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}
. Hence, we have proved (
8.31
). But
f^{*}
is not the exact solution to
\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0
with the Cauchy data
(f_{0},f_{1})
. So we seek another function
\mathbf{M}F
satisfying

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{M}F=-\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{*},\\
&(\mathbf{M}F,\partial_{t}\mathbf{M}F)=(0,0).\end{cases}

(8.34)

For given
F=-\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{*}\in L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
, we now form the function

F^{(1)}=\int^{t}_{0}f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)d\tau,

where
f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)
is the approximate solution of
\square_{\mathbf{g}}\tilde{f}=0
and
f^{(1)}
is formed like
f^{*}
(cf. (
8.30
)) with the Cauchy data

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{(1)}=0,\qquad(t,\mathbf{x})\in(\tau,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f^{(1)}(\tau,t,x)|_{t=\tau}=0,\quad\partial_{t}f^{(1)}(\tau,t,x)|_{t=\tau}=F(\tau,\cdot).\end{cases}

By calculating

\begin{split}\partial_{t}F^{(1)}=&\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{t}f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)d\tau+f^{(1)}(t;t,x)=\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{t}f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)d\tau,\\
\partial_{tt}F^{(1)}=&\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{tt}f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)d\tau+\partial_{t}f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)=\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{tt}f^{(1)}(t;t,x)d\tau+F(t,x),\\
\partial_{ti}F^{(1)}=&\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{ti}f^{(1)}(t;t,x)d\tau,\quad i=1,2,3,\\
\partial_{ij}F^{(1)}=&\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{ij}f^{(1)}(t;t,x)d\tau,\quad i,j=1,2,3,\end{split}

we then have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}F^{(1)}=\int^{t}_{0}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)d\tau+F.

By using (
8.31
), we derive that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}F^{(1)}-F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{(1)}(\tau;t,x)\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

For
F^{m},m\geq 2
, we perform an iteration as follows. Let

F^{(2)}=\int^{t}_{0}f^{(2)}(\tau;t,x)d\tau,

where
f^{(2)}(\tau;t,x)
is the approximate solution of
\square_{\mathbf{g}}\tilde{f}=0
and
f^{(2)}
is formed like
f^{*}
(cf. (
8.30
)) with Cauchy data

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{(2)}=0,\qquad(t,\mathbf{x})\in(\tau,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f^{(2)}(\tau;t,x)|_{t=\tau}=0,\quad\partial_{t}f^{(2)}(\tau;t,x)|_{t=\tau}=F-\square_{\mathbf{g}}F^{(1)}.\end{cases}

Then we can prove that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}F^{(2)}-(F-\square_{\mathbf{g}}F^{(1)})\|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\|F-\square_{\mathbf{g}}F^{(1)}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{0}\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

We rewrite the above equality as

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}(F^{(2)}+F^{(1)})-F\|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\|F-\square_{\mathbf{g}}F^{(1)}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{0}\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

For
m\geq 2
, we can set

F^{(m)}=\int^{t}_{0}f^{(m)}(\tau;t,x)d\tau,

where
f^{(m)}(\tau;t,x)
is the approximate solution of
\square_{\mathbf{g}}\tilde{f}=0
and
f^{(m)}
is formed like
f^{*}
(cf. (
8.30
)) with Cauchy data

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{(m)}=0,\qquad(t,\mathbf{x})\in(\tau,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&f^{(m)}(\tau;t,x)|_{t=\tau}=0,\quad\partial_{t}f^{(2)}(\tau;t,x)|_{t=\tau}=F-\square_{\mathbf{g}}(F^{(1)}+F^{(2)}+\cdots F^{(m-1)}).\end{cases}

we therefore obtain that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}(F^{(m)}+F^{(m-1)}+\cdots F^{(1)})-F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon^{m}_{0}\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

(8.35)

By using the contraction principle, we can see that there is a limit
\mathbf{M}F=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}(F^{(m)}+F^{(m-1)}+\cdots F^{(1)})
. Hence, we get

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{M}F=-\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{*},\quad(\mathbf{M}F,\partial_{t}\mathbf{M}F)=(0,0).

(8.36)

Gathering (
8.34
) and (
8.36
), we can write the solution
f
in the form

f=f^{*}+\mathbf{M}F,

where
f^{*}
is the approximation solution formed in (
8.30
) for initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})
specified at time
t=0
, and it satisfies the Strichartz estimates (
8.3
), and

\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

The Strichartz estimates of
\mathbf{M}F
now follow since they holds for each
f^{(m)}(\tau;t,x)
,
\tau\in[0,t]
. Therefore, the estimate (
7.1
) holds.

Step 2:
The case
r=2
. We also consider
t_{0}=0
. Given data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{2}\times H^{1}
, we find a solution of the form
f=\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\bar{f}
. Then we have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}=(\square_{\mathbf{g}}-\left<\partial\right>\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{-1})\bar{f}=[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>]\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\partial_{\alpha\beta}\bar{f}.

Hence,
\bar{f}
solves

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}=[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>]\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\partial_{\alpha\beta}\bar{f},\quad(t,\mathbf{x})\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
\bar{f}|_{t=0}=\left<\partial\right>f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}\bar{f}|_{t=0}=\left<\partial\right>f_{1}.\end{cases}

For
\bar{F}=[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>]\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\partial_{\alpha\beta}\bar{f}\in L_{t}^{1}L^{2}_{x}
, we form
\mathbf{M}\bar{F}
as above in Step 1. So we have
\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{M}\bar{F}=\bar{F}
, and
\mathbf{M}\bar{F}
has vanishing Cauchy data at
t_{0}=0
. Let
\bar{f}^{*}
be the solution for the homogeneous equation

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}^{*}=0,\quad(t,\mathbf{x})\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
\bar{f}^{*}|_{t=0}=\left<\partial\right>f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}\bar{f}^{*}|_{t=0}=\left<\partial\right>f_{1}.\end{cases}

Then we can seek a solution
\bar{f}
of the form
\bar{f}=\bar{f}^{*}+\mathbf{M}\bar{F}
, provided that

\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>]\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\partial_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{M}\bar{F}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\|\bar{F}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

(8.37)

Let us prove (
8.37
). Considering
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, by using Coifman-Maeyer estimate, we derive that

\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\partial\right>]\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\partial_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{M}\bar{F}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{M}\bar{F}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

(8.38)

By using (
8.36
), we also obtain

\|d\mathbf{M}\bar{F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\bar{F}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

(8.39)

Combining (
8.38
) with (
8.39
), and using
\|\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}
, we can get (
8.37
).

Therefore, by Duhamel’s principle, in the case of
r=1
or
r=2
, we can also obtain the Strichartz estimates for the linear, nonhomogeneous wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=G,\quad(t,\mathbf{x})\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
f|_{t=0}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f|_{t=0}=f_{1},\end{cases}

as

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}},\quad k<r-1.

(8.40)

Now, we claim that the following bounds
9
9

9

Comparing with (
8.40
), the bound (
8.41
) is stronger, for it includes the time-derivatives of
f
. It plays an important role in the next step.
hold for
r=1
and
r=2
,

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}df\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|G\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}},\quad k<r-2.

(8.41)

Let us now use (
8.40
) to prove (
8.41
).

To prove (
8.41
) for
r=2
, we first consider
G=0
. That is,

\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0,(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=0}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{2}\times H^{1}
. For
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, we then have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}df=d\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}f\in L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}.

(8.42)

The right hand side of (
8.42
) can be derived by energy estimates and Gronwall’s inequality. That is

\|\partial_{t}df\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial df\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{2}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{1}})\exp\{\int^{2}_{0}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dt\}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{2}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{1}}.

Then
d\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}f\in L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
holds. Thus, (
8.41
) holds for
r=2
and
G=0
. If
G\neq 0
, we can use the Duhamel principle to handle it. Decompose
f=f^{a}+f^{b}
, where

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{a}=0,\\
&(f^{a},\partial_{t}f^{a})|_{t=0}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{2}\times H^{1},\end{cases}

(8.43)

and

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{b}=G,\\
&(f^{a},\partial_{t}f^{b})|_{t=0}=(0,0).\end{cases}

(8.44)

So we can get the Strichartz estimates for
f^{a}

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}df^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}},\quad k<r-2.

For
f^{b}
, we use the iteration process like
\mathbf{M}F
in Step 1, and the following Strichartz estimates for
f^{b}

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}df^{b}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|G\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}},\quad k<r-2.

So the conclusion (
8.41
) holds for
f=f^{a}+f^{b}
.

To prove (
8.41
) for
r=1
, we note that,
\left<\partial\right>^{-1}f
has the Cauchy data in
H^{2}\times H^{1}
if the Cauchy data of
f
is in
H^{1}\times L^{2}
, and

\begin{split}\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{-1}f\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}=&\|\left<\partial\right>\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{-1}f\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|[\left<\partial\right>,\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}]\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\partial_{\alpha\beta}f\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|G\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Noting
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, then we give the following bounds by Coifman-Meyer estimate and energy estimates for
f

\begin{split}\|[\left<\partial\right>,\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}]\left<\partial\right>^{-1}\partial_{\alpha\beta}f\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).\end{split}

Step 3:
The case
1<r<2
. We also set
t_{0}=0
. Based on the above result in Step 1 and Step 2, we also transform the initial data in
H^{1}\times L^{2}
. Applying
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}
to (
8.1
), we have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f.

Let
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
. Then
\bar{f}
is a solution to

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f},\\
(\bar{f},\partial_{t}\bar{f})|_{t=0}\in H^{1}\times L^{2}.\end{cases}

(8.45)

Considering
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, let us calculate

\begin{split}-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}=&[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}\partial_{i}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\\
&\ +\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\partial_{i}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}).\end{split}

(8.46)

For
r-1\in(0,1)
, by product estimates and a refined Kato-Ponce inequalities (please cite Theorem 1.9 in
2.14
), we have

\begin{split}&\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}\partial_{i}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\partial_{i}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r}(\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{p_{1}}_{x}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}},\end{split}

(8.47)

where
p_{1}=\frac{3}{\frac{3}{2}-s+r}
and
q_{1}=\frac{3}{s-r}
. By Sobolev’s inequality, we get

\|\left<\partial\right>^{r}(\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{p_{1}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}.

(8.48)

By Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, we can see that
10
10

10

The number
s
and
s_{0}
satisfying
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
.

\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\bar{f}\|^{\frac{s-s_{0}}{\frac{5}{2}-s_{0}}}_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-s_{0}}d\bar{f}\|^{1-\frac{s-s_{0}}{\frac{5}{2}-s_{0}}}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

(8.49)

Considering
11
11

11

please see the definition of
\mathcal{H}
and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
)

\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

and combining with (
8.46
)-(
8.49
), we have

\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-s_{0}}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).

(8.50)

Using the discussion in case
r=1
, namely (
8.41
), we then get the Strichartz estimates for
\bar{f}
for
\theta<-1
,

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-s_{0}}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.51)

Note
1-s_{0}<-1
. So we take
\theta=1-s_{0}
, and then we obtain

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-s_{0}}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.52)

Using (
8.52
), (
8.51
) yields

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}),\quad\theta<-1.\end{split}

(8.53)

By (
8.52
), (
8.50
) and the energy estimates for
\bar{f}
, we have

\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\bar{f}(0)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{f}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}.

(8.54)

Substituting
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
in (
8.53
) and using (
8.54
), we can prove that

\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad\theta<r-2.

As a result, we also have

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad k<r-1.

Step 4:
The case
2<r\leq s
. We also set
t_{0}=0
. Based on the above result in Step 1 and Step 2, we transform the initial data in
H^{1}\times L^{2}
. Applying
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}
to (
8.1
), we have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f.

Let
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
. Then
\bar{f}
is a solution to

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f},\\
(\bar{f},\partial_{t}\bar{f})|_{t=0}\in H^{1}\times L^{2}.\end{cases}

(8.55)

Considering
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, let us calculate

\begin{split}-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}=&[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}\partial_{i}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\\
&\ +\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\partial_{i}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}).\end{split}

(8.56)

By Kato-Ponce commutator estimates and product estimates, we have

\begin{split}&\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}\partial_{i}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\partial_{i}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}},\end{split}

(8.57)

Considering
12
12

12

please see the definition of
\mathcal{H}
and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
)

\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad 2<r\leq s,

and combining with (
8.56
), (
8.57
), we have

\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).

(8.58)

Using the discussion in case
r=1
, namely (
8.41
), we then get the Strichartz estimates for
\bar{f}
for
\theta<-1
,

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.59)

Note
r>2
and
1-r<-1
. We then take
\theta=1-r
. So we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.60)

Using (
8.52
), (
8.59
) yields

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}),\quad\theta<-1.\end{split}

(8.61)

By (
8.60
), (
8.58
) and the energy estimates for
\bar{f}
, we have

\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\bar{f}(0)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{f}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}.

(8.62)

Substituting
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
in (
8.61
) and using (
8.62
), we can prove that

\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad\theta<r-2.

As a result, we also have

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad k<r-1.

Step 5:
The case
s<r\leq 3
. We also set
t_{0}=0
. Based on the above result in Step 1 and Step 2, we transform the initial data in
H^{1}\times L^{2}
. Applying
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}
to (
8.1
), we have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f.

Let
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
. Then
\bar{f}
is a solution to

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f},\\
(\bar{f},\partial_{t}\bar{f})|_{t=0}\in H^{1}\times L^{2}.\end{cases}

(8.63)

Considering
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, let us calculate

\begin{split}-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}=&[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\partial_{i}\bar{f}.\end{split}

(8.64)

By Kato-Ponce commutator estimates, we have

\begin{split}&\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\partial_{i}\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{p_{2}}_{x}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{q_{2}}_{x}},\end{split}

(8.65)

where
p_{2}=\frac{3}{\frac{1}{2}-s+r}
and
q_{2}=\frac{3}{1+s-r}
. By Sobolev’s inequality, we get

\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{p_{2}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}.

(8.66)

By Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, we can see that
13
13

13

The number
s
and
s_{0}
satisfying
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
.

\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{q_{2}}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\bar{f}\|^{\frac{s-2}{3-s}}_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{-\frac{s}{2}}d\bar{f}\|^{\frac{5-2s}{3-s}}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

(8.67)

Considering
14
14

14

please see the definition of
\mathcal{H}
and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
)

\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad s<r\leq 3,

and combining with (
8.64
), (
8.65
), we have

\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{-\frac{s}{2}}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).

Using the discussion in case
r=1
, namely (
8.41
), we then get the Strichartz estimates for
\bar{f}
for
\theta<-1
,

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.68)

Note
-\frac{s}{2}<-1
. We then take
\theta=-\frac{s}{2}
. So we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.69)

Using (
8.69
), (
8.68
) yields

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}),\quad\theta<-1.\end{split}

(8.70)

By (
8.67
), (
8.69
) and the energy estimates for
\bar{f}
, we have

\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\bar{f}(0)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{f}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}.

(8.71)

Substituting
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
in (
8.78
) and using (
8.79
), we can prove that

\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad\theta<r-2.

As a result, we also have

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad k<r-1.

Step 6:
The case
3<r\leq s+1
. We also set
t_{0}=0
. Based on the above result in Step 1 and Step 2, we transform the initial data in
H^{1}\times L^{2}
. Applying
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}
to (
8.1
), we have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f.

Let
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
. Then
\bar{f}
is a solution to

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f},\\
(\bar{f},\partial_{t}\bar{f})|_{t=0}\in H^{1}\times L^{2}.\end{cases}

(8.72)

Considering
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, let us calculate

\begin{split}-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}=&[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\partial_{i}\bar{f}.\end{split}

(8.73)

By Kato-Ponce commutator estimates, we have

\begin{split}&\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\partial_{\alpha}\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\partial_{i}\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}},\end{split}

(8.74)

Considering
15
15

15

please see the definition of
\mathcal{H}
and (
5.3
)-(
5.5
)

\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{r-1}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\qquad 3<r\leq s+1,

and combining with (
8.73
), (
8.74
), we have

\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\left<\partial\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).

(8.75)

Using the discussion in case
r=1
, namely (
8.41
), we then get the Strichartz estimates for
\bar{f}
for
\theta<-1
,

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.76)

Note
r>3
and
2-r<-1
. We then take
\theta=2-r
. So we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{2-r}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}).\end{split}

(8.77)

Using (
8.77
), (
8.76
) yields

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}),\quad\theta<-1.\end{split}

(8.78)

By (
8.60
), (
8.58
) and the energy estimates for
\bar{f}
, we have

\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\bar{f}(0)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{f}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}.

(8.79)

Substituting
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
in (
8.78
) and using (
8.79
), we can prove that

\|\left<\partial\right>^{\theta}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad\theta<r-2.

As a result, we also have

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad k<r-1.

At this stage, we have finished the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

9.
Continuous dependence of solutions on initial data.

In this part, we will study the continuous dependence of solutions of isentropic compressible Euler equations. We will discuss the problem of continuous dependence by extending a frequency envelope approach from the incompressible Euler equations
[
52
]
to the compressible Euler system, This approach was originally devised by Tao and developed by Ifrim-Tataru for quasilinear hyperbolic systems
[
21
]
. We should mention that there are some works considering the continuous dependence for incompressible Euler equations
[
10
,
23
]
and incompressible Neo-Hookean materials
[
6
]
, and they need some additional regularity conditions for the vorticity. Different with these historical results, we are concerned with the rough solutions for a wave-transport system, and the proof crucially relies on Strichartz estimates of a linear wave equation endowed with the acoustic metric.

Corollary 9.1

(Continuous dependence on initial data)
.

Let
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
. Let the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
be stated in Theorem
1.4
. Then

\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0}.

(9.1)

Let
\{(\mathbf{v}_{0m},\rho_{0m},h_{0m},\mathbf{w}_{0m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
be a sequence of initial data converging to
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
in space
H^{s}\times H^{s}\times H^{s_{0}+1}\times H^{s_{0}}
. Then for every
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, there exists
T>0
such that the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0m},\rho_{0m},h_{0m},\mathbf{w}_{0m})
has a unique solution
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
on
[0,T]
, and the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
has a unique solution
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
on
[0,T]
. Moreover, the sequence
\{(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
converge to
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
on
[0,T]
in
\in H^{s}_{x}\times H_{x}^{s}\times H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}\times H_{x}^{s_{0}}
.

Before we prove Corollary
9.1
, let us introduce a definition of frequency envelope, which is proposed by Tao
[
52
]
.

Definition 9.1
.

We say that
\{c_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\in\ell^{2}
is a frequency envelope for a function
f
in
H^{s}
if we have the
following two properties:

(1) Energy bound:

\|P_{k}f\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim c_{k},

(9.2)

(2) Slowly varying:

\frac{c_{k}}{c_{j}}\lesssim 2^{\delta|j-k|},\quad j,k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}.

(9.3)

We call such envelopes sharp, if

\|f\|^{2}_{H^{s}}\approx\sum_{k\geq 0}c^{2}_{k}.

Proof of Corollary
9.1
.

For
\{(\mathbf{v}_{0m},\rho_{0m},h_{0m},\mathbf{w}_{0m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
is a sequence of initial data converging to
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
in space
H^{s}\times H^{s}\times H^{s_{0}+1}\times H^{s_{0}}
, by using (
9.1
), we obtain

\|\mathbf{v}_{0m}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0m}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0m}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0m}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq 100M_{0},\quad\text{for \ large}\ m,

By using the existence result in Theorem
1.4
, there is
T>0
such that the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0m},\rho_{0m},h_{0m},\mathbf{w}_{0m})
has a unique solution
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
on
[0,T]
, and the Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
has a unique solution
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
on
[0,T]
. We then divide the proof of convergence into three steps.

Step 1: Convergence in weaker spaces
. By
L^{2}_{x}
energy estimates, and using the Strichartz estimates, for
m,l\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we have

\begin{split}&\|(\mathbf{v}_{m}-\mathbf{v}_{l},\rho_{m}-\rho_{l},h_{m}-h_{l})(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|(\mathbf{w}_{m}-\mathbf{w}_{l})(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|(\mathbf{v}_{0m}-\mathbf{v}_{0l},\rho_{0m}-\rho_{0l})\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h_{0m}-h_{0l})\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0m}-\mathbf{w}_{0l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

This implies that, for
t\in[0,T]
,

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})=(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\quad\mathrm{in}\ \ L^{2}_{x}.

By interpolation formula, we then have

\|\mathbf{v}_{m}-\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}_{m}-\mathbf{v}\|^{1-\frac{\sigma}{s}}_{L_{x}^{2}}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}-\mathbf{v}\|^{\frac{\sigma}{s}}_{H_{x}^{s}},\quad 0\leq\sigma<s.

This leads to

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{v}_{m}=\mathbf{v}\ \ \mathrm{in}\ \ H^{\sigma}_{x},\ \ 0\leq\sigma<s,

In a similar way, we can obtain that

\begin{split}&\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\rho_{m}=\rho\ \ \mathrm{in}\ \ H^{\sigma}_{x},\ \ 0\leq\sigma<s,\\
&\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}h_{m}=h\ \ \mathrm{in}\ \ H^{\gamma}_{x},\ \ 0\leq\gamma<s_{0}+1,\\
&\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{w}_{m}=\mathbf{w}\ \ \mathrm{in}\ \ H^{\theta}_{x},\ \ 0\leq\theta<s_{0}.\end{split}

(9.4)

It remains for us to prove the convergence with the highest derivatives of
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
.

Step 2: Constructing smooth solutions
. We set
\mathbf{U}_{0}=(v^{1}_{0},v^{2}_{0},v^{3}_{0},p(\rho_{0}),h)^{\mathrm{T}}
. By
[
52
,
21
]
, there exists a sharp frequency envelope for
v^{1}_{0},v^{2}_{0},v^{3}_{0}

\rho_{0}
and
h_{0}
respectively. Let
\{c^{(i)}_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
(
i=1,2,3,4
) be a sharp frequency envelope respectively for
v^{1}_{0},v^{2}_{0},v^{3}_{0},\rho_{0}
in
H^{s}
. Let
\{c^{(5)}_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
be a sharp frequency envelope respectively for
h_{0}
in
H^{s_{0}+1}
. Let
d^{(1)}_{k},d^{(2)}_{k},d^{(3)}_{k}
be a sharp frequency envelope for
w^{1}_{0},w^{2}_{0}
and
w^{3}_{0}
in
H^{s_{0}}
. We choose a family of regularizations
\mathbf{U}^{l}_{0}=(v^{1l}_{0},v^{2l}_{0},v^{3l}_{0},p(\rho^{l}_{0}),h^{l}_{0})^{\mathrm{T}}\in\cap^{\infty}_{a=0}H^{a}
at frequencies
\lesssim 2^{l}
where
l
is a frequency parameter and
l\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Denote

\mathbf{w}^{l}_{0}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}_{0}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}^{l}_{0}.

Then the functions
v^{1l}_{0},v^{2l}_{0},v^{3l}_{0},\rho^{l}_{0}
, and
\mathbf{w}^{l}_{0}=(w^{1l}_{0},w^{2l}_{0},w^{3l}_{0})^{\mathrm{T}}
have the following properties:

(i) uniform bounds

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}v^{il}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim c^{(i)}_{k},\quad\|P_{k}w^{il}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim d^{(i)}_{k},\qquad i=1,2,3,\\
&\|P_{k}\rho^{l}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim c^{(4)}_{k},\ \quad\|P_{k}h^{l}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim c^{(5)}_{k},\end{split}

(9.5)

(ii) high frequency bounds

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}v^{il}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s+1}}\lesssim 2^{k}c^{(i)}_{k},\quad\|P_{k}w^{il}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim 2^{k}d^{(i)}_{k},\qquad i=1,2,3,\\
&\|P_{k}\rho^{l}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s+1}}\lesssim 2^{k}c^{(4)}_{k},\quad\|P_{k}h^{l}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+2}}\lesssim 2^{k}c^{(5)}_{k},\end{split}

(9.6)

(iii) difference bounds

\begin{split}&\|v^{i(l+1)}_{0}-v^{il}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-sl}c^{(i)}_{l},\quad\|w^{i(l+1)}_{0}-w^{il}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-s_{0}l}d^{(i)}_{l},\quad i=1,2,3,\\
&\|\rho^{l+1}_{0}-\rho^{l}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-{s}l}c^{(4)}_{l},\quad\quad\|h^{l+1}_{0}-h^{l}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-(s_{0}+1)l}c^{(5)}_{l},\end{split}

(9.7)

(iv) limit

\begin{split}&\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{v}^{l}_{0}=\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathrm{in}\ H^{s},\qquad\quad\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\rho_{0}^{l}=\rho_{0},\mathrm{in}\ H^{s},\\
&\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{w}^{l}_{0}=\mathbf{w}_{0},\mathrm{in}\ H^{s_{0}},\qquad\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}h^{l}_{0}=h_{0},\mathrm{in}\ H^{s_{0}+1}.\end{split}

(9.8)

We set
\mathbf{v}^{l}=(v^{1l},v^{2l},v^{3l})
. Consider a Cauchy problem (
1.1
) with the initial data
with the initial data

\begin{split}&(\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l},h^{l},\mathbf{w}^{l})|_{t=0}=(\mathbf{v}^{l}_{0},\rho^{l}_{0},h^{l}_{0},\mathbf{w}^{l}_{0}).\end{split}

By Proposition
4.1
, we can obtain a family of smooth solutions
(\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l},h^{l},\mathbf{w}^{l})
on a time interval
[0,T]
(
T
depends only on the size of
\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}
). Furthermore, based on the estimates of (
9.5
)-(
9.8
), using Proposition
4.1
again, we can claim that

\bullet
high frequency bounds

\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s+1}}+\|\rho^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}+\|h^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+2}}\lesssim 2^{l}M_{0},

(9.9)

\bullet
difference bounds

\|(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l})\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-sl}c_{l},

(9.10)

and

\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim c_{l},

(9.11)

and

\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-(s-1)l}c_{l},\quad\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim c_{l},

(9.12)

where
c_{l}=\sum^{5}_{a=1}c^{(a)}_{l}+\sum^{3}_{a=1}d^{(1)}_{l}+d^{(2)}_{l}+d^{(3)}_{l}
. By using (
4.2
) in Proposition
4.1
and combining with (
9.6
), then the estimate (
9.9
) holds.
Let us postpone to prove (
9.10
)-(
9.12
). By using

\rho-\rho^{l}=\sum^{\infty}_{k=l}(\rho^{k+1}-\rho^{k}),

and the estimate (
9.10
), we can conclude

\|\rho-\rho^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim c_{\geq l}.

(9.13)

Here
c_{\geq l}=\sum_{j\geq l}c_{j}
. Similarly, by using (
9.11
) and (
9.12
), we also have

\|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim c_{\geq l},\quad\|h-h^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim c_{\geq l},\quad\|\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim c_{\geq l}.

(9.14)

Step 3: Convergence of the highest derivatives
. For the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0m},\rho_{0m},h_{0m},\mathbf{w}_{0m})
and
\mathbf{v}_{0m}=(v^{1}_{0m},v^{2}_{0m},v^{3}_{0m})
, let
\{c^{(i)m}_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
be frequency envelopes for the initial data
v^{i}_{0m}
in
H^{s}(i=1,2,3)
. Let
\{c^{(4)m}_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
be frequency envelopes for the initial data
\rho_{0m}
in
H^{s_{0}+1}
. Let
\{c^{(5)m}_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
be frequency envelopes for the initial data
h_{0m}
in
H^{s_{0}}
. Let
\{d^{(i)m}_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
be frequency envelopes for the initial data
w^{i}_{0m}
in
H^{s_{0}}(i=1,2,3)
. We choose a family of regularizations
(v^{1l}_{0m},v^{2l}_{0m},v^{3l}_{0m},\rho^{l}_{0m},h^{l}_{0m})\in\cap^{\infty}_{a=0}H^{a}
at frequencies
\lesssim 2^{l}
. Denote

\mathbf{w}^{l}_{0m}=\bar{\rho}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}_{0m}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}^{l}_{0m}.

Then the functions
v^{1l}_{0m},v^{2l}_{0m},\rho^{l}_{0m},h^{l}_{0m}
, and
\mathbf{w}^{l}_{0m}
can also have

(1) uniform bounds

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}v^{il}_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim c^{(i)m}_{k},\quad\|P_{k}w^{il}_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim d^{(i)m}_{k},\quad i=1,2,3,\\
&\|P_{k}\rho^{l}_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim c^{(4)m}_{k},\quad\|P_{k}h^{l}_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim c^{(5)m}_{k},\end{split}

(2) high frequency bounds for
a\geq 0

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}v^{il}_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{s+a}}\lesssim 2^{ak}c^{(i)}_{k},\quad i=1,2,3,\\
&\|P_{k}\rho^{l}_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{s+a}}\lesssim 2^{ak}c^{(4)}_{k},\quad\|P_{k}h^{l}_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1+a}}\lesssim 2^{ak}c^{(5)}_{k},\end{split}

(3) difference bounds

\begin{split}&\|v^{i(l+1)}_{0m}-v^{il}_{0m}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-sl}c^{(i)m}_{l},\quad\|w^{i(l+1)}_{0m}-w^{il}_{0m}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-s_{0}l}d^{(i)m}_{l},\qquad i=1,2,3,\\
&\|\rho^{l+1}_{0m}-\rho^{l}_{0m}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-sl}c^{(4)m}_{l},\quad\ \ \ \|h^{i(l+1)}_{0m}-h^{il}_{0m}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-(s_{0}+1)l}c^{(5)m}_{l},\end{split}

(4) limit

\begin{split}&\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{v}^{l}_{0m}=\mathbf{v}_{0m},\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{s},\qquad\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\rho_{0m}^{l}=\rho_{0m},\quad\mathrm{in}\ H^{s},\\
&\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{w}^{l}_{0m}=\mathbf{w}_{0m},\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{s_{0}},\qquad\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}h^{l}_{0m}=h_{0m},\ \mathrm{in}\ H^{s_{0}+1},\end{split}

(9.15)

By Proposition
4.1
, we can obtain a family of smooth solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{l},\rho_{j}^{l},h_{j}^{l},\mathbf{w}_{j}^{l})
on a time interval
16
16

16

The time
T
is uniform for
j
, because its initial data is uniformly bounded.

[0,T]
with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0m}^{l},\rho_{0m}^{l},\rho_{0m}^{l},\mathbf{w}_{0m}^{l})
. Let us now prove

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\rho_{m}=\rho,\quad\mathrm{in}\ \ H^{s}_{x}.

(9.16)

Inserting
\rho^{l}_{m}
into
\rho_{m}-\rho
, we have

\begin{split}\|\rho_{m}-\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\leq&\|\rho^{l}_{m}-\rho^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho^{l}-\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho^{l}_{m}-\rho_{m}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}.\end{split}

(9.17)

Due to (
9.15
), we have

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\rho^{l}_{0m}=\rho^{l}_{0}\quad\mathrm{in}\ {H^{\sigma}_{x}},\ 0\leq\sigma<\infty.

(9.18)

Using similar idea of the proof of (
9.4
), we can derive that

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\rho^{l}_{m}=\rho^{l}\quad\mathrm{in}\ {H^{\sigma}_{x}},\ 0\leq\sigma<\infty.

(9.19)

From (
9.18
), we have

c^{(4)j}_{k}\rightarrow c^{(4)}_{k},\ j\rightarrow\infty.

(9.20)

Using (
9.17
) and (
9.13
), (
9.17
) yields

\begin{split}\|\rho_{m}-\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim\|\rho^{l}_{m}-\rho^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+c_{\geq l}+c^{(4)j}_{\geq l},\end{split}

(9.21)

Taking the limit of (
9.21
) for
j\rightarrow\infty
, by using (
9.19
) and (
9.20
), this leads to

\begin{split}\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|\rho_{m}-\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim c_{\geq l}+c^{(4)}_{\geq l}\lesssim c_{\geq l}.\end{split}

(9.22)

Finally, taking
l\rightarrow\infty
for (
9.22
) and using
\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}c_{\geq l}=0
, we have

\begin{split}\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|\rho_{m}-\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}=0.\end{split}

(9.23)

Similarly, by using (
9.10
)-(
9.12
), we also get

\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}-\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}=0,\quad\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|h_{m}-h\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}=0,\quad\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}-\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}=0.

Hence, we have finished the proof of Theorem
1.6
.
∎

It only remains for us to prove (
9.10
)-(
9.12
). By using the Strichartz estimates of a linear wave equation endowed with the acoustic metric, namely in Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Estimates (
9.10
)-(
9.12
).

To prove (
9.10
)-(
9.12
), we also use the hyperbolic system to discuss
\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}
,
\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}
and
h^{l+1}-h^{l}
. But for
\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}
, we use it’s transport equations of
\mathbf{w}^{l+1}
and
\mathbf{w}^{l}
. However, if we consider the difference terms
(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l},\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})
, then the original system (
1.8
) is destroyed or it’s a disturbed system. To avoid the loss of derivatives of
\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}
and
\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}
, the Strichartz estimates in Proposition
7.1
plays a key role. While, the difference
\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}
and
h^{l+1}-h^{l}
is much worse than
\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}
for there is no dispersion.

We will prove (
9.10
)-(
9.12
) by bootstrap arguments. At time
t=0
, by Bernstein’s inequality, the data satiefies

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{v}_{0}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}_{0}^{l}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq C2^{-sl}c_{l},\qquad\ \ \|\rho_{0}^{l+1}-\rho_{0}^{l}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq C2^{-sl}c_{l},\\
&\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq C2^{-(s_{0}+1)l}c_{l},\quad\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq C2^{-s_{0}l}c_{l}.\end{split}

(9.24)

For
t\leq T
, we assume

\begin{split}&\|(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l})\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq 10C2^{-sl}c_{l},\quad\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}+1}}\leq 10Cc_{l},\\
&\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq 10C2^{-(s-\frac{1}{2})l}c_{l},\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq 10Cc_{l},\end{split}

(9.25)

and

\begin{split}&2^{l}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,t]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&\ +2^{l}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,t]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq Cc_{l}.\end{split}

(9.26)

where
C
is the constant stated in (
9.25
). We will establish the desired estimates into four steps.

Step 1: The difference of velocity and density
. Let
\mathbf{U}^{l}=(\mathbf{v}^{l},p(\rho^{l}),h^{l})^{\mathrm{T}}
. Then
\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l}
satisfies

\begin{cases}&A^{0}(\mathbf{U}^{l+1})\partial_{t}(\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l})+A^{i}(\mathbf{U}^{l+1})\partial_{i}(\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l})=\Pi^{l},\\
&(\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l})|_{t=0}=\mathbf{U}_{0}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{l},\end{cases}

(9.27)

where

\Pi^{l}=-[A^{0}(\mathbf{U}^{l+1})-A^{0}(\mathbf{U}^{l})]\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}^{l}-[A^{i}(\mathbf{U}^{l+1})-A^{i}(\mathbf{U}^{l})]\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}^{l}.

(9.28)

By energy estimates, for
a\geq 0
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{a}_{x}}\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{U}^{l+1},d\mathbf{U}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{a}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{U}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{a}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{U}^{l+1},d\mathbf{U}^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{a}_{x}}+2^{l}\|\mathbf{U}^{l+1}-\mathbf{U}^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial\mathbf{U}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{a-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(9.29)

By using (
1.8
) and (
9.29
), we then get

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{v}_{0}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}_{0}^{l},\rho_{0}^{l+1}-\rho_{0}^{l},h_{0}^{l+1}-h_{0}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|(\partial\mathbf{v}^{l+1},\partial\rho^{l+1},\partial h^{l+1})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|(\partial\mathbf{v}^{l},\partial\rho^{l},\partial h^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}^{l},\partial\rho^{l},\partial h^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(9.30)

By Gronwall’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq&\|\mathbf{v}_{0}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}_{0}^{l},\rho_{0}^{l+1}-\rho_{0}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}A(t)\leq 2^{-sl}c_{l}A(t),\end{split}

(9.31)

where

\begin{split}A(t)=&C\exp\left\{\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|(\partial\mathbf{v}^{l},\partial\rho^{l},\partial h^{l})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+2^{l}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)d\tau\right\}.\end{split}

By using (
9.26
), for
t\in[0,\frac{1}{400(1+C_{0})^{2}}T]
, (
9.31
) yields

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l},h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq 4C2^{-sl}c_{l}.\end{split}

(9.32)

Step 2: The difference of the specific vorticity
. Due to
\mathbf{w}^{l}=\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}^{l}
, so we obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\partial(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq 8C2^{-(s-1)l}c_{l}.\end{split}

(9.33)

For
\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}
, using elliptic estimates, we have

\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\|\textrm{div}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|\textrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-1}}.

(9.34)

Noting

\textrm{div}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})=\partial_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\partial^{i}\rho^{l+1}+\partial_{i}\mathbf{w}^{l}\partial^{i}(\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}),

and using (
9.36
), Hölder’s inequality and (
9.25
), we derive that

\begin{split}\|\textrm{div}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}\leq&\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+}}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}\\
\leq&3Cc_{l}.\end{split}

(9.35)

On the other hand, we also have

\begin{split}&\mathbf{T}^{l}(\mathrm{curl}^{i}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{l}+F^{il})=\partial^{i}\big(2\partial_{n}v^{l}_{a}\partial^{n}w^{al}\big)+K^{il},\quad i=1,2,3,\end{split}

(9.36)

where
K^{il}
has the same formulations with
K^{i}
by replacing
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
to
(\mathbf{v}^{l},\rho^{l},h^{l},\mathbf{w}^{l})
, and

F^{il}=-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\rho^{l}\cdot\mathrm{curl}_{k}\mathbf{w}^{l}-2\partial^{a}{\rho^{l}}\partial^{i}w^{l}_{a}+2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v^{ml}\partial_{m}\partial_{k}h^{l}-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{k}h^{l}\Delta v^{l}_{j}.

So we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{div}\mathbf{F}^{l}=\partial_{i}F^{il}=&-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}\rho^{l}\cdot\partial_{i}\mathrm{curl}_{k}\mathbf{w}^{l}-2\partial_{i}\partial^{a}{\rho^{l}}\partial^{i}w^{l}_{a}\\
&-2\partial^{a}{\rho^{l}}\Delta w^{l}_{a}-2\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial\rho^{l}\partial_{j}v^{lm}\partial_{m}\partial_{k}h^{l}\\
&+\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}}\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{i}\rho^{l}\partial_{k}h^{l}\Delta v^{l}_{j}-\mathrm{e}^{-\rho^{l}}\partial_{k}h^{l}\Delta(\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}w^{il}).\end{split}

(9.37)

Using the Hodge decomposition, we have

\|\textrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-1}}\leq\|\textrm{curl}\textrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}.

(9.38)

Using (
9.36
), we have

\begin{split}&\mathbf{T}^{l}\{\mathrm{curl}^{i}\mathrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})+F^{i(l+1)}-F^{il}\}\\
=&\partial^{i}\{2\partial_{n}(v^{l+1}_{a}-v^{l}_{a})\partial^{n}w^{a(l+1)}\}+\partial^{i}\{2\partial_{n}v^{l}_{a}\partial^{n}(w^{a(l+1)}-w^{al})\}+K^{i(l+1)}-K^{il}.\end{split}

(9.39)

For simplicity, we denote

G(t)=\|\mathrm{curl}^{i}\mathrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})+F^{i(l+1)}-F^{il}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}.

(9.40)

Operating
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}
on (
9.39
), multiplying
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\mathrm{curl}_{i}\mathrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})+F_{i}^{(l+1)}-F_{i}^{l}\right\}
, and integrating it on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we then obtain

\begin{split}G^{2}(t)\leq&G^{2}(0)+G^{a}+G^{b}+G^{c},\end{split}

(9.41)

where

\begin{split}G^{a}=&|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\partial^{i}\{2\partial_{n}(v^{l+1}_{a}-v^{l}_{a})\partial^{n}w^{a(l+1)}\}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\{\mathrm{curl}_{i}\mathrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})+F_{i}^{l+1}-F_{i}^{l}\}dxd\tau|,\\
G^{b}=&|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\partial^{i}\{2\partial_{n}v^{l}_{a}\partial^{n}(w^{a(l+1)}-w^{al})\}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\{\mathrm{curl}_{i}\mathrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})+F_{i}^{l+1}-F_{i}^{l}\}dxd\tau|,\\
G^{c}=&|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\{K^{i(l+1)}-K^{il}\}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\{\mathrm{curl}_{i}\mathrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})+F_{i}^{l+1}-F_{i}^{l}\}dxd\tau|.\end{split}

Using (
9.25
), it’s direct for us to get

G^{2}(0)\leq\frac{3}{2}(Cc_{l})^{2}.

(9.42)

Integrating by parts for
G^{a}
, we have

\begin{split}G^{a}\leq&\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\{2\partial_{n}(v^{l+1}_{a}-v^{l}_{a})\partial^{n}w^{a(l+1)}\}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\partial^{i}\{\mathrm{curl}_{i}\mathrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})+F_{i}^{(l+1)}-F_{i}^{l}\}dxd\tau\right|\\
=&\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\{2\partial_{n}(v^{l+1}_{a}-v^{l}_{a})\partial^{n}w^{a(l+1)}\}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\partial^{i}\{F_{i}^{(l+1)}-F_{i}^{l}\}dxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.10
and (
9.37
), we have

\begin{split}G^{a}\lesssim&\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|\partial\rho^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial h^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|\partial(\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial(h^{l+1}-h^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau+\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\end{split}

By using (
9.25
), for
t\in[0,\frac{1}{64(1+C)^{2}}T]
, we have

G^{a}\leq\frac{3}{2}(Cc_{l})^{2}.

(9.43)

Using a similar way to handle
G^{a}
, we can bound
G^{b}
by

\begin{split}G^{b}\lesssim&\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|\partial\rho^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial h^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|\partial(\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\partial(h^{l+1}-h^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau+\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}_{x}}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}d\tau\end{split}

By using (
9.25
), for
t\in[0,\frac{1}{400(1+C)^{2}}T]
, we have

G^{b}\leq\frac{3}{2}(Cc_{l})^{2}.

(9.44)

By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.10
, we have

\begin{split}G^{c}\lesssim&\int^{t}_{0}(\|\partial(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})G(\tau)d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|\partial(\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\partial\rho^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}})G(\tau)d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|\partial(h^{l+1}-h^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\partial h^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}})G(\tau)d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}})G(\tau)d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}+\|\rho^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}})G(\tau)d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|\rho^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}})G(\tau)d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\|\rho^{l}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|\rho^{l}\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}})G(\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

By using (
9.25
), for
t\in[0,\frac{1}{400(1+C)^{2}}T]
, we have

G^{c}\leq\frac{3}{2}(Cc_{l})^{2}.

(9.45)

Gathering (
9.41
)-(
9.45
), we show that

G(t)\leq 3Cc_{l}.

(9.46)

Hence, we get

\begin{split}\|\textrm{curl}\textrm{curl}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\leq&G(t)+\|\mathbf{F}^{l+1}-\mathbf{F}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\leq&5Cc_{l}.\end{split}

(9.47)

By (
9.35
), (
9.38
) and (
9.47
), we have proved

\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq 8Cc_{l}.

(9.48)

Step 3: The difference of the highest derivatives of entropy
. Due to (
9.32
), we only need to discuss
\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1+s_{0}}}
. Note
h^{l}
satisfying (
2.48
). Hence, for
l\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we get

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\mathbf{v}^{l}\cdot\nabla)\left\{\partial^{k}(\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}=&-2\partial^{k}(\partial_{i}v^{jl})\partial_{j}\partial^{i}h^{l}+Y^{kl},\end{split}

(9.49)

where

\begin{split}Y^{kl}=&-2\partial_{i}v^{lj}\partial^{k}\partial^{i}(\partial_{j}h^{l})+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}w^{l}_{m}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}\partial^{k}\rho^{l}\partial_{j}h^{l}+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}\partial^{k}w^{l}_{m}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}\partial_{j}h^{l}\\
&+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}w^{l}_{m}\partial_{j}h^{l}\partial_{k}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}w^{l}_{m}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}\partial^{k}\partial_{j}h^{l}.\end{split}

(9.50)

As a result, we find that

\begin{split}&(\partial_{t}+\mathbf{v}^{l+1}\cdot\nabla)\left\{\partial^{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}\\
=&(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l})\cdot\nabla\left\{\partial^{k}(\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}-2\partial^{k}(\partial_{i}v^{j(l+1)}-\partial_{i}v^{jl})\partial_{j}\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\\
&-2\partial^{k}\partial_{i}v^{jl}\partial_{j}\partial^{i}(h^{l+1}-h^{l})+Y^{k(l+1)k}-Y^{kl},\end{split}

(9.51)

where

\begin{split}Y^{lk}=&-2\partial_{i}v^{lj}\partial^{k}\partial^{i}(\partial_{j}h^{l})+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}w^{l}_{m}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}\partial^{k}\rho^{l}\partial_{j}h^{l}+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}\partial^{k}w^{l}_{m}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}\partial_{j}h^{l}\\
&+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}w^{l}_{m}\partial_{j}h^{l}\partial_{k}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}+\epsilon^{ijm}\mathrm{e}^{\rho^{l}}w^{l}_{m}\partial^{i}\rho^{l}\partial^{k}\partial_{j}h^{l}.\end{split}

(9.52)

For simplicity, we denote

B(t)=\|\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.

(9.53)

Operating
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}
on (
9.51
), and multiplying
\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}
, and integrating it on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we then obtain

\begin{split}B^{2}(t)\leq&B^{2}(0)+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}^{l+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}B^{2}(\tau)d\tau+V_{1}+V_{2}+V_{3}+V_{4},\end{split}

(9.54)

where

\begin{split}V_{1}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l})\cdot\nabla\partial^{k}(\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
V_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\partial_{i}v^{j(l+1)}-\partial_{i}v^{jl})\partial_{j}\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
V_{3}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}\partial_{i}v^{jl}\partial_{j}\partial^{i}(h^{l+1}-h^{l})\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
V_{4}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}(Y^{k(l+1)}-Y^{kl})\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

We will estimate the right terms in (
9.54
) one by one. By Lemma
2.10
, Bernstein’s inequality and (
9.25
), we can bound
V_{1}
as

\begin{split}V_{1}\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}2^{l}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\cdot\|\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\cdot B(\tau)d\tau\\
&\leq\frac{3}{2}(Cc_{l})^{2}.\end{split}

(9.55)

Using Lemma
2.10
, the Bernstein inequality and (
9.25
) again, we have

\begin{split}V_{3}\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\cdot 2^{l}\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}_{x}}\cdot B(\tau)d\tau\\
&\leq\frac{3}{2}(Cc_{l})^{2}.\end{split}

(9.56)

For
V_{4}
, by using (
9.52
), Lemma
2.10
, and Lemma
2.11
, we can derive that

\begin{split}V_{4}\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\cdot\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\cdot B(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\cdot\|h^{l}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\cdot B(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\cdot\|\rho^{l}\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(1+\|\rho^{l}\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}})\|h^{l}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\cdot B(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\cdot\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(1+\|\rho^{l}\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}})\|h^{l}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\cdot B(\tau)d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\cdot\|\rho^{l}\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(1+\|\rho^{l}\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}})\|h^{l+1}-h^{l}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}\cdot B(\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

By using (
9.25
), we get

\begin{split}V_{3}\leq\frac{3}{2}(Cc_{l})^{2}.\end{split}

(9.57)

For
V_{2}
, let us integrate it by parts

\begin{split}V_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\partial_{i}v^{j(l+1)}-\partial_{i}v^{jl})\partial_{j}\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\Delta v^{j(l+1)}-\Delta v^{jl})\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
&+C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\partial_{i}v^{j(l+1)}-\partial_{i}v^{jl})\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{i}\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\Delta v^{j(l+1)}-\Delta v^{jl})\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
&+C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{i}(\Delta v^{j(l+1)}-\Delta v^{jl})\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{i}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
=&V_{2}^{A}+V_{2}^{B}.\end{split}

Here

\begin{split}V_{2}^{A}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\Delta v^{j(l+1)}-\Delta v^{jl})\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\quad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|,\\
V_{2}^{B}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{i}(\Delta v^{j(l+1)}-\Delta v^{jl})\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{i}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

For
V_{2}^{A}
and
V_{2}^{B}
, there is a derivative loss, so we use the Hodge decomposition and (
1.8
) to transfer the derivatives. By
Equation (
2.32
), we have

\Delta v^{jl}=-\mathbf{T}^{l}(\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l})+\partial^{j}v^{kl}\partial_{k}{\rho}^{l}+\mathrm{curl}^{j}\big(\mathrm{e}^{{\rho}^{l}}\mathbf{w}^{l}\big),

where
\mathbf{T}^{l}=\partial_{t}+\mathbf{v}^{l}\cdot\nabla
. As a result, we get

\begin{split}\Delta(v^{j(l+1)}-v^{jl})=&-\mathbf{T}^{l+1}(\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l})+(\mathbf{v}^{l}-\mathbf{v}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l}\\
&+\mathrm{curl}^{j}\big(\mathrm{e}^{{\rho}^{l+1}}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l})\big)\ -\epsilon^{jmk}\partial_{m}(\mathrm{e}^{{\rho}^{l+1}}-\mathrm{e}^{{\rho}^{l}})\mathbf{w}^{l}_{k}\\
&+\partial^{j}v^{k(l+1)}\partial_{k}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}v^{kl}\partial_{k}{\rho}^{l}.\end{split}

(9.58)

Substituting (
9.58
) in
V^{A}_{2}
, gives

V^{A}_{2}\leq V^{A1}_{2}+V^{A2}_{2}+V^{A3}_{2}+V^{A4}_{2},

(9.59)

where

\begin{split}V^{A1}_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}\big(\mathbf{T}^{l+1}(\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l})\big)\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|,\\
V^{A2}_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}\big((\mathbf{v}^{l}-\mathbf{v}^{l+1})\cdot\nabla\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l}\big)\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|,\\
V^{A3}_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}\big(\mathrm{curl}^{j}(\mathrm{e}^{{\rho}^{l+1}}(\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}))-\epsilon^{jmk}\partial_{m}(\mathrm{e}^{{\rho}^{l+1}}-\mathrm{e}^{{\rho}^{l}})\mathbf{w}^{l}_{k}\big)\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|,\\
V^{A4}_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial^{k}(\partial^{j}v^{k(l+1)}\partial_{k}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}v^{kl}\partial_{k}{\rho}^{l})\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.10
and Hölder’s inequality, we can estimate
V^{A1}_{2},V^{A2}_{2},V^{A3}_{2}
and
V^{A4}_{2}
as follows,

\begin{split}V^{A2}_{2}\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial(\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|{\rho}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|h^{l+1}\|_{H^{s_{0}+1}_{x}}B(\tau)d\tau,\\
V^{A3}_{2}\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}^{l+1}-\mathbf{w}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}B(\tau)d\tau+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial(\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l})\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}B(\tau)d\tau,\\
V^{A4}_{2}\leq&C\int^{t}_{0}\{\|\partial\rho^{l+1}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\mathbf{v}^{l+1}-\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|\partial\mathbf{v}^{l}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\rho^{l+1}-\rho^{l}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\}B(\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

(9.60)

For
V^{A1}_{2}
, by commutator rule, we write it as

\begin{split}V^{A1}_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\mathbf{T}^{l+1}\big(\partial^{k}(\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l})\big)\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|\\
&+C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{[\partial^{k},\mathbf{T}^{l+1}](\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l})\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|.\end{split}

By a further calculation, we have

\begin{split}V^{A1}_{2}\leq&V^{A11}_{2}+V^{A12}_{2}+V^{A13}_{2}+V^{A14}_{2},\end{split}

(9.61)

where we set

\begin{split}V^{A11}_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{T}^{l+1}\left\{\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\big(\partial^{k}(\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l})\big)\partial_{j}h^{l+1}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|,\\
V^{A12}_{2}=&C\big|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\big(\partial^{k}(\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l+1}-\partial^{j}{\rho}^{l})\big)\mathbf{T}^{l+1}(\partial_{j}h^{l+1})\right\}\\
&\qquad\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x}\left\{\partial_{k}(\Delta h^{l+1}-\Delta h^{l}-\partial^{i}h^{l+1}\partial_{i}\rho^{l+1}-\partial^{i}h^{l}\partial_{i}\rho^{l})\right\}dxd\tau\big|,\\
