\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}(df_{m+1}-df_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T^{*}_{N_{0}}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}k}2^{-3\delta_{1}m}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+E^{3}(0))\times\{2^{\delta_{1}(m+1-N_{0})}\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}k}2^{-2\delta_{1}m}2^{-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}N_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+E^{3}(0)),\quad k<j.\end{split}

(10.195)

By phase decomposition, we have

df_{j}=P_{\geq j}df_{j}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=k}P_{k}(df_{m+1}-df_{m})+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}P_{k}df_{k}.

By using (
10.194
) and (
10.195
), we get

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T^{*}_{N_{0}}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+E^{3}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\delta_{1}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(10.196)

Using (
10.193
) and (
10.196
), we get

\begin{split}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}_{N_{0}}]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|df_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}_{N_{0}}]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&A_{*}\exp(B_{*}\mathrm{e}^{B_{*}}),\end{split}

(10.197)

where
A_{*}=C\textrm{e}^{C_{*}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}+C_{*})
,
B_{*}=C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+E^{3}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\delta_{1}})]^{-2}
and
C_{*}
is stated in (
10.59
). Therefore, the conclusion (
10.171
) hold.
∎

11.
Proof of Theorem
1.5

In this part, our goal is to prove Theorem
1.5
. We first reduce the proof to the case of initial data with frequency support, that is, using Proposition
11.1
to prove Theorem
1.5
. Then we use Proposition
4.2
to obtain Proposition
10.2
. Finally, we use Proposition
10.2
and develop a method to prove Proposition
10.1
.

11.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.5

To prove Theorem
1.5
, we will prove the well-posedness of solutions as a limit of a sequence. To find this sequence, let us introduce a sequence of initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},h_{0j})
with frequency support such that

(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},h_{0j})=(P_{\leq j}\mathbf{v}_{0},P_{\leq j}\rho_{0},P_{\leq j}h_{0}).

(11.1)

Above, the data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0})
is stated as (
1.37
) in Theorem
1.5
. Following (
1.5
), we define

\mathbf{w}_{0j}=\mathrm{e}^{-\rho_{0j}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}_{0j}.

(11.2)

Set

\mathbb{E}(0)=\|\mathbf{v}_{0j}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\rho_{0j}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0j}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|h_{0j}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}.

(11.3)

Using (
1.37
) and (
11.1
) and (
11.2
), we therefore get

\mathbb{E}(0)\leq C(\bar{M}_{0}+\bar{M}^{2}_{0}).

(11.4)

Before we give a proof of Theorem
1.5
, let us now introduce Proposition
11.1
.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
\epsilon
and (
1.27
)-(
1.37
) be stated in Theorem
1.5
. Let
(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},h_{0j},\mathbf{w}_{0j})
be stated in (
11.1
) and (
11.2
). For each
j\geq 1
, consider Cauchy problem (
1.8
) with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},h_{0j},\mathbf{w}_{0j})
. Then for all
j\geq 1
, there exists two positive constants
\bar{T}>0
and
\bar{M}_{1}>0
(
\bar{T}
and
\bar{M}_{1}
only depending on
\bar{M}_{0}
and
\epsilon
) such that (
1.8
) has a unique solution
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j})\in C([0,\bar{T}],H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}})
,
h_{j}\in C([0,\bar{T}],H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon})
, and
\mathbf{w}_{j}\in C([0,\bar{T}],H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon})
. To be precise,

\mathrm{(1)}
the solution
\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j}
and
\mathbf{w}_{j}
satisfy the energy estimates

\|\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|h_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\leq\bar{M}_{1},

(11.5)

and

\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\partial_{t}\rho_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\partial_{t}h_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\leq\bar{M}_{1},

(11.6)

\mathrm{(2)}
the solution
\mathbf{v}_{j}
,
\rho_{j}
, and
h_{j}
satisfy the Strichartz estimate

\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,\bar{T}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq\bar{M}_{1}.

(11.7)

\mathrm{(3)}
for
\frac{7}{6}\leq r<\frac{7}{2}
, consider the following linear wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{j}}f_{j}=0,\qquad[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f_{j},\partial_{t}f_{j})|_{t=0}=(f_{0j},f_{1j}),\end{cases}

(11.8)

where
(f_{0j},f_{1j})=(P_{\leq j}f_{0},P_{\leq j}f_{1})
and
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H^{r-1}_{x}
. Then there is a unique solution
f_{j}
on
[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Moreover, for
a\leq r-\frac{7}{6}
, we have

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}d{f}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,\bar{T}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\bar{M}_{3}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}),\\
&\|{f}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq\bar{M}_{3}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}),\end{split}

(11.9)

where
{\bar{M}_{3}}
is a universal constant depending on
C_{0},c_{0},\bar{M}_{0}
and
\epsilon
.

Based on Proposition
11.1
, we are now ready to prove Theorem
1.5
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
by using Proposition
11.1
.

Using (
11.5
)-(
11.6
), there is a limit for a subsequence of
\{(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j})\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
, i.e.

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(\mathbf{v}_{j_{n}},\rho_{j_{n}},h_{j_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{j_{n}})=(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})\quad\text{in}\ H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}\times H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}\times H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}\times H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}.

Thus,
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
is a solution to (
1.8
)-(
1.3
) and

(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})|_{t=0}=(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}),

Moreover, it satisfies

\|\mathbf{v},\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}}+\|\mathbf{v},\rho,h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\leq\bar{M}_{1},

and

\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\partial_{t}\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}+\|\partial_{t}h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}}\leq\bar{M}_{1},

and

\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{2}_{[0,\bar{T}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq\bar{M}_{1}.

Similarly, there is a limit for a subsequence of
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(f_{j_{n}},\partial_{t}f_{j_{n}})=(f,f_{t})\quad\text{in}\ H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}.

Using (
11.8
) and (
11.9
) and taking
j\rightarrow\infty
, the limit
f
is a solution of the following Cauchy problem

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}}f=0,\qquad[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=0}=(f_{0},f_{1}).\end{cases}

Furthermore, by (
11.9
),
f
has the following estimates

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,\bar{T}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{\bar{M}_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}),\\
&\|{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C_{\bar{M}_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

Using a similar idea in Section
9
, we can also prove the continuous dependence. So we have finished the proof of Theorem
1.5
.
∎

It remains for us to prove Proposition
11.1
. Our idea is to prove Proposition
11.1
via small solutions, and using scaling technique and Strichartz estimates to obtain the solutions satisfying (
11.5
)-(
11.7
). We next turn to introduce Proposition
11.2
, which is about small solutions.

11.2.
Proposition
11.2
for small data

Our object is to prove Proposition
11.1
via small solutions, and using scaling technique and Strichartz estimates to obtain the solutions satisfying (
11.5
)-(
11.7
). Let us now state Proposition
11.2
considering small data.

Proposition 11.2
.

Let
\epsilon_{2}
and
\epsilon_{3}
be stated in (
1.29
). Let
0<\epsilon<\frac{1}{9}
. For each small, smooth initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0},h_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
which satisfies

\begin{split}&\|(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0})\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{2+\epsilon}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{3+\epsilon}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\end{split}

(11.10)

there exists a unique smooth solution
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})
to (
1.13
) on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

\|(\mathbf{v},\rho)\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{2+\epsilon}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{3+\epsilon}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

(11.11)

Furthermore, the solution satisfies the properties

\mathrm{(1)}
dispersive estimate for
\mathbf{v}
,
\rho
, and
h

\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,dh\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{a}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\quad a\in[0,\frac{1}{2}).

(11.12)

\mathrm{(2)}
Let
f
satisfy equation (
1.35
). For each
1\leq r\leq\frac{5}{2}+1
, the Cauchy problem (
1.35
) is well-posed in
H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
, and the following estimate holds:

\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}},\quad k<r-1,

(11.13)

and the similar estimates hold if we replace
\left<\partial\right>^{k}
by
\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d
.

Proof of Proposition
11.2
.

Taking
s=\frac{5}{2},s_{0}=2+\epsilon
in Proposition
4.2
, then Proposition
11.2
holds.
∎

Based on Proposition
11.2
, we are now ready to give a proof of Proposition
11.1
.

11.3.
Proof of Proposition
11.1

Proof of Proposition
11.1
by using Proposition
11.2
.

In Proposition
11.2
, the corresponding statement is considering the small, supported data. While, the initial data is large in Proposition
11.1
. Firstly, using a scaling method, we can reduce the initial sequence
\{(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},\mathbf{w}_{0j},h_{0j})\}
to be small. Taking scaling

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}={\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(Tt,Tx),\quad\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}=\rho_{0j}(Tt,Tx),\quad\widetilde{h}_{0j}={h}_{0j}(Tt,Tx),\end{split}

and setting

\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}=\mathrm{e}^{-\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},

we then have

\begin{split}&\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}\leq T\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}\leq T\cdot\mathbb{E}(0),\\
&\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}\leq T\|{\rho}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}\leq T\cdot\mathbb{E}(0),\\
&\|\widetilde{h}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{3+\epsilon}}\leq T^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\|{h}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{3+\epsilon}}\leq T^{\frac{3+\epsilon}{2}}2^{\frac{j}{2}}\cdot T^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\mathbb{E}(0),\\
&\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{2+\epsilon}}\leq T^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{2+\epsilon}}\leq T^{\frac{3+\epsilon}{2}}2^{\frac{j}{2}}\cdot T^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\mathbb{E}(0).\end{split}

(11.14)

Set

{T}_{j}:=2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}[\mathbb{E}(0)]^{-1}.

(11.15)

Hence, if we replace
T
with
T_{j}
in (
11.14
), then we have

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{3+\epsilon}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{2+\epsilon}}\leq 2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{2(3+\epsilon)}j}(1+\mathbb{E}(0)).\end{split}

By using (
1.27
) and (
11.1
), we also have

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},\widetilde{\rho}_{0j},\widetilde{h}_{0j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},{\rho}_{0j},{h}_{0j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{0}.

(11.16)

Here, for a small parameter
\epsilon_{3}
, we can choose
{N}_{1}=N_{1}(\epsilon,\bar{M}_{0})
such that

\begin{split}2^{-\frac{\epsilon N_{1}}{2(3+\epsilon)}}(1+\mathbb{E}(0))\ll\epsilon_{3},\\
(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}_{*})2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}N_{1}}(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))^{-1}\leq 1.\end{split}

(11.17)

Above,
\mathbb{E}_{*}
is denoted by

\mathbb{E}_{*}=C(\mathbb{E}(0)+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\exp\{C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}\}.

(11.18)

As a result, for
j\geq{N}_{1}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{3+\epsilon}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}\|_{\dot{H}^{2+\epsilon}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(11.19)

For (
11.19
) is about the homogeneous norm, we need to use physical localization to get the same bound for inhomogeneous norm. Utilize the similar standard physical localization(page 44, Step 2). For the propagation speed of (
1.13
) is finite, we set
c
be the largest speed of (
1.13
). Set
\chi
be a smooth function supported in
B(0,c+2)
, and which equals
1
in
B(0,c+1)
. For given
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we define the localized initial data for the velocity and density near
\mathbf{y}
:

\begin{split}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{x})=&\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{x})-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})\right),\\
\bar{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{x})=&\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{x})-\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})\right),\\
\bar{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{x})=&\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{x})-\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})).\end{split}

(11.20)

Following (
1.5
), we shall set

\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}=\mathrm{e}^{-\bar{\rho}_{0j}}\mathrm{curl}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}.

(11.21)

Based on (
11.20
), (
11.21
) and (
11.19
), it follows that

\begin{split}\|\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}\|_{{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\bar{\rho}_{0j}\|_{{H}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\bar{h}_{0j}\|_{{H}^{3+\epsilon}}+\|\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}\|_{{H}^{2+\epsilon}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(11.22)

For each fixed
j
, using Proposition
11.2
, there is a smooth solution
(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\bar{\rho}_{j},\bar{h}_{j},\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

\begin{cases}\square_{\widetilde{g}_{j}}v^{i}=-\mathrm{e}^{\rho+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y)}\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}^{i}+\widetilde{Q}^{i},\\
\square_{\widetilde{g}_{j}}{\rho}=-\frac{1}{\gamma}\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\Delta h+\widetilde{D},\\
\square_{\widetilde{g}_{j}}h=\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\Delta h+\widetilde{E},\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}h=0,\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}w^{i}=w^{a}\partial_{a}v^{i}+\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\mathrm{e}^{(h+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(y))+(\gamma-2)(\rho+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y))}\epsilon^{iab}\partial_{a}\rho\partial_{b}h,\\
(\mathbf{v},\rho,h,\mathbf{w})|_{t=0}=(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},\bar{\rho}_{0j},\bar{h}_{0j},\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}).\end{cases}

(11.23)

Above, the quantities
\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}
and
\widetilde{{g}}
are given by

\begin{split}&\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}=\frac{dp}{d{\rho}}({\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y),{h}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(y)),\\
&\widetilde{g}_{j}=g({\mathbf{v}}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(y),{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y),{h}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(y)),\\
&\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}=\partial_{t}+({\mathbf{v}}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(y))\cdot\nabla,\end{split}

(11.24)

and

\begin{split}\widetilde{Q}^{i}=&\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\rho\partial_{\beta}h+\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{1j}\partial_{\alpha}\rho\partial_{\beta}v^{j}+\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{2j}\partial_{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}v^{j},\\
\widetilde{D}=&\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\rho\partial_{\beta}h+\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j}\partial_{\alpha}\rho\partial_{\beta}v^{j}+\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}\partial_{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}v^{j}\\
\widetilde{E}=&\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\rho\partial_{\beta}h+\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j}\partial_{\alpha}\rho\partial_{\beta}v^{j}+\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}\partial_{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}v^{j},\end{split}

and
\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta},\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{1j},\widetilde{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{2j},\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta},\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},\widetilde{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j},\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta},\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},\widetilde{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}
have the same forms with
{Q}^{i\alpha\beta},{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{1j},{Q}^{i\alpha\beta}_{2j}
,
{D}^{\alpha\beta},{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},{D}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j},{E}^{\alpha\beta},{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{1j},{E}^{\alpha\beta}_{2j}
by replacing
({\mathbf{v}},{\rho},{h})
to
({\mathbf{v}}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(y),{\rho}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y),{h}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(y))
.
Seeing (
1.11
) and (
1.12
), let us set

\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{+j}=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}-\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{-j},\quad\Delta\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{-j}=-\mathrm{e}^{\bar{\rho}_{j}}\mathrm{curl}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j}.

Using Proposition
11.2
again, we could find

\|\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\bar{\rho}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{2+\epsilon}}+\|\bar{h}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{3+\epsilon}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(11.25)

and

\|d\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j},d\bar{\rho}_{j},d\bar{h}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{a}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\quad a\in[0,\frac{1}{2}).

(11.26)

Furthermore, the linear equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}_{j}}f=0,\qquad[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&{f}(t_{0},x)={f}_{0},\ \partial_{t}{f}(t_{0},x)={f}_{1},\quad t_{0}\in[-2,2],\end{cases}

(11.27)

admits a solution
{f}\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-2,2],H_{x}^{r-1})
. Moreover, for
k<r-1
, we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k}{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}),\end{split}

(11.28)

and the similar estimates hold when we replace
\left<\partial\right>^{k}
by
\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d
. Here
\widetilde{g}=g(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(y),\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y),\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(y))
(citing (
11.24
)). Note (
11.23
). Then the function
(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
is also a solution of the following system

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{g}}(\bar{v}^{i}_{j}+\widetilde{v}^{i}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))=-\mathrm{e}^{\rho+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y)}\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\mathrm{curl}(\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})}\mathbf{w}^{i})+\widetilde{Q}^{i},\\
&\square_{\widetilde{g}}(\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))=-\frac{1}{\gamma}\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\Delta(\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))+\widetilde{D},\\
&\square_{\widetilde{g}}(\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))=\widetilde{c}_{s}^{2}\Delta(\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))+\widetilde{E},\\
&\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}(\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))=0,\\
&\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}(\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})}w^{i})=\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})\widetilde{w}^{a}\partial_{a}(\bar{v}^{i}_{j}+\widetilde{v}^{i}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\mathrm{e}^{(h+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(y))+(\gamma-2)(\rho+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(y))}\epsilon^{iab}\partial_{a}(\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))\partial_{b}(\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})),\\
&(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j}))|_{t=0}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},\widetilde{\rho}_{0j},\widetilde{h}_{0j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0j})\ \text{in}\ B(\mathbf{y},c+1).\end{cases}

(11.29)

Consider the restrictions, for
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\left(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})\right)|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\quad(\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\\
\quad(\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}))|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},\\
\quad\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j}|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},

(11.30)

where
\mathrm{K}^{y}=\left\{(t,\mathbf{x}):ct+|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|\leq c+1,|t|<1\right\}
, then the restrictions (
11.29
) solve (
1.1
)-(
1.3
) on
\mathrm{K}^{y}
. By finite speed of propagation, a smooth solution
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j},\widetilde{h}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
solves (
1.13
) in
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j},\widetilde{h}_{j}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}
is denoted by

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})\ &=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\ \ \ (t,\mathbf{x})\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\widetilde{\rho}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})\ &=\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\ \ \ (t,\mathbf{x})\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\widetilde{h}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})\ &=\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y}),\ \ \ (t,\mathbf{x})\in\mathrm{K}^{y},\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j},\ \ \qquad\qquad(t,x)\in\mathrm{K}^{y}.\end{split}

(11.31)

Therefore, if we set

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\textstyle{\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})),\\
\widetilde{\rho}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\textstyle{\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\bar{\rho}_{j}+\widetilde{\rho}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})),\\
\widetilde{h}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\textstyle{\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})(\bar{h}_{j}+\widetilde{h}_{0j}(\mathbf{y})),\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}(t,\mathbf{x})&=\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{j}}\mathrm{curl}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\end{split}

(11.32)

then
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j},\widetilde{h}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
is a smooth solution of (
1.13
) on
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
with the initial data
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j},\widetilde{h}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})|_{t=0}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},\widetilde{\rho}_{0j},\widetilde{h}_{0j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0j})
, where
\psi
is supported in the unit ball such that

\textstyle{\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}}\psi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})=1.

Therefore, the function
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j},\widetilde{h}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
defined in (
11.32
) is the solution of (
1.13
) according to the uniqueness of solutions, i.e. Corollary
3.6
. By (
11.25
) and (
11.26
), we can obtain that
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j},\widetilde{h}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
satisfies (
11.10
) and (
11.11
).

On the other hand, we recall that the initial data
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},\widetilde{\rho}_{0j},\widetilde{h}_{0j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0j})
is a scaling of
(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},h_{0j},\mathbf{w}_{0j})
. As a scaling-invariant system (
1.13
), then

\begin{split}({\mathbf{v}}_{j},{\rho}_{j},{h}_{j},{\mathbf{w}}_{j})=(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j},\widetilde{h}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})((T_{j})^{-1}t,(T_{j})^{-1}x))\end{split}

is also a solution of (
1.13
) on the time interval
[0,{T}_{j}]
(
{T}_{j}
is stated in (
11.15
)). Moreover, it matches the initial data

({\mathbf{v}}_{j},{\rho}_{j},{h}_{j},{\mathbf{w}}_{j})|_{t=0}=({\mathbf{v}}_{0j},{\rho}_{0j},{h}_{0j},{\mathbf{w}}_{0j}).

We still expect the behaviour of a linear wave equation endowed with
g_{j}=g(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j})
. So let us consider the following linear wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{j}}f=0,\quad[0,T_{j}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f,f_{t})|_{t=0}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H^{r-1}_{x},\quad 1\leq r\leq\frac{5}{2}+1.\end{cases}

(11.33)

Following the proof of (
4.50
) for System (
4.43
), then we can conclude that

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{k-1}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&CT_{j}^{r-1-k}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}})\\
\leq&C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(11.34)

Above, we also use
k<r-1
and
T_{j}=2^{-\frac{j}{3+\epsilon}}
. Following the proof of (
4.53
) for System (
4.43
), then we obtain

\begin{split}\|{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{j}]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{j}]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(11.35)

From (
11.16
), (
11.25
), and (
11.26
), we shall prove

\begin{split}&\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{a}}+\|d\widetilde{\rho}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{a}}+\|d\widetilde{h}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{a}}\\
\leq&C(\|d\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j},d\bar{\rho}_{j},d\bar{h}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{a}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},\widetilde{\rho}_{0j},\widetilde{h}_{0j}\|_{L^{\infty}})\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}(0)),\qquad a\in[0,\frac{1}{2}).\end{split}

(11.36)

and

\begin{split}&\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\rho}_{j})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+}}\\
\leq&C(\|(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\bar{\rho}_{j})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\|\bar{h}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+}}+\|\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0j},\widetilde{\rho}_{0j},\widetilde{h}_{0j}\|_{L^{\infty}})\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}(0)).\end{split}

(11.37)

By using (
11.36
) and by changing of coordinates
(t,\mathbf{x})\rightarrow((T_{j})^{-1}t,(T_{j})^{-1}\mathbf{x})
, we have

\begin{split}\|d{\mathbf{v}}_{j},d{\rho}_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},d\widetilde{\rho}_{j},d\widetilde{h}_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},d\widetilde{\rho}_{j},d\widetilde{h}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
=&C(1+\mathbb{E}(0)).\end{split}

(11.38)

By using Theorem
3.5
and (
11.38
), we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}({T}_{j})=&\|{\mathbf{v}}_{j},{\rho}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{j}]}H^{\frac{5}{2}}_{x}}+\|{h}_{j},\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{j}]}H^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}_{x}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{j}]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(\mathbb{E}(0)+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\mathrm{e}^{C(1+\mathbb{E}(0))e^{C(1+\mathbb{E}(0))}}.\end{split}

(11.39)

The estimates (
11.38
) and (
11.39
) are about the solutions of (
1.8
).
In (
11.39
) and (
11.38
),
{T}_{j}
depends on
j
. So our next goal is to find a uniform time-interval and also the energy is uniform bounded.

11.3.1.
Strichartz estimates on a short time-interval

Considering
{T}_{j}=2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
, then we disscuss some good propertities of the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
in high frequency and low frequency.

Case 1: High frequency
. By space-time scaling
(t,\mathbf{x})\rightarrow((T_{j})^{-1}t,(T_{j})^{-1}\mathbf{x})
, we get

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}C^{a}_{x}}\leq\{T_{j}\}^{-(\frac{1}{2}+a)}\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},d\widetilde{\rho}_{j},d\widetilde{h}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}C^{a}_{x}},\quad a\in[0,\frac{1}{2}).\end{split}

(11.40)

Using (
11.36
) again, (
11.40
) yields

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}C^{a}_{x}}\leq C\{T_{j}\}^{-(\frac{1}{2}+a)}(1+\mathbb{E}(0)).\end{split}

(11.41)

Using Hölder’s inequality, it follows

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}C^{a}_{x}}\leq(T_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}C^{a}_{x}}\leq C\{T_{j}\}^{-a}(1+\mathbb{E}(0)).\end{split}

(11.42)

For
k\geq j
, by Bernstein inequality and (
11.41
)-(
11.42
), we have

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&2^{-ka}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}C^{a}_{x}}\\
\leq&C2^{-ka}\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}C^{a}_{x}}\\
\leq&C2^{-ka}\{T_{j}\}^{-(\frac{1}{2}+a)}(1+\mathbb{E}(0)),\end{split}

(11.43)

and

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C2^{-ka}\{T_{j}\}^{-a}(1+\mathbb{E}(0)).\end{split}

(11.44)

Taking
a=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{40}
in (
11.43
)-(
11.44
), and using (
11.15
), so we obtain

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C2^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{40})k}[2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}E(0)]^{1-\frac{\epsilon}{40}}(1+\mathbb{E}(0))\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{20})j}2^{\frac{1-\frac{\epsilon}{40}}{3+\epsilon}j}(1+\mathbb{E}^{3}(0))\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{3}(0))\cdot 2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j}2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}j},\end{split}

(11.45)

and

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\cdot 2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j},\quad k\geq j.\end{split}

(11.46)

Summing (
11.46
) over
k
from
j
to infinity, we get

\begin{split}\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\cdot(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})^{-1}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}.\end{split}

(11.47)

Case 2: Low frequency
. For
k<j
, we decompose

\begin{split}P_{k}d{\mathbf{v}}_{j}=&P_{k}d{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{j}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{k})\\
=&P_{k}d{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\textstyle{\sum_{m=k}^{j-1}}P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}).\end{split}

(11.48)

Similarly, we have

\begin{split}P_{k}d{\rho}_{j}=&P_{k}d{\rho}_{k}+\textstyle{\sum_{m=k}^{j-1}}P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),\\
P_{k}d{h}_{j}=&P_{k}d{h}_{k}+\textstyle{\sum_{m=k}^{j-1}}P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m}).\end{split}

(11.49)

In (
11.48
)-(
11.49
),
P_{k}{\rho}_{k},P_{k}{h}_{k}
and
P_{k}{\mathbf{v}}_{k}
can be handled as (
11.43
) and (
11.44
).

For any fixed integer
m
, the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{m+1},{\rho}_{m+1},{h}_{m+1},\mathbf{w}_{m+1})

and
(\mathbf{v}_{m},{\rho}_{m},{h}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
exist on the same region
[0,{T}_{m+1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. By using (
11.33
) and (
11.34
), we can see

\begin{split}&\|d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m},d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m},d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\\
\leq&C\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-{\mathbf{v}}_{m},{\rho}_{m+1}-{\rho}_{m},,{h}_{m+1}-{h}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H_{x}^{2+\frac{1}{10}\epsilon}}\\
&+C(\|{\mathbf{w}}_{m+1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{10}\epsilon}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}_{m+1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{10}\epsilon}})\\
&+C\|{h}_{m+1}-{h}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{2}+\frac{1}{10}\epsilon}}.\end{split}

(11.50)

Now let us estimate the right hand side of (
11.50
). Note
(\mathbf{v}_{m+1},\rho_{m+1},h_{m+1})
and
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m})
is the solution of (
2.1
). Let
\mathbf{U}_{m}=(\mathbf{v}_{m},p(\rho_{m}),h_{m})^{\mathrm{T}}
. Then
\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m}
satisfies

\begin{cases}&A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{m+1})\partial_{t}(\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m})+A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{m+1})\partial_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m})=\Pi_{m},\\
&(\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m})|_{t=0}=\mathbf{U}_{0(m+1)}-\mathbf{U}_{0m},\end{cases}

(11.51)

where

\Pi_{m}=-[A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{m+1})-A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{m})]\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{m}-[A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{m+1})-A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{m})]\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}_{m}.

Multiplying
\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m}
on (
11.51
) and integrating it on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq&C\|(d\mathbf{U}_{m+1},d\mathbf{U}_{m})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(11.52)

Then Strichartz estimate (
11.38
) and Gronwall’s inequality tell us

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{U}_{m+1}-\mathbf{U}_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}\leq&C\|\mathbf{U}_{0(m+1)}-\mathbf{U}_{0m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\leq&C\|\mathbf{v}_{0(m+1)}-\mathbf{v}_{0m},\rho_{0(m+1)}-\rho_{0m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\leq&C\{2^{-\frac{5}{2}m}\mathbb{E}(0)\}^{2}.\end{split}

(11.53)

By Lemma
2.8
and (
11.53
), then we get

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m+1}-\rho_{m},h_{m+1}-h_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}\leq C\mathbb{E}(0)2^{-\frac{5}{2}m}.\end{split}

(11.54)

Similarly,
\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}
satisfies

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}(\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m})+(\mathbf{v}_{m+1}\cdot\nabla)(\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m})\\
=&-(\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-\mathbf{v}_{m})\cdot\nabla\mathbf{w}_{m}+(\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m})\cdot\nabla\mathbf{v}_{m+1}\\
&+\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\nabla(\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-\mathbf{v}_{m})+\mathbf{B}_{m+1}-\mathbf{B}_{m}.\end{split}

(11.55)

Above, we set
\mathbf{B}_{m}=(B^{1}_{m},B^{2}_{m},B^{3}_{m})
and
B^{i}_{m}=\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}\mathrm{e}^{h_{m}+(\gamma-2)\rho_{m}}\epsilon^{iab}\partial_{a}\rho_{m}\partial_{b}h_{m}
. So we have

\|\mathbf{B}_{m+1}-\mathbf{B}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\partial(\rho_{m+1}-\rho_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial h_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\partial(h_{m+1}-h_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial\rho_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.

(11.56)

Multiplying with
\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}
on (
11.55
), then integrating it on
[0,T_{m+1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and using (
11.56
), we can obtain

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\leq&C\|\mathbf{w}_{0(m+1)}-\mathbf{w}_{0m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+C\int^{T_{m+1}}_{0}\|\mathbf{B}_{m+1}-\mathbf{B}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{T_{m+1}}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}_{m+1},\partial\rho_{m+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{T_{m+1}}_{0}\|\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{T_{m+1}}_{0}\|\rho_{m+1}-\rho_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{T_{m+1}}_{0}\|\rho_{m+1}-\rho_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial(\rho_{m+1}-\rho_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{T_{m+1}}_{0}\|\partial(\rho_{m+1}-\rho_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial h_{m+1},\partial h_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{T_{m+1}}_{0}\|\partial(h_{m+1}-h_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial\rho_{m+1},\partial\rho_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(11.57)

Using (
11.38
), (
11.54
), (
11.56
), (
11.57
) and
T_{m+1}=2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(m+1)}
, and
\|\partial\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 2^{m}\mathbb{E}(0)
, we shall prove

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-(\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon)m}.\end{split}

(11.58)

On the other hand, we can derive

\partial_{t}(h_{m+1}-h_{m})+(\mathbf{v}_{m+1}\cdot\nabla)(h_{m+1}-h_{m})=-(\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-\mathbf{v}_{m})\cdot\nabla h_{m}.

(11.59)

By (
11.59
) and it’s energy estimates, we obtain

\begin{split}\|h_{m+1}-h_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}\leq&\|h_{0(m+1)}-h_{0m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+C\int^{{T}_{m+1}}_{0}\|\partial\mathbf{v}_{m+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|h_{m+1}-h_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{{T}_{m+1}}_{0}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-\mathbf{v}_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial h_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|h_{m+1}-h_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\end{split}

(11.60)

Using (
11.38
), (
11.54
), and
T_{m+1}=2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(m+1)}
, we shall obtain

\begin{split}\|h_{m+1}-h_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-(\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon)m},\end{split}

(11.61)

which is a stronger one than in (
11.54
) for
h_{m+1}-h_{m}
. Combing (
11.54
), (
11.58
), and (
11.61
), we have proved

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m+1}-\rho_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}&\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{5}{2}m},\\
\|h_{m+1}-h_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}&\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-(\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon)m}.\\
\|\mathbf{w}_{m+1}-\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}_{m+1}]}L^{2}_{x}}&\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-(\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon)m}.\end{split}

(11.62)

Therefore, by (
11.62
), (
11.50
) yields

\begin{split}&\|d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m},d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m},d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}m}2^{-\frac{8\epsilon}{10}m}.\end{split}

(11.63)

Therefore, for
k<j
and
k\leq m
, we get

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}2^{-\frac{7\epsilon}{10}m}.\end{split}

(11.64)

Then it follows

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}2^{-\frac{7\epsilon}{10}m}.\end{split}

(11.65)

11.3.2.
Extending the solution from short time-interval
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{N_{1}}]

Our goal is to extend the time interval
I_{1}=[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{N_{1}}]
based on (
11.45
), (
11.46
), (
11.64
), (
11.65
) and Theorem
3.5
. Here
T_{N_{1}}=[\mathbb{E}(0)]^{-1}2^{-\frac{N_{1}}{3+\epsilon}}
. To be simple, we first set

I_{1}=[0,T_{j}]=[t_{0},t_{1}],\quad\quad|I_{1}|=[\mathbb{E}(0)]^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}.

(11.66)

Recall (
11.46
) and (
11.65
), we can see

\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j},\quad k\geq j,

(11.67)

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}d({\rho}_{m+1}-{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}d({\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}d(h_{m+1}-h_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m},\quad k<j.\end{split}

(11.68)

By frequency decomposition, we get

\begin{split}d\mathbf{v}_{j}=&\textstyle{\sum}^{\infty}_{k=j}d\mathbf{v}_{j}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j}\\
=&P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-1}P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m})+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k}.\end{split}

(11.69)

Similarly, we also have

\begin{split}d\rho_{j}=&P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-1}P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m})+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}P_{k}d\rho_{k}.\end{split}

(11.70)

and

\begin{split}dh_{j}=&P_{\geq j}dh_{j}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-1}P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}P_{k}dh_{k}.\end{split}

(11.71)

Considering (
11.69
)-(
11.71
) and (
11.67
)-(
11.68
), we can see that each term in (
11.69
)-(
11.71
) is much different. For example, the component
P_{1}d\rho_{1}
in
\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}P_{k}d\rho_{k}
has time-interval
T_{1}=[\mathbb{E}(0)]^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}
, so we don’t need to extend it any more. But the term
d\rho_{j}
only exists on the time interval
[0,T_{j}]
at present. So we need to treat (
11.69
)-(
11.71
) in a precise way when we extend the time interval of
\rho_{j}
from
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{N_{1}}]
.

Step 1: Extending
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{j-1}]\ (j\geq N_{1}+1)
.
To start, referring (
10.55
), so we need to calculate
\mathbb{E}(T_{j})
for obtaining the length of a time-interval. Then we shall calculate
\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]L^{\infty}_{x}}}
. Using (
11.67
) and (
11.68
), we derive that

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},,P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-1}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{k}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-1}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}_{k=j}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

So we get

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.72)

By (
11.72
) and Theorem
3.5
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(T_{j})\leq C(\mathbb{E}(0)+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\exp\{C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}\}=\mathbb{E}_{*}.\end{split}

(11.73)

Above,
\mathbb{E}_{*}
is stated in (
11.18
). Starting at the time
T_{j}
, seeing (
11.15
) and (
11.73
), we shall get an extending time-interval of
(\mathbf{v},\rho_{j},h_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
with a length of
\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
. But, if
T^{*}_{j}+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}\geq T_{j-1}
, so we have finished this step. Else, we need to extend it again.

\mathit{Case1:T_{j}+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}\geq T_{j-1}}
, then we get a new interval

I_{2}=[T_{j},T_{j-1}],\quad|I_{2}|=(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}(0)^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}.

(11.74)

Moreover, referring (
11.67
) and (
11.68
), we deduce that

\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j},T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{2})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j},\quad k\geq j,

(11.75)

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{j}-d{\rho}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{j}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{j-1},P_{k}(d{h}_{j}-d{h}_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j},T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{2})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}(j-1)},\quad k\leq j-1.\end{split}

(11.76)

Using (
11.17
) and
j\geq N_{0}+1
, (
11.75
) and (
11.76
) yields

\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j},T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j},\quad k\geq j,

(11.77)

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{j}-d{\rho}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{j}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{j-1},P_{k}(d{h}_{j}-d{h}_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j},T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(j-1)},\quad k\leq j-1.\end{split}

(11.78)

Therefore, we could obtain

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
+&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{j}-d\mathbf{v}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d\rho_{j}-d\rho_{j-1}),P_{k}(dh_{j}-dh_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-2}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-2}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(11.79)

Due to (
11.67
) and (
11.77
), it yields

\begin{split}\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{\infty}_{k=j}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}(2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j}+2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j})\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{\infty}_{k=j}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times 2.\end{split}

(11.80)

Due to (
11.68
) and (
11.78
), it yields

\begin{split}&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{j}-d\mathbf{v}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d\rho_{j}-d\rho_{j-1}),P_{k}(dh_{j}-dh_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T^{*}_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}(2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}(j-1)}+2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(j-1)})\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(j-1)}\times 2.\end{split}

(11.81)

Inserting (
11.80
)-(
11.81
) into (
11.79
), we derive that

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}_{k=j}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times 2\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(j-1)}\times 2\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-2}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-2}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.82)

By (
11.82
) and Theorem
3.5
, we also prove

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(T_{j-1})\leq\mathbb{E}_{*}.\end{split}

(11.83)

\mathit{Case2:T_{j}+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}<T_{j-1}}
. In this situation, we will record

I_{2}=[T_{j},t_{2}],\quad|I_{2}|=\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}.

Referring (
11.75
) and (
11.76
), we also deduce that

\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j},T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{2})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j},\quad k\geq j,

(11.84)

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{j}-d{\rho}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{j}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{j-1},P_{k}(d{h}_{j}-d{h}_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j},T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{2})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}(j-1)},\quad k\leq j-1.\end{split}

(11.85)

Using (
11.17
) and
j\geq N_{1}+1
, (
11.84
) and (
11.85
) yields

\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j},\quad k\geq j,

(11.86)

and

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{j}-d{\rho}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{j}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(j-1)},\quad k<j.\end{split}

(11.87)

Similarly, we can also get

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T^{*}_{k}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
+&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{j}-d\mathbf{v}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d\rho_{j}-d\rho_{j-1}),P_{k}(dh_{j}-dh_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-2}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-2}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

Noting
I_{1}\cup I_{2}\subseteq T_{k}
when
k\leq j-1
, then it follows that

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
+&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{j}-d\mathbf{v}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d\rho_{j}-d\rho_{j-1}),P_{k}(dh_{j}-dh_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-2}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-2}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\end{split}

Inserting (
11.67
), (
11.68
) and (
11.86
) and (
11.87
), we have

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{I_{1}\cup I_{2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}_{k=j}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times 2\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(j-1)}\times 2\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-2}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-2}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(m+1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}(m+1)}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.88)

By (
11.88
) and Theorem
3.5
, we also prove

\begin{split}E(t_{2})\leq\mathbb{E}_{*}.\end{split}

(11.89)

Therefore, we can repeat the process with a length with
\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
till extending it to
T_{j-1}
. Moreover, on every new time-interval with
\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
(
11.67
) and (
11.68
) hold. Set

Y_{1}=\frac{T_{j-1}-T_{j}}{\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}}=(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}^{-1}(0)\mathbb{E}_{*}.

(11.90)

So we need a maximum of
X_{1}
-times to reach the time
T_{j-1}
both in case 2(it’s also adapt to case 1 for calculating the times). As a result, we shall calculate

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{k}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
+&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{j}-d\mathbf{v}_{j-1}),P_{k}(d\rho_{j}-d\rho_{j-1}),P_{k}(dh_{j}-dh_{j-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-2}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-2}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\end{split}

Due to (
11.67
), (
11.68
) and (
11.86
) and (
11.87
), and (
11.17
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}_{k=j}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times\{(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}^{-1}(0)\mathbb{E}_{*}+1\}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(j-1)}\times\{(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}^{-1}(0)\mathbb{E}_{*}+1\}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}_{j-1}^{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-2}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-2}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.91)

Therefore, by (
11.91
) and Theorem
3.5
, we can see that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(T_{j-1})\leq\mathbb{E}_{*}.\end{split}

(11.92)

At this moment, both in case 1 or case 2, seeing from (
11.91
), (
11.92
), (
11.82
), (
11.83
), through a maximum of
X_{1}=(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}^{-1}(0)\mathbb{E}_{*}
times with each length
\mathbb{E}_{*}^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
or
(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}(0)^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
, we shall extend the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
from
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,,T_{j-1}]
, and

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(T_{j-1})\leq&\mathbb{E}_{*},\\
\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.93)

As a result, we also have extended the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
(
m\in[N_{0},j-1]
) from
[0,T_{m}]
to
[0,T_{m-1}]
. Moreover, referring (
11.67
) and (
11.68
), (
11.75
), (
11.76
), and (
11.90
), we get

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{m},P_{k}d\rho_{m},P_{k}dh_{m}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{m},P_{k}d\rho_{m},P_{k}dh_{m}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{m},P_{k}d\rho_{m},P_{k}dh_{m}\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{m},T_{m-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}\\
&\ \ +C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}_{*})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}j}\times(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}^{-1}(0)\mathbb{E}_{*}.\end{split}

(11.94)

Using (
11.17
), for
k\geq m
and
m\geq N_{0}+1
, (
11.94
) yields

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{m},P_{k}d\rho_{m},P_{k}dh_{m}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}.\end{split}

(11.95)

Similarly, if
m\geq N_{0}+1
, the following estimate

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m}-d{\rho}_{m-1}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m-1}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m}-d{h}_{m-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m}-d{\rho}_{m-1}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m-1}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m}-d{h}_{m-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m}-d{\rho}_{m-1}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m-1}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m}-d{h}_{m-1})\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{m},T_{m-1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(m-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}(m-1)}\\
&\ \ +C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}_{*})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(m-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}(m-1)}\times(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\mathbb{E}^{-1}(0)\mathbb{E}_{*},\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(m-1)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}(m-1)}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}},\quad k<j,\end{split}

(11.96)

hold. Above, we also use the condition (
11.17
).

Step 2: Extending time interval
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{N_{1}}]
. Based the above analysis in Step 1, we can give a induction to achieve our goal. We assume the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
can be extended from
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{j-l}]
through a maximam
Y_{l}
times and

\begin{split}Y_{l}=&\frac{T_{j-l}-T_{j}}{\mathbb{E}^{-1}_{*}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}}\\
=&\frac{\mathbb{E}(0)^{-1}(2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-l)}-2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j})}{\mathbb{E}^{-1}_{*}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}}\\
=&\frac{\mathbb{E}_{*}}{\mathbb{E}(0)}(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l}-1).\end{split}

(11.97)

Moreover, the following bounds

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-l}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l},\qquad k\geq m,\end{split}

(11.98)

and

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-l}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l},\quad k<j,\end{split}

(11.99)

and

\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-l}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\delta_{\epsilon}}{40}})]^{-2},

(11.100)

and

\mathbb{E}(T_{j-l})\leq\mathbb{E}_{*}.

(11.101)

In our step 1, seeing from (
11.90
), (
11.93
), (
11.95
), and (
11.96
), then (
11.97
)-(
11.101
) hold when
l=1
. Next, we will prove (
11.97
)-(
11.101
) if we replace
l
to
l+1
. If we want to extend the solution from
[0,T_{j-l}]
to
[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]
. As a result, we have

j-l\geq N_{1}+1.

(11.102)

Else, the solution
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
has existed on
[0,T_{N_{1}}]
. so we don’t need to extend it if
j-l\leq N_{1}
. In the following, we are extending the solution starting at the time
T_{j-l}
based on assumptions (
11.97
)-(
11.101
).

Seeing (
11.15
) and (
11.101
), we shall get an extending time-interval of
(\mathbf{v},\rho_{j},h_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
with a length of
(\mathbb{E}_{*})^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
. So we can go to the case 2 in step 1, and the length every new time-interval is
(\mathbb{E}_{*})^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}
. Therefore, we need to extend it with
Y
-times to the time-interval
[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]
, where

Y=\frac{T_{j-(l+1)}-T_{j-l}}{(\mathbb{E}_{*})^{-1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}}=2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l}(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\frac{\mathbb{E}_{*}}{\mathbb{E}(0)}.

(11.103)

As a result, we extend the solutions with
Y_{l}+Y
-times from
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]
. Calculate

Y_{l}+Y=(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}-1)\frac{\mathbb{E}_{*}}{\mathbb{E}(0)}.

Then we shall set

Y_{l+1}=(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}-1)\frac{\mathbb{E}_{*}}{\mathbb{E}(0)}.

(11.104)

Moreover, for
k\geq j
, we have

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-l}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j-l},T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(11.105)

Using (
11.84
) and (
11.85
)

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[T^{*}_{j-l},T^{*}_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{2})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l}(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\frac{\mathbb{E}_{*}}{\mathbb{E}(0)},\quad k\geq j.\end{split}

(11.106)

Due to (
11.17
), we can see

\begin{split}(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))(1+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{2})\frac{\mathbb{E}_{*}}{\mathbb{E}(0)}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}N_{0}}\leq 1.\end{split}

Hence (
11.106
) yields

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{j-l},T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l}(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1),\quad k\geq j.\end{split}

(11.107)

Using (
11.98
) and (
11.98
), for
k\geq j
, we get

\begin{split}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{k}d\rho_{j},P_{k}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}.\end{split}

(11.108)

On the other hand, if
k<j
, then we have

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-l}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{m-l},T_{m-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(11.109)

When we extend the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
from
[0,T_{j-l}]
to
[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]
, then the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\rho_{m},h_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
is also extended from
[0,T_{m-l}]
to
[0,T_{m-(l+1)}]
. Seeing (
11.85
), (
11.96
) and (
11.101
), we can obtain

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{m-l},T_{m-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}_{*}^{2})2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l}(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1)\frac{\mathbb{E}_{*}}{\mathbb{E}(0)}.\end{split}

(11.110)

Using (
11.17
) again, then (
11.110
) becomes to

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[T_{m-l},T_{m-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}l}(2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}-1).\end{split}

(11.111)

Combing (
11.99
) and (
11.111
), it follows

\begin{split}&\|P_{k}(d{\rho}_{m+1}-d{\rho}_{m}),P_{k}(d{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-d{\mathbf{v}}_{m}),P_{k}(d{h}_{m+1}-d{h}_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}.\end{split}

(11.112)

We are now ready to bound the following Strichartz estimate

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|P_{\geq j}\mathbf{v}\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}\partial\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}\partial h_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=j-l}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=k}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
=&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T^{*}_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=j-l}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+1)}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=j-(l+1)}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\\
&+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}_{m=k}^{j-(l+2)}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
=&\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}+\Omega_{3}+\Omega_{4}+\Omega_{5},\end{split}

(11.113)

where

\begin{split}\Omega_{1}=&\|P_{\geq j}d\mathbf{v}_{j},P_{\geq j}d\rho_{j},P_{\geq j}dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}},\\
\Omega_{2}=&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{m=k}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}},\\
\Omega_{3}=&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=j-(l+1)}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}},\\
\Omega_{4}=&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+1)}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}},\\
\Omega_{5}=&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=j-l}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k},P_{k}dh_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(11.114)

To get the estimate on time-interval
[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]
for
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},\Omega_{3},\Omega_{4}
and
\Omega_{5}
, we should note that
there is no growth for
\Omega_{2}
and
\Omega_{4}
in this extending process. For example, considering
\Omega_{2}
, the existing time-interval of
P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m})
is actually
[0,T_{m+1}]
, and
[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]\subseteq[0,T_{m+1}]
if
m\geq j-(l+2)
. So we can use the initial bounds (
11.67
) and (
11.68
) to handle
\Omega_{2}
and
\Omega_{4}
. While, considering
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{3}
, and
\Omega_{5}
, we need to calculate the growth in the Strichartz estimate. Based on this idea, let us give a precise analysis on (
11.114
).

According to (
11.108
), we can estimate
\Omega_{1}
as

\begin{split}\Omega_{1}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{\infty}_{k=j}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}.\end{split}

(11.115)

Due to (
11.68
), we have

\begin{split}\Omega_{2}\leq&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{m=k}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}},\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{m=k}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}.\end{split}

(11.116)

Using (
11.112
), it follows

\begin{split}\Omega_{3}\leq&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=j-(l+1)}\|P_{k}(d\mathbf{v}_{m+1}-d\mathbf{v}_{m}),P_{k}(d\rho_{m+1}-d\rho_{m}),P_{k}(dh_{m+1}-dh_{m})\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=j-(l+1)}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}.\end{split}

(11.117)

Above, we use
k\leq j-1<j
and
m\leq j-1<j
. Due to (
11.67
), we can see

\begin{split}\Omega_{4}\leq&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+1)}_{k=1}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{k}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+1)}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}.\end{split}

(11.118)

Noting
k\leq j-1<j
and using (
10.152
) again, we can estimate

\begin{split}\Omega_{5}\leq&\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=j-l}\|P_{k}d\mathbf{v}_{k},P_{k}d\rho_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=j-l}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}k}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}[(k+l+1)-j]}.\end{split}

(11.119)

Inserting (
11.115
)-(
11.119
) to (
11.113
), it follows

\begin{split}&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{\infty}_{k=j}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}j}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+2)}_{m=k}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}m}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=j-(l+1)}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}m}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(l+1)}\\
&+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-(l+1)}_{k=1}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{10}k}\\
&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=j-l}2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}k}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{40}k}\times 2^{\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}[(k+l+1)-j]}\end{split}

(11.120)

In the case of
j-(l+1)\geq N_{1}
and
j\geq N_{1}+1
, (
10.164
) yields

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{j-(l+1)}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})^{-2}\big\{2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}(j-(l+1))}+2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}\\
&\quad+2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}j}+2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}}+2^{-\frac{1}{3+\epsilon}[j-(l+1)]}\big\}\\
\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.121)

Due to Theorem
3.5
and (
11.121
), we can achieve

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(T_{j-(l+1)})\leq\mathbb{E}_{*}.\end{split}

(11.122)

Gathering (
11.104
), (
11.108
), (
11.112
), (
11.121
) and (
11.122
), we know that (
11.97
)-(
11.101
) holding for
l+1
. So our induction hold (
11.97
)-(
11.101
) for
l=1
to
l=j-N_{1}
. Therefore, we can extend the solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},\mathbf{w}_{j})
from
[0,T_{j}]
to
[0,T_{N_{1}}]
when
j\geq N_{1}
. Moreover, setting
l=j-N_{1}
in (
11.97
)-(
11.101
), we have

\begin{split}&\bar{T}=T_{N_{1}}=\mathbb{E}^{-1}(0)2^{-\frac{N_{1}}{3+\epsilon}},\qquad\mathbb{E}(T)\leq\mathbb{E}_{*},\\
&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},dh_{j}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,\bar{T}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2},\end{split}

(11.123)

where
N_{1}
and
\mathbb{E}_{*}
only depend on
\epsilon
and
\bar{M}_{0}
, which is stated in (
11.17
) and (
11.18
).

By using (
11.123
) and Newton-Leibniz formula, we can see

\|\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\leq C_{0}+C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.

Also, using a similar way of calculating (
11.123
), we shall conclude

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j},d\rho_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,\bar{T}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{40}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.124)

∎

11.3.3.
Strichartz estimates of linear wave equation on time-interval
[0,T_{N_{1}}]

We still expect the behaviour of a linear wave equation endowed with
g_{j}=g(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},h_{j})
. So we claim a theorem as follows

Proposition 11.3
.

For
\frac{7}{6}\leq r<\frac{7}{2}
there is a solution
f_{j}
on
[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying the following linear wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{j}}f_{j}=0,\\
(f_{j},\partial_{t}f_{j})|_{t=0}=(f_{0j},f_{1j}),\end{cases}

(11.125)

where
(f_{0j},f_{1j})=(P_{\leq j}f_{0},P_{\leq j}f_{1})
, and
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H^{r-1}_{x}
, and
\bar{T}
is stated in (
11.123
). Moreover, for
a\leq r-\frac{7}{6}
, we have

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}d{f}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,\bar{T}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq{\bar{M}_{3}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}),\\
&\|{f}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq{\bar{M}_{3}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(11.126)

Proof of Proposition
11.3
.

Our proof also relies a Strichartz estimates on a short time-interval. Then a loss of derivatives are then obtained by summing up the short time estimates with respect to these time intervals.

Recall (
11.33
)-(
11.34
). For
1\leq r_{1}\leq\frac{5}{2}+1
, consider the homogeneous linear wave equation on short time interval

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{j}}F=0,\quad[0,T_{j}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(F,F_{t})|_{t=0}=(F_{0},F_{1})\in H_{x}^{r_{1}}\times H^{r_{1}-1}_{x}.\end{cases}

(11.127)

For
a_{1}<r_{1}-1
, then we can conclude that

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a_{1}-1}dF\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|{F}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r_{1}}}+\|{F}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r_{1}-1}}),\end{split}

(11.128)

and

\begin{split}\|{F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{j}]}H^{r_{1}}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{j}]}H^{r_{1}-1}_{x}}\leq C(\|{F}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r_{1}}}+\|{F}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r_{1}-1}}).\end{split}

(11.129)

Using (
11.125
), (
11.127
) and (
11.128
), we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1+\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{20}}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|f_{0j}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1j}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})\\
\leq&C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}),\end{split}

(11.130)

where we use
a\leq r-\frac{7}{6}
and

a+\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{20}\leq r-\frac{7}{6}+\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{20}=r-1-\frac{7\epsilon}{60(3+\epsilon)}<r-1.

(11.131)

Due to Bernstein inequality, for
k\geq j
, we shall obtain

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}=&C2^{-(\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{20})k}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1+\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{20}}P_{k}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{80}j}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1+\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{20}}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\end{split}

(11.132)

Combining (
11.130
) with (
11.132
), we get

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}j}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{80}j}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(11.133)

On the other hand, for any integer
m\geq 1
, we also have

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{m}}f_{m}=0,\quad[0,T_{m}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f_{m},\partial_{t}f_{m})|_{t=0}=(f_{0m},f_{1m}),\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{m+1}}f_{m+1}=0,\quad[0,T_{m+1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f_{m+1},\partial_{t}f_{m+1})|_{t=0}=(f_{0(m+1)},f_{1(m+1)}).\end{cases}

So the difference term
f_{m+1}-f_{m}
satisfies

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{m+1}}(f_{m+1}-f_{m})=({g}^{\alpha i}_{m+1}-{g}^{\alpha i}_{m})\partial_{\alpha i}f_{m},\quad[0,T^{*}_{m+1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f_{m+1}-f_{m},\partial_{t}(f_{m+1}-f_{m}))|_{t=0}=(f_{0(m+1)}-f_{0m},f_{1(m+1)}-f_{1m}).\end{cases}

(11.134)

Using (
11.128
), and Duhamel’s principle, and (
11.131
), we get

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}(df_{m+1}-df_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C\|f_{0(m+1)}-f_{0m}\|_{H_{x}^{r_{1}}}+C\|f_{1(m+1)}-f_{1m}\|_{H_{x}^{r_{1}-1}}\ +C\|({g}_{m+1}-{g}_{m})\cdot\nabla df_{m}\|_{H_{x}^{r_{1}}}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{20}m}\|(f_{0},f_{1})\|_{H^{r}_{x}\times H^{r-1}_{x}}+C\|{g}_{m+1}-{g}_{m}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial df_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H_{x}^{r_{1}}}.\end{split}

(11.135)

where we set
r_{1}=r-(\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon}{20})
. By using the energy estimates, we obtain

\begin{split}\|\partial df_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H_{x}^{r_{1}-1}}\leq&\|df_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H_{x}^{r}}\\
\leq&C(\|f_{0m}\|_{H^{r+1}_{x}}+\|f_{1m}\|_{H^{r}_{x}})\\
\leq&C2^{m}(\|f_{0m}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1m}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(11.136)

By using Strichartz estimates (
11.128
) and Lemma
2.14
, and (
11.47
), we shall prove that

\begin{split}&2^{m}\|{g}_{m+1}-{g}_{m}\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&2^{m}T^{\frac{1}{2}}_{m+1}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-{\mathbf{v}}_{m},{\rho}_{m+1}-{\rho}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C2^{m}2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}(m+1)}(\|{\mathbf{v}}_{m+1}-{\mathbf{v}}_{m},{\rho}_{m+1}-{\rho}_{m},{h}_{m+1}-{h}_{m}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H^{1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}_{x}}\\
&\quad+\|{\mathbf{w}}_{m+1}-{\mathbf{w}}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}_{x}}+\|{h}_{m+1}-{h}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}_{x}})\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}m}2^{-\frac{39\epsilon}{80}m}(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0)).\end{split}

(11.137)

Due to (
11.136
) and (
11.137
), for
k<j
, and
k\leq m
, we get

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}(df_{m+1}-df_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{m+1}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}m}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{80}m}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0)).\end{split}

(11.138)

In the following part, let us consider the energy estimates. Taking the operator
\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}
on (
11.125
), then we get

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{j}}\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{j}=[\square_{{g}_{j}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f_{j},\\
(\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{j},\partial_{t}\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{j})|_{t=0}=(\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{0j},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}f_{1j}).\end{cases}

(11.139)

To estimate
[\square_{{g}_{j}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f_{j}
, we will divide it into two cases
\frac{7}{6}\leq r<\frac{5}{2}
and
\frac{5}{2}<r\leq\frac{7}{2}
.

Case 1:
\frac{7}{6}\leq r<\frac{5}{2}
.
For
\frac{7}{6}\leq r\leq\frac{5}{2}
, note that

\begin{split}[\square_{{g}_{j}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f_{j}=&[{g}^{\alpha i}_{j}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}\partial_{i}]\partial_{\alpha}f_{j}+\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\partial_{i}g_{j}\partial_{\alpha}f_{j})\\
=&[{g}_{j}-\mathbf{m},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}\nabla]df_{j}+\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(\nabla g_{j}df).\end{split}

(11.140)

By (
10.182
) and Kato-Ponce estimates, we have
28
28

28

If
r=\frac{5}{2}
, then
L^{\frac{3}{s-r}}_{x}=L^{\infty}_{x}
.

\|[\square_{{g}_{j}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq C(\|dg_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r}(g_{j}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{\frac{3}{r-1}}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{\frac{5}{2}-r}}_{x}})

(11.141)

By Sobolev’s inequality, it follows

\|\left<\partial\right>^{r}(g_{j}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{\frac{3}{r-1}}_{x}}\leq C\|g_{j}-\mathbf{m}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}_{x}}.

(11.142)

By Hölder’s inequality, we can get

\begin{split}\|df_{j}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{\frac{5}{2}-r}}_{x}}\leq&C\|df_{j}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(11.143)

Case 2:
\frac{5}{2}\leq r<\frac{7}{2}
.
For
\frac{5}{2}<r<\frac{7}{2}
, using Kato-Ponce estimates, we have

\begin{split}\|[\square_{{g}_{j}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=&\|{g}^{\alpha i}_{j}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha i},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]\partial_{\alpha i}f_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(\|dg_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}+\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(g_{j}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{\frac{3}{r-2}}_{x}}\|\nabla df_{j}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{\frac{7}{2}-r}}_{x}}).\end{split}

(11.144)

By Sobolev’s inequality, it follows

\|\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}(g_{j}-\mathbf{m})\|_{L^{\frac{3}{r-2}}_{x}}\leq C\|g_{j}-\mathbf{m}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}_{x}}.

(11.145)

By Hölder’s inequality, we can get

\begin{split}\|\nabla df_{j}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{\frac{7}{2}-r}}_{x}}\leq&C\|df_{j}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(11.146)

For
\frac{7}{6}\leq r<\frac{7}{2}
, gathering (
11.141
)-(
11.143
) with (
11.144
)- (
11.146
), so we have

\begin{split}\|[\square_{{g}_{j}},\left<\partial\right>^{r-1}]f_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&C\|dg_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|^{2}_{H^{r-1}_{x}}+C\|g_{j}-\mathbf{m}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|^{2}_{H^{r-1}_{x}}\end{split}

(11.147)

By (
11.139
) and (
11.147
), we get

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|df_{j}\|^{2}_{H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&C\|dg_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|^{2}_{H^{r-1}_{x}}+C\|g_{j}-\mathbf{m}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}_{x}}\|df_{j}\|^{2}_{H^{r-1}_{x}}\end{split}

(11.148)

Using Gronwall’s inequality, we get

\begin{split}\|df_{j}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&C\|df_{j}(0)\|^{2}_{H^{r-1}_{x}}\cdot\exp\{\int^{t}_{0}\|dg_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|g_{j}-\mathbf{m}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}_{x}}d\tau\}.\end{split}

(11.149)

Note

\begin{split}\|f_{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq&\|f_{j}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\partial_{t}f_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(11.150)

By (
11.149
), (
11.150
) and (
10.168
), if
t\in[0,\bar{T}]
, then it follows

\begin{split}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|df_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,\bar{T}]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})\mathrm{e}^{\mathbb{E}_{*}},\end{split}

(11.151)

where
\mathbb{E}_{*}
is stated in (
11.17
).

Based on (
11.133
), (
11.138
), and (
11.151
), following the extending method in subsecion
10.3.2
, for
a\leq r-\frac{7}{6}
we shall obtain for
k\geq j

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{N_{1}}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{j}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\times 2^{\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}(j-N_{1})}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}N_{1}}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{80}j}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(11.152)

and for
k<j

\begin{split}&\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}P_{k}(df_{m+1}-df_{m})\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{N_{1}}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{80}m}2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}m}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))\times 2^{\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}(m-N_{1})}\\
\leq&C2^{-\frac{1}{2(3+\epsilon)}N_{1}}2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}k}2^{-\frac{3\epsilon}{80}m}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0)).\end{split}

(11.153)

By phase decomposition, we have

df_{j}=P_{\geq j}df_{j}+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{m=k}P_{k}(df_{m+1}-df_{m})+\textstyle{\sum}^{j-1}_{k=1}P_{k}df_{k}.

By using (
11.152
) and (
11.153
), we get

\begin{split}\|\left<\partial\right>^{a-1}df_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T_{N_{1}}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}_{x}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}_{x}})(1+\mathbb{E}^{2}(0))[\frac{1}{3}(1-2^{-\frac{\epsilon}{80}})]^{-2}.\end{split}

(11.154)

Therefore, seeing (
11.154
) and (
11.151
), then (
11.126
) hold.
∎
